```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "confirm_update", "write_id": uuid, "state": "superseded",
       "by_request_id": uuid, "call_ref": null, "made_by" (model null), "notify": []}
```
- `body` is "Replaced by your newer request.", and `notify: []` means it is silent
  (`envelope_risi_confirm_update_superseded.json`).
- Risi posts it in C, right after the rule above runs, **only when U has a `risi_items` device**,
  because a nightly.48 app would show the body as a stray line.
- A Phase 2 app shows the card with that `write_id` greyed, with the state "Replaced by your newer
  request" and no buttons. The first decisive message wins: `confirm_write`, `cancel_write` or
  `confirm_update`, in MLS order.
- A Phase 2 app shows an unknown `state` as "Closed". Only the agent leaf may send
  `confirm_update`; one from anywhere else is ignored.

### 34.3 "Ask me again" re-asks the original question (server, normative)
Every retry chip is the §25.4 `next_actions` entry
**`{"label": "Ask me again", "action": "ask", "text": <the ORIGINAL request text of the failed turn>}`**.
That is the asker's `risi_request.text` exactly as received: a chip's own text when the request was
a chip tap, at most 1000 grapheme clusters.
- **Never the failed tool's args.** A retry is a fresh turn, so the §34.1 classifier and routing run
  again.
- **When it is added.** The server adds this entry itself in these cases:
  - an `answer` whose turn has a step `failed`, `timeout` or `denied` and no successful write;
  - every `error` card of a turn except `superseded` (a `tool_timeout` also keeps its [Retry], below);
  - every §29.7 no-read answer.
- The model's own retry-like `next_steps` and `next_actions` ("Try again", "Retry", "Ask me
  again") are dropped and replaced by this one.
- `error` gains the optional field **`next_actions`** (the §25.4 shape;
  `envelope_risi_error_ask_again.json`). nightly.48 renders `next_actions` on answers. A v1.35 app
  renders them on error cards too, and an older app shows only the body.
- **Unchanged:** `tool_timeout` on a **confirmed** write keeps the §25.4 [Retry] (`confirm_write`
  again for the same `write_id`), because that retries the write the user already approved.
- Example of an answer with the chip: `envelope_risi_answer_ask_again.json`.

### 34.4 Risi's items (server, normative; Android in Phase 2)
**What it is.** A per-user ledger of everything Risi set up for the user. Only the user's own items
appear.

| `kind` | Written when | `ref` | Source of truth |
|---|---|---|---|
| `phone_event_added` | a device `calendar_add` result is `ok` with `event_id` and `verified: true` (§25.3) | `{"event_id": str (opaque), "write_id", "device_id", "calendar": {"name", "account"} \| null}` | this row (title, start/end and calendar come from the confirm card's `args` and the result; **no new phone data**) |
| `risi_calendar_event` | a `risi_calendar_add` is confirmed (§29.8) | `{"event_id": uuid}` | `risi_events` (§29.5) |
| `reminder` | a `set_reminder` is confirmed (§25.4) | `{"reminder_id": uuid}` | the reminder |
| `scheduled_message` | a `schedule_message` result is `ok` (§26.6) | `{"schedule_id": uuid, "device_id", "target_conversation_id", "repeat": "daily" \| null}` | the phone. **The text is never stored on the server** (§26.6): `title` is `null`, and the phone shows its local text |
| `follow_up` | — (read live) | `{"item_id": uuid}` | ledger items where the user is a counterpart (§28.7 `promised_to_me`) |
| `promise` | — (read live) | `{"item_id": uuid}` | ledger items where the user is the owner (§28.7 `i_promised`) |

**Storage.** The new Postgres table **`risi_items`** holds rows for the first four kinds:
```
risi_items(id uuid PK, user_id uuid NOT NULL, kind text NOT NULL, ref jsonb NOT NULL,   -- ids only
           title_sealed bytea NULL, calendar_sealed bytea NULL,                          -- RISI_DATA_KEY
           start_at timestamptz NULL, end_at timestamptz NULL, all_day bool NOT NULL,
           state text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active', created_at, updated_at)
  INDEX (user_id, start_at)
```
- Sealing works as in §29.5, through `RisiMe.Agent.Seal`, with AAD `risi_items:<id>:title` and
  `…:calendar`. There is no plaintext title column.
- `follow_up` and `promise` are not copied. The read joins the §28.7 query (open items) at query
  time, with `start` = `due`.
- **Freshness.** On read, a `risi_calendar_event` or `reminder` row takes its current state and
  time from its source. A row whose source is gone, cancelled or fired is left out.
- **Retention** follows each kind's own rules:
  - `phone_event_added`: 30 days after `end`, as the §26.4 undo window, or on delete;
  - `risi_calendar_event`: with the event's purge (§29.5);
  - `reminder`: 30 days after it fires or is cancelled;
  - `scheduled_message`: on cancel, or 30 days after the last send of a one-off;
  - all rows are deleted with account deletion.
- None of this reaches the learning log, `risi_turn_steps`, logs or pushes (titles as §29.5).
- Rows are written from the same success paths that write the §26.4 activity entries.

**REST** (auth, with `X-Device-Id` of a device of the caller, else `403 invalid_device`):

| Method, path | Body / query | Reply |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/risi/items?from=ts&to=ts&kinds=k1,k2` | `from` defaults to now, `to` to `from` + 92 days (at most 366 days, else `422 bad_request`); `kinds` defaults to all; an unknown kind is `422` | `200 {"items": [RisiItem]}`, at most 500, sorted by `start` with items without a `start` last (`risi_items_reply.json`) |
| `PATCH /api/v1/risi/items/{id}` | `reminder` only: `{"at"?: ts, "text"?: 1–500 chars}` (`risi_items_patch_reminder.json`) | `200 {"item": RisiItem}`; another kind is `422 bad_request` |
| `DELETE /api/v1/risi/items/{id}` | — | `204`; routed by kind, see below |

```
RisiItem = {"id": uuid, "kind", "title": str | null, "start": ts | null, "end": ts | null,
            "all_day": bool, "state": "active", "calendar": {"name", "account"} | null,
            "conversation_id": str | null, "ref": {…as the table}, "created_at": ts,
            "actions": ["open" | "edit" | "delete"]}
```
- For a `follow_up` or `promise`, `id` = `item_id`. `actions` is computed for the calling device.
- **What DELETE does for each kind:**
  - `risi_calendar_event`: the §29.4 `DELETE`.
  - `reminder`: cancels it, and writes a `reminder_cancelled` activity entry.
  - `phone_event_added`, `scheduled_message`: **client actions.** The phone in `ref.device_id`
    first deletes the provider row by `event_id`, or cancels the schedule locally, after a confirm
    dialog. It then calls `DELETE` to drop the row. From any other device the reply is
    `403 invalid_device`. As in §26.4, the phone touches only rows that are in its own local record
    of what it created.
  - `follow_up`, `promise`: `422 bad_request`. These are edited in My promises (§28.7, §27.5).
- **Errors.** `404 not_found` for someone else's item or an unknown one. `503 agent_unavailable` as
  §24.11 when the sealed rows can't be opened.

**Server tool `risi_items`** `{from?, to?, kinds?}` answers "what have you set up for me?":
- It is offered to every asker with a Risi chat, and it is in the `risi_next_action` enum.
- The classifier also fires on it: "what have you set up / added / scheduled for me", "my
  reminders", "what did you put in my calendar", in all four languages. The server then forces this
  tool as step 1, in the same way as §34.1.
- **The answer comes from the ledger only.** The model's text is discarded, and the server builds
  the `answer`: one line per item, by time, grouped by kind. A `scheduled_message` line names only
  the recipient and the time (§26.4). With no items the answer is "I haven't set anything up for you
  from <from> to <to>."
- `sources` carries one **`{"type": "risi_item", "item_id": uuid, "kind": str}`** per item. This is a
  new `Source`; old apps show nothing for it (`envelope_risi_answer_risi_items.json`).

**Android (Phase 2): Settings → Risi skills → Calendar → "Risi's items".**
- An upcoming list from `GET /risi/items` (from now), grouped by day. A `phone_event_added` item
  shows its provider row when this phone has it, as the `added_event` card does (§25.3). A
  `scheduled_message` item shows the local text on its own phone, and "On your other phone"
  elsewhere.
- **Open:**
  - `phone_event_added`: `ACTION_VIEW` of the event;
  - `risi_calendar_event`: the event screen;
  - `reminder`: the Risi chat;
  - `scheduled_message`: the chat's "Scheduled messages";
  - `promise` and `follow_up`: My promises at the item.
- **Edit:**
  - `phone_event_added`: `ACTION_EDIT`;
  - `risi_calendar_event`: the §29.3 `PATCH`;
  - `reminder`: the `PATCH` above;
  - `scheduled_message`: the local Edit (§26.6);
  - `promise`: the §27.5 `item_edit`.
- **Delete:** a confirm dialog, then the `DELETE` routing above. Buttons appear only as `actions`
  allows.

### 34.5 Name check before a write (server, normative)
**When it runs.** Before the server posts the confirm card of a write that names people:
- `calendar_add`, `risi_calendar_add`;
- `set_reminder` with a person;
- `schedule_message` (the recipient's name, before it is resolved to a conversation).

It runs also under "Allowed" (§26.3).

**Which names are checked.** The names come from the draft or step slot `with: [str]` (§29.8). This
release extends that slot to `calendar_add`, `set_reminder` and `schedule_message` drafts; it is
server-side only, and the phone's `calendar_add` args are unchanged. The server also checks the
words after "with " in an English title, up to punctuation.

**Matching.** Each name is matched against the asker's friends: their contacts (§1.4) and the
co-members of their chats, by display name and by each word of it.
- Before matching, both sides are normalised: NFKD, combining marks removed, case-folded, spaces
  collapsed.
- **Exact match:** the write goes straight through.
- **Close match:** a Levenshtein distance of at most 2 (at most 1 for names of 4 characters or
  fewer), **or** an equal Double Metaphone primary code (Latin script only).
- **No close match:** the write goes through unchanged, because the person need not be a contact.

**Unknown name with a close match.** The confirm card is held, and a question is asked first: at
most one name per turn, and at most once per name per draft. The question is in the conversation of
the card, normally the Risi chat. "Shutazi" with the contact "Shirazi" asks **"Did you mean
Shirazi?"**, with up to 3 candidates, best first; ties go to the most recently messaged contact.
- **Phase 1 (nightly.48; the clarify form it already renders):** an `answer` with
  `next_actions: [{"label": "Shirazi", "action": "ask", "text": "Use Shirazi"},
  {"label": "Keep \"Shutazi\"", "action": "ask", "text": "Keep Shutazi"}]` and the field
  **`clarify`** below (`envelope_risi_answer_name_clarify.json`).
  - The draft remembers the offered chip texts.
  - The next request whose text **equals** one of them (case-insensitive, trimmed) resolves the
    name without a model call: the draft's name is replaced, or kept. The server then posts the
    confirm card. These are server-issued strings matched exactly; free text is still not parsed
    for intent (decision 066).
  - Any other request supersedes the question (§34.2).
- **Phase 2 (`risi_items` device):** the same `answer`, where the app renders `clarify` natively as
  a card with one button per option plus [Keep "<said>"]. A tap sends the `ask` of the matching
  `next_actions` entry, so there is one path for both phases.
- The `clarify` field:
  ```
  "clarify": {"about": "name", "write_id": uuid, "said": "Shutazi",
              "options": [{"name": "Shirazi", "user_id": uuid | null}], "keep": true}
  ```
  `write_id` is the one the following confirm card will carry, so `confirm_update` (§34.2) also
  closes the question. An answer to the question never counts as a §28.3 question.

### 34.6 Amendments, examples, gate
**Amendments.**
- **§25.1:** the enforced read step, the write-free enum and the `tool`-first schema (§34.1).
- **§25.4:** `error.next_actions`; the `error` code `superseded`; the `Source` `risi_item`; the kind
  `confirm_update` (§34.2, §34.3, §34.4).
- **§28.2:** drafts are posted only when patched in this turn, and are superseded by a new request;
  `with` covers every write.
- **§28.3:** the name question does not count as a question.
- **§29.7:** the source `risi_items`, the server-built list, and `local_events` + Risi's items.
- **§26.4:** rows of `risi_items` are written alongside activity entries.
- **New:** the device capability `risi_items` (Phase 2) and the table `risi_items`.

**Examples** (`contract/v1/examples/`):
- `envelope_risi_answer_schedule_read.json`
- `envelope_risi_error_superseded.json`
- `envelope_risi_confirm_update_superseded.json`
- `envelope_risi_error_ask_again.json`
- `envelope_risi_answer_ask_again.json`
- `risi_items_reply.json`
- `risi_items_patch_reminder.json`
- `envelope_risi_answer_risi_items.json`
- `envelope_risi_answer_name_clarify.json`
- `device_put_risi_items.json`

**Fixture.** `contract/v1/risi_routing_cases.json` holds classifier and name-check cases. The server
must pass all of them; Android does not use it.

**Gate.** These canary and regression cases are normative. They are server tests, plus the
`scripts/fake-llm --script` replay, plus the model eval for 1 and 2:
1. Schedule questions in English, Sinhala, Tamil and Singlish (every `schedule_read` case of the
   fixture) call `risi_calendar_check`, or `calendar_check` where Risi Calendar is off, before any
   answer. This holds even when the scripted model answers straight away or picks
   `schedule_message`.
2. "Check my calendar for Monday, October 12th" calls the read tool for Mon 12 Oct, and **never**
   `schedule_message` or any other write tool.
3. A pending `schedule_message` card or draft does not hijack the next turn:
   - the next request supersedes it;
   - a `confirm_write` on it gives the `superseded` error and no tool call;
   - a Phase 2 device sees `confirm_update`;
   - no card is posted from an unpatched draft.
4. After a failed turn, "Ask me again" is an `ask` with the original text and runs a fresh turn
   with fresh routing.
5. "what have you set up for me?" calls `risi_items`, and the answer lists exactly the ledger rows
   (all four stored kinds plus promises and follow-ups).
6. "add a meeting with Shutazi tomorrow at 3pm", with the friend "Shirazi", gives the name question
   before any confirm card. "Use Shirazi" gives a card with Shirazi. "Keep Shutazi" gives a card
   with Shutazi. An exact "Shirazi", or a name with no close match ("Zebediah"), goes straight to
   the card.
7. The encoded `risi_next_action` schema has `tool` as its first property.

**Android:**
- decodes every new example;
- Phase 2 UI tests: a greyed superseded card; the clarify card; the Risi's items list with Open,
  Edit and Delete; and Delete of a `phone_event_added` item removing the provider row.

## 35. Risi Brain v2: long-term memory of Official, retrieval, autonomy (v1.36)
Decision 077 (Harsha 2026-10-11) **overrides the 24-hour limit** of §24.12, §25 (`search_chats` "the 24 h buffer"),
§27 and §30 for **Official** only. Private is never read or stored (unchanged).

### 35.1 Retention
- Every Official message Risi decrypts as a member is kept, sealed with `RISI_DATA_KEY` (per-row AAD), as long-term
  memory **until deleted** (§35.3). Risi's own posts are kept the same way. The 24-hour buffer remains as the
  working set for the turn loop; memory is its long-term store.
- Derived knowledge: **items** of kind `person` · `project` · `decision` · `promise` · `meeting` · `deadline` ·
  `open_question` · `summary`, each with `{text, people: [user_id | name], project | null, at | null,
  sources: [{conversation_id, message_ids}]}`, plus embeddings (pgvector, computed on spark2 only; sealed vectors
  are not used, the vector index holds no text). Items appear in "What Risi knows about me" (§24.13) as their
  `kind`; clients show an unknown kind as text.
- A participant who opted out (§35.3) is never stored or extracted from that point; the server drops their
  messages before sealing.

### 35.2 Retrieval (server tools; §25 routing, §34.1 intent router extended)
- The deterministic router runs before the model:
  - schedule → the calendar read (§34.1);
  - "summarise <range | person | project>", "what did I agree with <person>", "what's pending with <person>" →
    `memory_search` and `summarise_range`;
  - promises → the ledger (My promises);
  - "what have you set up" → `risi_items` (§34.4).
- `memory_search {query, range: {from, to} | null, people: [str], kinds: [str], conversation_id | null}` and
  `summarise_range {range, people, project | null}`: map-reduce over the stored history of the asker's Official
  chats only (a member of each); the answer cites `answer.sources` of type `memory` `{conversation_id,
  message_ids, at}`. The model writes the text only from the tool results; no result → "I don't have anything
  about that", never invented.

### 35.3 Controls (REST, the caller's own scope)
- `DELETE /api/v1/risi/memory?conversation_id=<grp:>` → `204`: deletes the caller's messages, Risi's posts to the
  caller, and the items derived from them in that chat (other members' rows stay).
- `DELETE /api/v1/risi/memory` → `204`: the same over every chat (with `X-Confirm: delete-all`, else `400`).
- `PUT /api/v1/risi/learning` `{"conversation_id": "grp:…" | null, "enabled": bool}` → `204`: "Stop Risi learning
  from my messages" for one chat or everywhere (`null`); `GET /api/v1/risi/learning` → `{"global": bool,
  "chats": [{"conversation_id", "enabled"}]}`. Any participant may set their own.
- Existing `DELETE /api/v1/risi/facts[/{id}]` also deletes memory items and embeddings.
- Examples: `risi_learning_put.json`, `risi_learning_reply.json`, `envelope_risi_answer_memory_sources.json`.

### 35.4 Autonomy (server, Oban; rules are server-normative, wire shapes are existing kinds)
- **Ingest on every Official message:** extract promises, deadlines, meetings, decisions, cancellations and changes;
  update items and the §34.4 ledger.
- **Agreed meetings and deadlines** → a proposed calendar event per participant through the existing card/add path
  (§25.3 v1.32 read-back): the participant's Calendar skill setting decides (Allowed → added directly, with the
  `added_event` card; Ask → a `confirm` card).
- **Quiet-period summary** (30 min after the last message, or "@Risi summarise"): to the chat (§27 discussion
  summary) and a Risi Note per participant in their own Risi chat, in that participant's language.
- **Cancellation or change** of a meeting Risi created → update the entries it created (read-back), notify the other
  participants, offer "Reschedule?" with free slots computed from each participant's free/busy (§29.7 reads).
- **Follow-ups:** a nudge to the owner before a promise is due; after a missed deadline, ask the owner for status and
  tell the counterpart (per their settings).
- **Daily brief** at 07:00 local in the user's Risi chat (`digest` kind): today's meetings, promises due, overdue,
  waiting on others, yesterday's key decisions.
- Every autonomous post follows the honesty rules (§25.3 v1.32): no claim without a tool result.
- **Wire uses (existing kinds, additive fields):** the brief is a `digest` with `scope: "brief"` and the fields
  `events`, `risi_items`, `items`, `decisions`, `language`. Every line comes from stored data, and it ends with a
  "Checked:" line. A missed-deadline question to the owner is a `reminder` with `phase: "overdue"`. "Reschedule?"
  offers up to 3 slots free for everyone **in Risi Calendar**, as `next_actions` `ask` chips; phone calendars can't
  be read by the server, and the text says so. Older apps show `body`. `RISI_BRIEF=off` disables the brief.
  Example (v1.49): `envelope_risi_digest_brief.json`. Its `decisions` are objects `{"text", "conversation_id"}`;
  phones draw a brief (and any digest `scope` they don't know) from `body`, which is also the chat-list preview.

### 35.5 Backfill (later; specified when it starts)
With the chat owner's consent the phone uploads an Official chat's existing history, encrypted to the Risi member.
Needs an APK; not in v1.36.

## 36. Call transcription fast path: phone-side chunks (v1.39)
Decision 080 (Harsha 2026-10-12). This is a second, quicker transcription path. It sits next to §27.7–§27.8,
the LiveKit listener, which is unchanged. **Additive only:** one device capability (`call_transcribe`), one
`/auth/config` switch, one channel push with its signal, two REST endpoints, three Risi kinds, one optional
`call_offer` field, the optional `lang` on two §27 kinds, and a developer setting. Old apps see the new Risi
kinds as `body`.

### 36.0 Principles
- **Official calls only.** v1.39 covers the 1:1 Official call (a §16/§19 call placed from the Official tab,
  §24.5 v1.33). Group Official calls come later (§36.9). **A Private call never has a Transcribe button and no
  code path:** the phone never sends a request, an allow or a chunk for a call it holds as Private. The server
  refuses any session whose conversation is not an Official `grp:` with Official `on`.
- **Everyone consents, and anyone stops.** Transcription runs only while **every** participant has allowed.
  Any participant can stop it for everyone with one tap. Every start, decline and stop is recorded in the chat
  as a Risi system line, naming who did it and when.
- **Each phone sends only its own microphone.** Chunks of 10–15 s (Ogg/Opus) go over TLS to the server. The
  speaker is the authenticated uploader, so no diarisation is needed. **Raw audio is never stored:** each
  chunk is held in memory (or tmpfs) only until its transcription, then deleted.
- **This path is not end-to-end encrypted to the members.** The server (inside `RisiMe.Agent`) hears the
  audio. The UI says so honestly (decision 048): **"Audio is sent to Risi for transcription."**
- **Local models only**, on spark2, with no commercial fallback for speech.

### 36.1 Switch, capability, developer flag
- **`GET /auth/config`** gains **`"call_transcribe": "on" | "off"`** (`auth_config_v139.json`). It is `on`
  only while the server env `RISI_CALL_TRANSCRIBE=on` (default `off`), Risi is available, and the speech
  service is healthy. Absent means `off`. While it is `off`, phones hide the button, and the server answers
  every push with `transcribe_off` and every upload with `503 transcribe_off`. Turning it off stops running
  sessions (reason `switch_off`). This is the kill switch: no new app is needed.
- **Capability:** a v1.39 app advertises **`"call_transcribe"`** in `mls.capabilities`
  (`device_put_call_transcribe.json`) once it implements §36.2–§36.7 and renders the §36.5 kinds.
- **`call_offer.tab`** (inside MLS, §16.2; `call_offer_official_payload.json`): the caller sets
  `"tab": "official"` on a call placed from a 1:1's Official tab. If it is absent, the call is Private.
  - Both phones show **[Transcribe]** only when the call's offer said `"official"`, `/auth/config` says `on`,
    and this device has `call_transcribe`.
  - A phone that holds the call as Private answers any request for it with `decline` and
    `"reason": "private_call"`, without asking the user.
  - The server can't see the tab: it enforces Official `on` and full consent, and the phones enforce the tab.
- **Developer flag.** The server env `RISI_DEV_USERS` lists user ids (in `.env`).
  - `User` (§1.3) gains **`"risi_dev": true`** for those users only; it is absent for everyone else
    (`me_reply_risi_dev.json`).
  - **`GET` / `PUT /api/v1/me/risi/dev`** with `{"transcribe_compare": bool}` returns
    `200 {"transcribe_compare": bool}` (`me_risi_dev_put.json`, `me_risi_dev_reply.json`). It answers
    **`403 not_developer`** to anyone not listed. The setting is stored per user and defaults to `false`.
  - The app shows the toggle (Settings → About → tap the version 7 times) only when `risi_dev` is true.
  - The server decides `compare` on each request (§36.2); the client never sets it. A user removed from the
    list loses compare at their next request.

### 36.2 Consent: the session and its state machine
One **session** belongs to one call attempt. A call may have at most **3 sessions** in sequence, and only
one of them may be live at a time.

```
Session = {"session_id": uuid, "call_id": uuid, "conversation_id": "grp:…" (the Official conversation),
  "chat_id", "state": "requested" | "on" | "declined" | "expired" | "stopped" | "ended",
  "requested_by": uuid, "requested_at": ts, "expires_at": ts | null,
  "participants": [{"user_id", "consent": "allowed" | "pending" | "declined", "at": ts | null}],
  "compare": bool, "started_at": ts | null, "ended_at": ts | null,
  "by": uuid | null, "reason": null | "stopped" | "declined" | "expired" | "ended" | "left" | "official_off"
        | "switch_off" | "max_length" | "unavailable" | "private_call",
  "discard": bool, "chunk": {"min_ms": 5000, "max_ms": 20000, "max_bytes": 262144}, "server_ts"}
```

**Transitions** (server, normative):
```
(none) / declined / expired / stopped ──request──▶ requested    (requester = allowed; the others = pending)
requested ──allow by the last pending──▶ on                     (started_at = now)
requested ──decline by anyone──▶ declined
requested ──stop by anyone──▶ stopped (reason stopped)         (stop before start = cancel)
requested ──30 s without full consent──▶ expired
on ──stop by anyone──▶ stopped        (optional discard)
on ──stop with reason "ended" (hang-up)──▶ ended
on ──90 s with no chunk from a participant──▶ ended (reason left)
on ──4 h after started_at──▶ ended (reason max_length)
on / requested ──Official off──▶ stopped (official_off) · switch off ──▶ stopped (switch_off)
on ──speech service down for 60 s──▶ stopped (unavailable)
```
`declined`, `expired`, `stopped` and `ended` are final for that session. A new **request** opens a new
`session_id`, and every participant is asked again.

**Participants** in a 1:1 are the two users of the call:
- They are taken from the server's own record of the `call_id`: the `from`/`to` of its `ring: true`
  `call_signal`, kept in memory for 4 h.
- That call's `dm:` must be the `chat_id` of the named Official conversation.
- **The device is bound:** a user's consent and uploads come only from the device that sent their `request`
  or `allow`. Any other device of that user gets `invalid_device`.

**Leaving and rejoining (1:1):**
- **Hang-up:** the phone sends `stop` with `"reason": "ended"` before it tears down.
- **Socket drop or ICE restart:** the session stays `on`. The phone keeps recording its own microphone and
  holds **at most 2 chunks** in memory, dropping the oldest. On reconnect it uploads them with their original
  `seq`. If no chunk arrives from a participant for 90 s, the session ends (reason `left`).
- **A new call** (a new `call_id`) never inherits consent.

### 36.3 The wire: push `call:transcribe` and signal `call_transcribe`
**`call:transcribe`** is a client → server push on the inbox topic (§2). Every push has the shape
`{"action", "call_id", "conversation_id": "grp:…" (the chat's Official conversation), …extra}`:

| `action` | extra fields | who may send | when |
|---|---|---|---|
| `request` | — | a participant, from a `call_transcribe` device | no live session, fewer than 3 sessions so far, not within 10 s of this user's last request |
| `allow` | `session_id` | a participant whose consent is `pending` | `requested` |
| `decline` | `session_id`, `reason`: `null` \| `"private_call"` | a pending participant | `requested` |
| `stop` | `session_id`, `reason`: `"stopped"` \| `"ended"`, `discard`: bool | any participant | `requested` or `on` |

Examples: `call_transcribe_request.json`, `call_transcribe_allow.json`, `call_transcribe_decline.json`,
`call_transcribe_stop.json`.

- **Reply ok:** `{"session": Session}` (`call_transcribe_reply.json`).
  - A repeated `allow`, `decline` or `stop` on a final session is idempotent: the reply is ok with the
    current state.
- **Reply error** (`{"reason": …}`):

  | Reason | Meaning |
  |---|---|
  | `transcribe_off` | the switch is off |
  | `not_official` | the conversation is not an Official `grp:` with Official `on`, or the call's `dm:` doesn't match its `chat_id` |
  | `unknown_call` | the server has no record of this `call_id` from the last 4 h |
  | `not_participant` | the sender is not a participant of the call |
  | `invalid_device` | the device has no `call_transcribe`, or is not the bound device |
  | `peer_not_ready` | the other participant has no live `call_transcribe` device |
  | `busy` | a session is already live, or 3 sessions were used |
  | `bad_state` | the action doesn't fit the current state |
  | `rate_limited` | over the limits below |
  | `transcribe_unavailable` | Risi or the speech service is down, or at capacity |

- **Rate limits:** 30 `call:transcribe` pushes per user per minute, and 10 requests per user per hour.
- **The signal `call_transcribe`** (§2.3, ephemeral; `signal_call_transcribe.json`):
  `{"kind": "call_transcribe", "data": Session}`.
  - It is sent to the live sockets of every participant's `call_transcribe` devices on every transition,
    including the request itself, so the peer sees the request.
- **`GET /api/v1/calls/{call_id}/transcribe`** (auth; participants only, else `404 not_found`) returns
  `200 {"session": Session | null}`, the newest session of the call, in the same shape as
  `call_transcribe_reply.json`. Phones call it after a reconnect, before they trust any local state.

### 36.4 Upload: `POST /api/v1/calls/{call_id}/transcribe/chunks`
The request is auth plus `X-Device-Id` (the bound device), sent as `multipart/form-data`:

| Part | Content |
|---|---|
| `session_id` | the session's id |
| `seq` | an int, 0, 1, 2… per (session, device), strictly increasing; gaps are allowed |
| `offset_ms` | the chunk's start, in ms since `started_at`, from the phone's server-corrected clock (§16.3 offset) |
| `duration_ms` | 1–20 000 |
| `codec` | `"ogg_opus"`: Ogg/Opus, mono, 16 or 48 kHz, 16–32 kbit/s |
| `audio` | the file part, at most **256 KiB** |

- The phone cuts its own microphone into chunks of **10–15 s** (a fast session, v1.50 §36.12: 1–4 s at pauses). It sends silent chunks too: they act as the
  keep-alive.
- It records only while the state is `on` and its own microphone is not muted. While the microphone is muted
  it sends silent chunks.
- **Reply:** `202 {"seq": int, "accepted": true, "duplicate": bool}` (`call_transcribe_chunk_reply.json`).
  A repeated `seq` gets `202` with `duplicate: true`, and the chunk is not transcribed twice.
- **Errors** (§0 shape; e.g. `error_not_consented.json`, `error_not_official.json`, `error_transcribe_off.json`):

  | Status | Code | Meaning |
  |---|---|---|
  | `403` | `not_official` | the call isn't Official |
  | `403` | `not_participant` | the uploader isn't a participant |
  | `403` | `invalid_device` | not the bound device |
  | `404` | `not_found` | unknown session, or the session isn't of this call |
  | `409` | `not_consented` | the state is `requested` |
  | `409` | `stopped` | the state is `declined`, `expired`, `stopped` or `ended`; the phone stops recording at once |
  | `413` | `too_large` | over 256 KiB |
  | `422` | `bad_request` | codec, duration, or a `seq` that isn't higher |
  | `429` | `rate_limited` | over 8 chunks per participant per 60 s, with a burst of 3 after a reconnect |
  | `503` | `transcribe_off` | the switch is off |
  | `503` | `transcribe_unavailable` | the speech service is down or full; the phone retries the same `seq` once after 5 s, then drops it |

- **No storage** (server, normative):
  - The chunk is read into memory, or into the speech service's size-limited tmpfs, and is never written to
    disk or to a log.
  - It is deleted right after transcription, and also on any error.
  - On `stop` with `discard`, chunks still queued are dropped without being transcribed.
  - At most **2 queued chunks per participant** are held. Beyond that, the newest chunk is refused with
    `503 transcribe_unavailable`.

### 36.5 Models and the Risi kinds
**Routing** (server, normative):
1. For each chunk, the speech service detects the language with `faster-whisper-large-v3` among `si`, `ta`
   and `en`.
2. The chunk then goes to one model by language:

   | Language | Model alias |
   |---|---|
   | `si` | `whisper-small-sinhala` |
   | `ta` | `tamil-asr-qwen3` |
   | `en` (and unsure) | `faster-whisper-large-v3` |

   The aliases are served on loopback (decision 080).
3. A chunk with no speech (VAD) posts nothing.

**The transcript line `call_transcript`** (a `text` envelope from Risi's leaf in the Official conversation,
§24.11; `envelope_risi_call_transcript.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "call_transcript", "call_id", "session_id", "turn": int (1-based per session),
  "speaker": uuid, "start": ts, "end": ts, "text": str (≤ 2000 chars), "language": "si" | "ta" | "en",
  "model": alias, "call_ref": null, "made_by": {"model": alias, "provider": "risime", "at", "also": []},
  "notify": [], "romanised"?: true}
```
- **`romanised`** (v1.40, optional; §36.11): present and `true` only when a Sinhala chunk of the turn was
  still not in Sinhala script after the speech service's retry. Absent otherwise.
- **`body`:** `"Harsha 17:52: <text>"`. The name is the server's display name; the phone renders the name
  from its own data and the time in its own zone. These posts are silent.
- **Grouping** (a fast session, v1.50: §36.12 instead):
  - Consecutive chunks of one speaker are merged into one turn, until another participant's speech starts,
    the turn reaches 60 s, or the session ends.
  - A turn is posted when it closes, or after 30 s of an open turn; a continuation is posted as the next
    `turn`.
  - Posts follow the order of `start` within the session, on a best-effort basis.

**The consent line `call_transcribe`** (Risi system line in Official; `envelope_risi_call_transcribe_on.json`,
`envelope_risi_call_transcribe_stopped.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "call_transcribe", "call_id", "session_id",
  "state": "on" | "declined" | "expired" | "stopped" | "ended",
  "requested_by": uuid, "consents": [{"user_id", "consent", "at"}], "by": uuid | null, "reason",
  "discard": bool, "started_at", "ended_at", "compare": bool, "call_ref": null,
  "made_by": {"model": null, …}, "notify": []}
```
It is posted once per final transition and once on `on`. The `body` lines:

| State | `body` |
|---|---|
| `on` | "Harsha asked Risi to transcribe this call; Kamal allowed (17:50). Audio is sent to Risi for transcription." |
| `declined` | "Kamal declined transcription (17:50)." |
| `expired` | "No answer to the transcription request (17:50)." |
| `stopped` | "Kamal stopped transcription (18:02)." |
| `stopped` with `discard` | "Kamal stopped transcription and deleted the transcript (18:02)." |
| `ended` | "Transcription ended with the call (17:50–18:02)." |

**Stop with `discard`:** Risi deletes its `call_transcript` and `call_transcript_compare` posts of that session
for everyone (§15, sender delete), deletes the buffer rows, and makes **no summary**. The consent line stays.

**Compare mode `call_transcript_compare`** (only when `session.compare`; one post per chunk with speech, and
it replaces `call_transcript` for that session; `envelope_risi_call_transcript_compare.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "call_transcript_compare", "call_id", "session_id", "seq", "speaker",
  "start", "end", "language_detected": "si" | "ta" | "en", "chosen": alias (the routed model),
  "results": [{"model": alias, "text": str | null, "language": str | null, "elapsed_ms": int,
               "error": null | "timeout" | "failed",
               "script"?: {"expected": "sinh", "sinhala_chars": int, "latin_chars": int, "ok": bool},
               "romanised"?: bool}],
  "call_ref": null, "made_by": {"model": null, "provider": "risime", "at", "also": [{model, provider, task}…]},
  "notify": []}
```
- Every candidate model runs on the same chunk, with a 30-s timeout each.
- **`body`:** one line per model, `"<model> (<elapsed> ms): <text>"`, with `chosen` first.
- **Consent:** the request shown to the peer says "(developer model comparison on)", so the peer allows
  knowing it.

- **`script`, `romanised`** (v1.40, optional; §36.11): on each result without an `error` when the chunk is
  Sinhala (`language_detected: "si"`). `envelope_risi_call_transcript_compare_si_script.json`.

### 36.6 The phone (android, normative)
- **[Transcribe]** appears on the Official call screen under §36.1. A tap sends `request` and shows
  "Asking Kamal…".
- **The peer's prompt:** a sheet over the call screen says "<name> wants Risi to transcribe this call. Audio
  is sent to Risi for transcription." It offers **[Allow] [Decline]** and closes when `expires_at` passes.
- **While `on`:** both phones show a **red bar, "Risi transcribing"**, with [Stop], from the `on` signal
  until a final state.
  - [Stop] opens two choices: "Stop" and "Stop and delete transcript".
  - **The bar fails toward showing:** it is removed only on a final state from the signal, from `GET`, or from
    a `409 stopped`.
- Each phone uploads only its own microphone track, captured before the network path: the local
  `AudioRecord` / WebRTC capture tap, never the remote track. It never uploads in a call held as Private.
- The phone stops recording at once on any final state or error, and zeroes its buffers.

### 36.7 After the call: summary and follow-ups (reuses §27, §29)
- **Trigger:** at `ended`, or at `stopped` without `discard`, when the session has **≥ 30 s of speech from
  ≥ 2 speakers**.
  - The server runs the §27.2 call extraction on the session's transcript (`source: "call"`, `call_id`,
    `media` from the call's cleartext `media`).
  - It posts the per-person **`discussion_summary`** (§27.3, in each Risi chat) and one **`discussion_card`**
    (§27.4, in Official).
  - Agreed meetings become **§29.8 `confirm`** calendar cards in each owner's Risi chat (§35.4 rules). Each
    owner confirms their own items.
  - `made_by.also` lists every speech model used, with `task: "transcribe"`.
- **Language, reader first:** both kinds gain an optional **`"lang": "si" | "ta" | "en"`**
  (`envelope_risi_discussion_card_transcribed.json`).
  - A per-person copy is written in its reader's language: the language that reader spoke most in the
    session, else `en`.
  - The Official card is written in the call's majority language.
  - Clients ignore `lang` if they don't know it; it changes no rendering rule.

### 36.8 Storage, retention, learning log
- **Transcript working set:** the §27.8 `risi_buffer` rows `(conversation_id, call_id, seg_n, speaker, start,
  end, text)`, sealed with `RISI_DATA_KEY`, TTL 24 h. They are deleted after the §36.7 extraction, on a
  discard, and on Official off.
- **The posted lines** are Risi posts in Official, so §35.1 applies: sealed, kept until deleted, and covered
  by §35.3 controls and opt-out.
  - A participant who opted out of memory is still transcribed for the chat (they consented), but their
    lines are not kept in memory.
- **Session state** (Postgres `risi_call_transcribe_sessions`, additive):
  - It holds `session_id`, `call_id`, `conversation_id`, the participants with their consents and bound
    devices, `state`, `reason`, `compare`, timestamps and counts. No text, no audio.
  - Rows are kept 90 days as the consent record.
- **Learning log** (§24.12):
  - **Normal sessions:** one `transcribe` row per session, as in §27.8: models, audio seconds per language,
    chunk count, latency, and `output: null`.
  - **Compare sessions:** one **`transcribe_compare`** row per chunk per model. It holds the model,
    `elapsed_ms`, the detected language, `audio_ms`, the input `{session_id, seq, speaker}` plus a SHA-256 of
    the chunk, and **`output: {"text"}` sealed with `RISI_DATA_KEY`, written with a 30-day TTL**.
  - This amends §27.9 ("never transcript text in the learning log") for compare rows only. They exist only
    with the developer's setting and the peer's informed consent.
  - Never logged anywhere: audio, and transcript text outside these rows.

### 36.9 Groups (later; reserved)
(v1.45: activated and specified by §40.9.)
Group Official calls (§20) use the same push, signal, upload and kinds, with these differences:
- the participants are the human identities in the LiveKit room;
- a joiner while `on` turns the state to **`paused`** (uploads get `409 not_consented`) until they allow;
- a joiner's decline stops the session for everyone;
- the §27.7 listener and this path never run in the same call (`busy`).

Not in v1.39.

### 36.10 Amendments, examples, gate
- **§16.2:** `call_offer` gains the optional `tab`. **§24.5 / §27.7:** a 1:1 Official call may be transcribed
  under §36 only, and never a Private call. **§27.3 / §27.4:** the optional `lang`. **§27.9:** compare rows
  (§36.8). **§1.3:** `User.risi_dev`.
- **Examples:**
  - `auth_config_v139.json`, `device_put_call_transcribe.json`, `call_offer_official_payload.json`;
  - `call_transcribe_request.json`, `call_transcribe_allow.json`, `call_transcribe_decline.json`,
    `call_transcribe_stop.json`, `call_transcribe_reply.json`, `signal_call_transcribe.json`;
  - `call_transcribe_chunk_reply.json`, `error_not_consented.json`, `error_not_official.json`,
    `error_transcribe_off.json`;
  - `envelope_risi_call_transcript.json`, `envelope_risi_call_transcript_compare.json`,
    `envelope_risi_call_transcribe_on.json`, `envelope_risi_call_transcribe_stopped.json`,
    `envelope_risi_discussion_card_transcribed.json`;
  - `me_reply_risi_dev.json`, `me_risi_dev_put.json`, `me_risi_dev_reply.json`.
- **Gate:**
  - **Server:** the state machine (every transition, including expiry at 30 s and `left` at 90 s), refusal of
    a Private or unknown call, the bound device, the upload errors and limits, a test that no chunk ever
    reaches disk (tmpfs only), a discard that deletes the posts, the switch-off stop, and the `not_developer`
    refusal.
  - **Android:** the button only on Official calls, the automatic `private_call` decline, the red bar fails
    toward showing, only the own microphone is uploaded, and recording stops on `409 stopped`.
  - **Both:** parse every new example. The release gates apply (APK and protocol).

### 36.11 Sinhala script check (v1.40)
Whisper decoders sometimes answer Sinhala speech in Latin letters ("romanised") or in another script. The
speech service (decision 071, amendment 2026-10-12 b) therefore:
- runs a Sinhala chunk (detected `si`, or a `si` hint) with `language=si` and a short Sinhala-script initial
  prompt;
- calls a text **romanised** when it has letters but **no** Sinhala character (U+0D80–U+0DFF); Tamil or other
  scripts count as romanised too. Code-mixed text with some Sinhala characters is not romanised;
- **normal sessions:** retries a romanised chunk **once** with another Sinhala-tuned model (order:
  `whisper-large-v3-sinhala`, else `whisper-small-sinhala`, whichever is not the first and fits memory). If the
  final text is still romanised, the `call_transcript` of that turn carries `"romanised": true`;
- **compare sessions:** runs every model with the Sinhala prompt (no retry) and adds to each result
  `script: {"expected": "sinh", "sinhala_chars": int, "latin_chars": int, "ok": bool}` (`ok` = not romanised)
  and `romanised: bool`.

Rendering (android): the developer compare card shows the script check per model (e.g. "Sinhala script ✓ 17"
or "romanised ✗ 0 / 23 Latin"). Normal transcript lines are unchanged (no model names, no script badge).
Clients ignore both fields if they don't know them; a v1.39 client is unaffected.

**Examples:** `envelope_risi_call_transcript_compare_si_script.json`,
`envelope_risi_call_transcript_romanised.json`. **Gate:** both sides parse them; the server test checks the
pass-through from the speech service into both kinds.

### 36.12 Live lines in about 2 s: fast sessions (v1.50)
Proposal 2026-10-15-live-call-latency (Harsha P0-1b, 2026-10-15: live lines arrived about 8 s after speech; 3-way
calls need ≤ 2 s). **Additive.** Server flag **`RISI_LIVE_FAST`** (`off` | `canary` | `on`, default `off`;
`canary` = a call with a `RISI_DEV_USERS` participant); Android developer setting **"Fast live call transcript"**
(on unless a developer turns it off; it acts only when the server asks). Applies to §36 and §40.9 sessions alike.

- **The session says "fast" (§36.2).** The server decides at the `request` (never for a `compare` session) and
  keeps it for the session (`risi_call_transcribe_sessions.fast`, additive). `Session.chunk` gains two optional
  fields, **`mode`** (`"vad"`) and **`overlap_ms`** (int); a fast session announces
  `{"min_ms": 1000, "max_ms": 4000, "max_bytes": 262144, "mode": "vad", "overlap_ms": 500}`
  (`call_transcribe_reply_fast.json`). Without `mode` nothing changes. A client that doesn't know `mode` (or sees
  another value) keeps the §36.4 10–15 s chunks, which a fast session accepts too.
- **The phone's chunks (android, normative while `mode` is `"vad"` and the setting is on):**
  - it cuts its own microphone **at pauses** (a 20-ms energy gate over an adaptive noise floor): a chunk closes at a
    pause of **≥ 300 ms** once it is **≥ 2 s** long; at the end of an utterance (**≥ 600 ms** of silence after
    **≥ 200 ms** of speech) even if shorter; at **4 s** at the latest; and is uploaded as soon as it closes
    (§36.4 endpoint and parts unchanged; `duration_ms` 1–4 000);
  - the next chunk starts up to **`overlap_ms` before** the cut (all of it after a 4-s cut; only the trailing
    silence after a pause), so its `offset_ms` may be lower than the previous chunk's end; `seq` still grows;
  - silence before speech is not sent (0.5 s of it leads a chunk); a short keep-alive (≤ 0.6 s) goes out after
    **15 s** without a chunk (the 90-s `left` rule holds); muted audio is silence;
  - everything else in §36.4/§36.6 holds (own microphone only, memory only, zeroed, at most 2 held).
- **The server (normative for a fast session):**
  - **limits:** **40 chunks per participant per 60 s, burst 8; 4 queued** per participant (other §36.4 errors
    unchanged);
  - **priority:** chunks go to the speech service at its **`live`** priority; while the session is on the server
    holds the speech-guard pause file (`reason=live_call`, so evals, the night compare run and the TTS lab start no
    GPU work);
  - **English and unsure** chunks: the language is detected first (`faster-whisper-large-v3`, with the call's hint,
    §36.5); `en` goes to the **fast English model** (`RISI_SPEECH_FAST_EN`, default
    `faster-whisper-large-v3-turbo`) with `language=en`, else (the service can't load it: 503) to the §36.5 route;
    **each chunk with speech is posted at once as its own `call_transcript`** (`turn` + 1; no merging, no 30-s hold;
    `envelope_risi_call_transcript_fast.json`);
  - **Sinhala and Tamil** chunks are only detected, then **joined** per speaker: the speaker's next chunk that follows
    without a pause joins them whatever its own detection says; they are transcribed together (one Ogg/Opus stream
    without the overlaps, the service's `model=auto` route of §36.5/§36.11) and posted as **one** `call_transcript`
    when they reach **8 s** of audio, **≥ 4 s** ending at a phone pause cut (a chunk shorter than 3 s), a pause of
    **≥ 600 ms** after ≥ 4 s, another language or the same speaker's English, **6 s** after the first one arrived, or
    the end of the session. Waiting audio stays in memory only and is dropped untranscribed on stop with `discard`;
  - **overlap:** when a line's audio starts before the end of the same speaker's previous line, the server drops the
    longest head of its text (at most 6 words, compared lower-cased without punctuation) that repeats the end of that
    line; a line left without text is not posted.
  - `call_transcript`'s shape is unchanged; `model` / `made_by.model` name the model that answered.
- **Old apps, old servers:** an old app in a fast session keeps 12-s chunks within the higher limits (its lines are
  posted per chunk); a new app with an old server never sees `mode`. Kill switch: `RISI_LIVE_FAST=off` (the next
  request is a normal session).
- **Speech service (infra, no wire):** `WHISPER_LIVE_RESERVE` (default 0) keeps that many GPU slots free of batch
  work (compare, evals).
- **Measured** (`scripts/call-latency-bench`, 3 speakers, live pilot service, `docs/status/ci.md`): speech→line p50
  en 2.7 s (end of phrase 1.7 s), si 4.2 s, ta 4.7 s, against 8.6–19 s before; WER/CER en 5.3 → 7.0 %,
  si 3.4 → 2.6 %, ta 14 → 9.5 %.
- **Examples:** `call_transcribe_reply_fast.json`, `envelope_risi_call_transcript_fast.json`. **Gate:** server
  `ExamplesV150Test`, `call_transcribe_fast_test.exs`, `call_transcribe_overlap_test.exs`,
  `call_transcribe_ogg_join_test.exs`, `speech_http_test.exs`; Android `ContractExamplesTest`, `VadChunkerTest`,
  `CallTranscribeManagerTest`.

## 37. Voice notes (v1.41)
Decision 081 (NEXT-PHASE §A; proposal 2026-10-11-basic-messaging reserved the type). **Additive:** one MLS
envelope type (`voice`), one device capability (`voice`), one `msg:ack` status (`played`) with its receipt
fields, one Risi kind (`voice_transcript`), one `risi_action` value, one `/auth/config` switch and one user
setting. Old apps drop an unknown envelope type (§10.3) and show Risi kinds as `body`.

### 37.0 Principles
- **A voice note is an E2EE blob, exactly like a photo** (§14): `purpose=media`, format `A256GCM-S64K`
  (§14.3), a fresh key per upload, caps/quota/TTL of §14.5. **A blob is never reused across conversations**
  (§33.5): forward = a fresh upload with a fresh key per target. The server never sees plaintext audio.
- **Private voice notes have no Risi path at all.** Risi is not a member of a Private conversation; no
  client, server or speech code path touches a Private note, and no audio is sent anywhere but the blob store.
- **Official voice notes may be transcribed by Risi**, which is a visible MLS member of Official (§24.11): it
  decrypts the note like any member, transcribes it **in memory**, and discards the audio (§37.6). The UI says
  so honestly (decision 048). The sender can opt out, per note and by setting.
- **Local speech models only** (§36.5 routing and §36.11 script check); no commercial fallback for speech.

### 37.1 Switch, capability, readiness
- **Capability `"voice"`** in `mls.capabilities` (`device_put_voice.json`) once a device implements §37.2–§37.5
  and renders `voice_transcript`. It also means "understands `played`" (§37.4).
- **Readiness:** `GET /api/v1/mls/groups/{conversation_id}` gains **`voice_ready`** and **`missing_voice`**,
  computed exactly as `files_ready` (§33.1). The composer's mic button is shown only when `voice_ready`; a DM
  or group that isn't shows the §33.1 notice ("Kamal needs to update RisiMe to get voice notes").
- **`GET /auth/config`** gains **`"voice_transcribe": "on" | "off"`** (`auth_config_v141.json`; absent =
  `off`): `on` only while `RISI_VOICE_TRANSCRIBE=on` (default `off`), Risi is available and the speech service
  is healthy. It is the kill switch for §37.6; voice notes themselves work whatever it says.

### 37.2 The `voice` envelope (MLS application message)
`voice_payload.json`:
```json
{"v": 1, "type": "voice",
 "blob": {"blob_id": "…", "size": 49168, "sha256": "<b64 SHA-256 of the ciphertext>"},
 "enc": {"alg": "A256GCM-S64K", "key": "<b64 32 bytes>", "plain_size": 49152},
 "mime": "audio/ogg; codecs=opus", "duration_ms": 12400,
 "waveform": "<b64 of exactly 64 bytes>"}
```
- **`blob`, `enc`:** exactly §14.4 (no nonce field: the key is single-use, §14.3). `plain_size` ≤ the
  `media` cap (§33.13).
- **`mime`:** `"audio/ogg; codecs=opus"` (senders always produce this: mono, 48 kHz, 24–32 kbit/s VBR) or
  `"audio/mp4"` (AAC-LC; receive-only, for future iOS/web senders). Receivers play both. Any other value →
  dropped.
- **`duration_ms`:** 500–1 800 000 (30 min; recording stops itself at 30 min).
- **`waveform`:** exactly **64** levels, one byte each (0 = silence, 255 = loudest), the peak amplitude per
  1/64 of the note, normalised to the note's own peak. Base64 of 64 bytes (88 chars). It is drawn as the
  bubble's bars and the playback progress.
- **Optional:**
  - **`"transcribe": false`** (`voice_payload_reply_no_transcribe.json`): the sender's opt-out for this note
    (§37.6). Absent or `true` = allowed. Senders set `false` when their §37.7 setting is off. It is meaningful
    only in Official; it is never set in a Private tab (there is nothing to opt out of).
  - **`reply_to`** (§33.9) and **`forwarded`** (§33.4, `voice_payload_forwarded.json`).
  - **`request_id`** (uuid-v4) in the user's Risi chat (§37.8, `voice_payload_risi_chat.json`) and (v1.49) on an
    Official note recorded with the @Risi chip on (§37.11, `voice_payload_official_chip.json`); absent and ignored
    everywhere else (never in Private, never in a `dm:`). Dropped on forward (§37.3), kept on resend.
- No caption. Envelope bound 22 KiB (§14.4).
- **Strict validation before anything is stored or fetched:** the §14.4 `blob`/`enc` checks; `mime` as above;
  `duration_ms` in range; `waveform` decodes to exactly 64 bytes. Otherwise the envelope is **dropped as
  malformed and logged** (`voice_payload_bad_waveform.json`: 40 bytes).

### 37.3 Client (android, normative where it says "must")
- **Record:** hold the mic to record; slide left to cancel; slide up to lock (hands-free) with [Delete] and
  [Send]. Notes shorter than 0.5 s are discarded. The microphone permission is asked on first use.
- **Play:** the waveform with progress; 1×/1.5×/2× speed (remembered per app); auto-play the next unplayed
  voice note of the same chat; keeps playing with the screen off (foreground media session); switches to the
  earpiece when the proximity sensor is near.
- **Download:** received voice notes **auto-download on any network** (they are small); verify order §14.7.
  The decrypted audio is never written outside `noBackupFilesDir`; the ciphertext cache is not evicted while
  the message exists (hard rule 9).
- **Preview text** (chat list, notifications, quotes, §33.3 copy): "🎤 Voice note 0:12" (`h:mm:ss` from one
  hour); the copy line is `<Voice note 0:12>` (proposal table). A reply quote shows "🎤 Voice note 0:12".
- **Unplayed dot:** a received note shows a dot until it is played on this device (§37.4).
- **Forward (§33.4/§33.5):** a fresh upload with a fresh key per target; `blob`/`enc` new; `mime`,
  `duration_ms`, `waveform` copied; **`transcribe`, `request_id`, `reply_to` dropped**. A forwarded note is
  never transcribed by Risi (§37.6). Targets that aren't `voice_ready` are skipped with the §33.4 notice.
- **Delete (§15):** `msg:delete` `blob_ids` include voice blobs ("media targets" now covers `voice`).
- **Official tab, first use:** before the first Official voice note while `voice_transcribe` is `on`, a one-time
  note: "Risi transcribes voice notes in Official chats. You can turn this off in Settings → Risi." The Private
  tab never shows any Risi text near the mic.

### 37.4 The `played` receipt (blue mic)
- **`msg:ack`** (§2.2) accepts **`"status": "played"`** (`msg_ack_played.json`), sent by a recipient's `voice`
  device when playback of a received voice note **starts** for the first time on that device.
  Never for one's own notes, never for other types (the server can't see the type; clients must not).
- **Order:** `delivered` < `read` < `played`; `played` implies `read` (status only moves forward). A server
  that gets `played` for a message not yet `read` records both.
- **DM:** the server forwards `status: "played"` (`event_status_played.json`) **only to the sender's `voice`
  devices**; other devices of the sender see `read` as before.
- **Group:** `group_receipt` (§12.7) gains **`"played": n`** and **`"all_played": bool`**, present only once
  the message has at least one `played` ack (`event_group_receipt_v141.json`); a `group_receipt` without them
  means 0 / false. It is also sent when `all_played` turns true. `GET …/receipts` rows gain **`played_at`**
  (same rule; `group_receipts_reply_v141.json`).
- **Ticks** on a voice note: ✓ / ✓✓ / accent ✓✓ as today, and the **mic icon turns accent (blue)** when
  `played` (DM) or `all_played` (group). Info (§33.12) gains "Played" times.
- **Own devices:** the unplayed dot is local per device (`played_at` on the row). The server sends `played`
  only once per (message, recipient user): later `played` acks from that user's other devices are accepted and
  ignored.

### 37.5 History, backups, restore
- The `voice` envelope travels in history shares (§17.7) and backups (§22.5) as `payload`, **by reference**
  like images: the backup holds the key, not the audio. After the blob's TTL (§14.5) a restored note shows
  "This voice note is no longer available". (Audio inside backups is reserved for a later backup schema.)
- `backup_entry_message_v141.json`: a message entry gains optional **`played_at`** (incoming voice rows: when
  this user first played it; `null`/absent otherwise) and `status` may be `"played"` for outgoing rows.
- Hard rule 9 applies: an update never deletes a voice row or its cached ciphertext; the upgrade gate counts
  voice notes per conversation alongside photos.

### 37.6 Risi transcription (Official only)
**When.** Risi transcribes a `voice` note **only** when all hold: the conversation is an Official `grp:` with
Official `on` (or the user's Risi chat, §37.8); `voice_transcribe` is `on`; the sender is a human member; the
envelope has no `forwarded` and no `transcribe: false`; the sender's §37.7 setting `transcribe` is not `false`
(the server checks it too: either opt-out wins); `duration_ms` ≤ **300 000** (5 min; longer notes get no
transcript). Never in Private, in any form.

**The audio path (server, normative; §36.4 no-storage rules).**
1. Risi's device receives the MLS message like any member and validates it (§37.2).
2. It fetches the blob (§14.2, as a member), verifies size and SHA-256, decrypts **in memory** (or the speech
   service's size-limited tmpfs), and sends the Ogg/Opus to the speech service on loopback.
3. Routing is §36.5 (detect among `si`/`ta`/`en`, then one model by language) with the §36.11 Sinhala prompt,
   retry and script check.
4. The plaintext audio, the ciphertext and the key are deleted right after transcription and on any error.
   Never written to disk, logs or the learning log.
   **Exception, the §37.8 retry queue only (v1.49):** while a Risi-chat note waits for the speech service, the
   server keeps the note's blob reference (id, size, ciphertext SHA-256) and its key in one row of
   `risi_voice_queue`, **sealed** with AES-256-GCM under `RISI_DATA_KEY` (AAD
   `risi_voice_queue:<conversation_id>:<message_id>`). Never audio (plaintext or ciphertext), never a transcript; the
   job's arguments hold ids only. Each attempt reads the ciphertext from the blob store and decrypts in memory exactly
   as the first. The row is deleted when the note is transcribed, given up (≤ 30 min), or found deleted for everyone.
   Official-group notes are not queued (a failure there is the `failed` card, as before). The §37.12 Try again keeps
   a stopped or failed note in memory only (≤ 10 min), never on disk.

**The kind `voice_transcript`** (a `text` envelope from Risi's leaf **in the same conversation**, §24.11;
`envelope_risi_voice_transcript.json`, `envelope_risi_voice_transcript_unclear.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "voice_transcript", "conversation_id": "grp:…", "message_id": timeuuid (the note),
  "speaker": uuid (the note's authenticated sender), "duration_ms": int,
  "status": "ok" | "unclear" | "failed", "text": str (≤ 4000 chars) | null,
  "language": "si" | "ta" | "en" | null, "script"?: {"expected": "sinh", "sinhala_chars", "latin_chars", "ok"},
  "romanised"?: true, "model": alias | null, "call_ref": null, "made_by", "notify": []}
```
- `status: "ok"`: `text` is the transcript. `"unclear"`: no usable speech, the language probability under
  0.6, or still romanised after the §36.11 retry; `text: null`. `"failed"`: the speech service failed twice;
  `text: null`. `script`/`romanised` as §36.11 (Sinhala only).
- **`body`:** "Voice note from Harsha (0:12): <text>", or "Risi couldn't hear Harsha's voice note (0:04)
  clearly." Silent.
- **Rendering:** every member sees it (Risi is visible, §24.11), **attached under the note's bubble,
  collapsed** to one line ("Transcript ▾"), expandable; no separate bubble. If the note isn't on the phone, it
  shows as a small line. `unclear`/`failed` show "Couldn't transcribe" collapsed. Phones without `voice` show
  `body`.
- **Delete:**
  - deleting the note for everyone (§15) makes Risi delete its `voice_transcript` (sender delete), its buffer
    row and derived items (§38) within 1 min;
  - the note's sender can delete only the transcript: **`risi_action` `transcript_delete`** with
    `target` = the note's `message_id`, `edit: null` (`envelope_risi_action_transcript_delete.json`), sent in
    the same conversation. Anyone else's is ignored.
- **Retention (§36.8, §35.1):** the posted transcript is a Risi post in Official (sealed, kept until deleted,
  §35.3 controls); the working copy in `risi_buffer` is sealed with `RISI_DATA_KEY`, TTL 24 h.
- **Learning log:** one `transcribe` row per note: model, language, audio seconds, latency, status;
  `output: null`. Never text or audio. (v1.49) In Official, one `risi_agent_runs` row (agent `spoken_mention`) per
  §37.11 decision: codes only, never text.

### 37.7 The sender's setting
`GET` / `PUT /api/v1/me/risi/voice` (auth) with `{"transcribe": bool, "action_items": bool}` → `200` the same
shape (`me_risi_voice_put.json`, `me_risi_voice_reply.json`). Both default `true`, stored per user.
- `transcribe: false`: Risi never transcribes this user's Official voice notes; phones also stamp
  `transcribe: false` on each note (§37.2), so the note is honoured even before the server sees the setting.
- `action_items: false`: no §38 extraction from this user's voice notes **or call lines** (§38.6).
- Settings → Risi: "Transcribe my voice notes in Official chats" and "Find action items in my calls and voice
  notes".

### 37.8 Voice notes in the Risi chat (§25.2)
- The composer of the Risi chat sends a `voice` note with a fresh **`request_id`**. Risi transcribes it
  (`transcribe` is ignored there: the note is a request to Risi), posts `voice_transcript` in the Risi chat,
  and then handles `text` exactly as a §24.11 `risi_request` `ask` with that `request_id` (§25 loop, §34
  routing). The **answer is text, in the transcript's language** (`lang` as §36.7), never audio.
- **Risi never says "please type it", anywhere (v1.49, Harsha 2026-10-14).** Every line below is an ordinary
  rule-made `answer` (§24.11) with the note's `request_id` (as the §25 offline notice: a later reply is a second Risi
  message for the same request); no new kind, field or event. No tools run for any of them.
  - **Very short:** a note under **1 000 ms** (500–999 ms pass §37.2) is not transcribed; Risi answers **"That was
    very short. Could you say it again?"**. An `unclear` transcript with no speech (empty or noise only) gets the
    same answer.
  - **`unclear`** otherwise (low language probability, still romanised): the transcript card says so, and Risi
    answers **"I couldn't hear that clearly. Could you say it again?"**.
  - A blob that can't be fetched or verified: the `failed` card and **"I couldn't open that voice note. Could you
    send it again?"**.
  - Over 5 min: **"That voice note is over 5 minutes, too long for me. Could you send a shorter one?"**.
  - **The speech service down, busy, out of memory or timing out** (both §37.6 attempts failed): **no** `failed`
    card yet and **never** "type it". Risi at once answers **"Got your voice note. I'll reply in a moment."** (an
    `answer` with the note's `request_id`, then `risi_progress` `done`) and queues the note. If the service already
    reports unhealthy when the note arrives, the acknowledgement goes out before the first attempt (the note is still
    tried).
  - **The retry queue:** a job retries with backoff (15 s, doubling, at most every 5 min: 15, 30, 60, 120, 240, 300, 300 … s) while the speech
    service is unhealthy or failing (storage: the §37.6 step 4 exception). When it answers, the note gets its normal
    `voice_transcript` card and is handled as the §24.11 `ask` (a second `answer` for the same `request_id`).
  - **Bounded give-up** after **30 min** (server config): the `failed` card, and Risi answers **"I couldn't listen
    to your voice note: my speech service isn't answering. Please send it again a bit later."**
  - A note deleted for everyone while it waits is dropped silently.
- **The switch off for this user (v1.49):** when `RISI_VOICE_TRANSCRIBE` is `off`, or `canary` and the sender isn't
  in `RISI_DEV_USERS`, a Risi-chat note gets the answer **"Voice notes to Risi are coming soon for your account."**.
  `canary` covers the Risi chat path: a `RISI_DEV_USERS` sender's notes are transcribed there. `/auth/config`
  `voice_transcribe` stays `off` under `canary` (decision 048 honesty).
- No §38 extraction from Risi-chat notes (they are requests).

### 37.9 Amendments, examples, gate
- **§2.2:** `msg:ack` status `played`. **§12.7:** `group_receipt` `played`/`all_played`, receipt rows
  `played_at`. **§15.2:** media targets include `voice`. **§22.5:** `played_at`, `status: "played"`.
  **§33.4/§33.9:** `voice` is forwardable and repliable (fields above). **§24.11:** `risi_action`
  `transcript_delete`.
- **Examples:** `auth_config_v141.json`, `device_put_voice.json`, `voice_payload.json`,
  `voice_payload_reply_no_transcribe.json`, `voice_payload_forwarded.json`, `voice_payload_risi_chat.json`,
  `voice_payload_bad_waveform.json`, `msg_ack_played.json`, `event_status_played.json`,
  `event_group_receipt_v141.json`, `group_receipts_reply_v141.json`, `envelope_risi_voice_transcript.json`,
  `envelope_risi_voice_transcript_unclear.json`, `envelope_risi_action_transcript_delete.json`,
  `me_risi_voice_put.json`, `me_risi_voice_reply.json`, `backup_entry_message_v141.json`.
- **Gate:** NEXT-PHASE §A (two-phone send/receive/play, the upgrade gate with voice counts, no plaintext audio
  on the server: a test that the blob store and logs hold ciphertext only); server: `played` ordering and
  delivery filter, the Risi audio path never touches disk, no Risi path for a Private conversation, a forwarded
  or `transcribe: false` note is not transcribed; android: strict validation (bad waveform dropped), forward
  re-upload, the Private tab has no Risi text. The full release gates apply (APK, protocol).

### 37.10 The sender phone's own transcript (v1.47, Part 12)
Applied from `contract/proposals/2026-10-14-phone-transcript.md`. Additive and optional: no capability, no readiness
change; old apps ignore the field. Android sends it only behind the developer flag `phone_stt` (Harsha's
`risi_dev` account first).

`voice` envelope (§37.2) gains one optional field **`phone_transcript`**:

| field | type | rule |
|---|---|---|
| `phone_transcript` | object, optional | absent = no phone transcript (every note before v1.47, and every note while the flag is off) |
| `phone_transcript.text` | string | 1–4000 characters after trimming, ≤ 12 000 UTF-8 bytes; the recogniser's final text (segments joined with one space) |
| `phone_transcript.lang` | string | the BCP-47 locale tag the recogniser ran with, 2–35 chars, e.g. `"si-LK"`, `"ta-LK"`, `"ta-IN"`, `"en-US"`, `"en-IN"`. Singlish (Sinhala with English mixed) is recognised as `"si-LK"`: no on-device pack exists for code-mixed speech |
| `phone_transcript.engine` | string | 1–64 chars; v1.47 Android senders write **`"android-on-device"`**; receivers accept any value (e.g. a future `"apple-on-device"`) and don't show it |


#### Sender rules (normative)
- **On-device only.** Only an on-device recogniser may produce it. A phone must **never** use an online recogniser
  (Android: never `SpeechRecognizer.createSpeechRecognizer`, never `ACTION_RECOGNIZE_SPEECH` activities); if the
  chosen language has no installed on-device pack, the note goes without `phone_transcript` and the UI says so. No
  audio leaves the phone except the §37.2 E2EE blob.
  - **Exception (v1.49, Harsha 2026-10-14): a `risi_dev` account's explicit opt-in.** Only a `risi_dev` account whose
    user turned the developer option on for themselves may attach the transcript of Google's **online** recogniser,
    with **`engine: "android-online-google"`**, on their own notes in Official and their own Risi chat (never Private:
    there the on-device rule holds without exception). The opt-in's UI says plainly that the audio of those notes goes
    to Google. Everyone else is unchanged (on-device only). The other sender rules below hold for it too.
- Only the device that **recorded** the note writes it, from its own recording, before encryption.
- **Not written:** on a note with `transcribe: false` (the sender opted out of transcription in Official, so no
  text of that note goes to Risi's leaf either); on a **forward** (§37.3: dropped like `transcribe`/`request_id`).
- Allowed in Private, Official and (v1.49, Harsha 2026-10-14) **the user's Risi chat**, where Risi may compare it with
  its own transcript and fall back to it (Server / Risi below). It is part of the MLS-encrypted content (never
  plaintext metadata).
- Best effort: the recogniser may take at most a few seconds after the recording stops (Android: 8 s); after that
  the note is sent without it. Never delays a note beyond that, never blocks it.

#### Receiver rules
- §0 old apps ignore the unknown field; the note plays as before. **No capability**, no readiness change.
- Validation is lenient: a malformed `phone_transcript` (wrong types, empty or too long `text`, missing `lang` or
  `engine`) is **ignored and the note is kept** (unlike the §37.2 strict fields).
- Rendering: under the bubble, collapsed to one line **"Transcript (sender's phone) ▾"**, expandable (the same
  block as §37.6). In Official, a Risi `voice_transcript` (§37.6) is shown first, then this one. Shown in Private too.
  Never claims to be Risi's.
- Copy/forward/quote texts are unchanged (§37.3 previews stay "🎤 Voice note 0:12").
- Backups (§22.5) and history shares (§17.7) carry the envelope as `payload`, so it travels with them.

#### Server / Risi
- The server sees only a larger ciphertext (it already learns the envelope size). Honest note for decision 048:
  nothing else is learned.
- **Risi's device in Official and in the user's Risi chat can decrypt the field** (never Private: Risi isn't a
  member there). In v1.47 Risi ignored it. **From v1.49 (proposal
  2026-10-14-phone-transcript-fallback) Risi may use a valid `phone_transcript` only as a guarded fallback input**,
  behind the server flag `RISI_VOICE_PHONE_FALLBACK` (`off` | `canary` | `on`, default `off`; `off` = the v1.47
  behaviour, the field ignored):
  - **When:** only when Risi's own transcription (§37.6) times out or fails. The live time budget is **3 × the note's
    duration, at least 15 s**. Order: (1) Risi's routed model (§36.5) within the budget; (2) the note's
    `phone_transcript`, if present and valid (receiver rules above); (3) a fast local model (omniASR-CTC-1B, CPU int8)
    on the same audio; (4) otherwise `status: "failed"` as today.
  - **Never a primary path:** Risi never skips its own model because a phone transcript exists (the compare view and
    the Voice agent's transcription Index keep scoring both). One exception, the `risi_dev` opt-in below.
  - **The post** is the normal §37.6 `voice_transcript` with `status: "ok"`, the phone's `text`, `language` = the
    primary subtag of `lang` when it is `si` / `ta` / `en` (else the script's language or `null`; the §36.11 Sinhala
    script check applies), `model: "phone-on-device"`, and two new optional fields: **`"source": "phone"`** (absent =
    Risi's own model) and **`"engine"`** (the `phone_transcript.engine` it came from). `made_by` = `{"model":
    "phone-on-device", "provider": "phone", …}` (`envelope_risi_voice_transcript_phone.json`).
  - **Google transcript (the §37.10 `risi_dev` opt-in):** `engine: "android-online-google"` and `model:
    "phone-google"` (`envelope_risi_voice_transcript_google.json`). For a `risi_dev` user's own notes the server may use
    it even while `RISI_VOICE_PHONE_FALLBACK` is off (the user's choice): when Risi's model fails the hallucination
    guard, or when the Voice agent's per-user route for Sinhala is `phone` (Google leads on the user's labelled notes;
    Risi's model takes over once it beats Google by the promotion rule).
  - **Rendering:** a phone that knows `source` shows **one** transcript block for the note when `source` is `phone`:
    **"Transcript (Google)"** for `engine: "android-online-google"`, **"Transcript (sender's phone)"** for any other
    engine. Old phones show the post as today (`body`).
  - **Same rules as Risi's own transcript:** the opt-outs still win (a note with `transcribe: false` carries no
    `phone_transcript` anyway); memory, the Ledger and §38 see it like any `ok` transcript; the learning-log row has
    `model: "phone-on-device"` (or `phone-google`), `output: null`; the text is never logged. Never Private (Risi isn't
    a member). §37.11 never runs on a phone transcript.

#### Size
Envelope bound stays 22 KiB (§14.4): 12 000 bytes of text + ≈ 600 bytes of the rest fit.

- **Examples:** `voice_payload_phone_transcript.json` (valid), `voice_payload_phone_transcript_bad.json` (the
  field has no `lang`: ignored, the note is kept and valid).
- **Gate:** android: encode/validate round trip; a malformed field ignored with the note kept; the field absent →
  envelope bytes identical to v1.46 (parity); forward drops it; the online recogniser is never constructed.
  server: both examples pass the §37.2 validator; Risi ignores the field (no post, no log). The full release gates
  apply (APK, protocol).
- **v1.49 examples:** `envelope_risi_voice_transcript_phone.json`, `envelope_risi_voice_transcript_google.json`.
  **v1.49 gate:** server: the fallback order (timeout → phone → CTC → failed), the budget formula, the flag off → the
  v1.47 behaviour (field ignored), no text in logs or `risi_agent_runs`. Android: `source: "phone"` renders once, with
  the engine's label. Server-only behind the flag (Risi fast lane); the rendering ships with an APK.

### 37.11 Spoken @Risi in Official (v1.49)
Applied from `contract/proposals/2026-10-14-spoken-risi.md` (Harsha 2026-10-14 06:09 and 07:41 SLT; it supersedes the
never-applied `2026-10-13-voice-address-risi.md`). **No new envelope, `kind` or `risi_request` field.** Two additive
changes: the optional `risi.voice_ref` on Risi's replies, and the §37.2 `request_id` on an Official note recorded with
the @Risi chip (a meaning of an existing optional field; old servers ignore it there, old phones never send it there).
No capability, no readiness, no `/auth/config` switch.

**Scope (server, normative).** Only an Official `grp:` with Official `on` (1:1 and group Official alike). **Never
Private, never a `dm:` conversation, never the user's Risi chat** (there every note already is a request, §37.8). It
runs only on Risi's own §37.6 transcript of a note that was transcribed (`status: "ok"`), so every §37.6 condition
holds: a human member's own note, not forwarded, no `transcribe: false`, the sender's §37.7 `transcribe` not `false`,
≤ 5 min. `phone_transcript` (§37.10) is never used for this.

**Addressed vs mentioned.** The note counts as @Risi only when Risi is **addressed**: a vocative at the start of the
utterance or of a sentence, followed by a request. Merely mentioning Risi never counts. Decision order (the first that
applies):
1. **Name forms** (whole word; case-insensitive Latin): `risi`, `rishi`, `risee`, `reesi`, `reesee`, `risie`, `rizi`,
   `risy`; Sinhala `රිසි`, `රිසී`, `රීසි`, `රිශි`; Tamil `ரிசி`, `ரிஷி`, `ரீசி`. A form followed by a letter, a combining
   mark or an apostrophe is not the name in vocative use (`Risi's`, `රිසිට`, `ரிசிக்கு` are mentions).
2. **A member's name.** If an active human member of the conversation has a first name equal to the form heard (e.g. a
   colleague called Rishi), the note is **not** @Risi.
3. **Sentence start.** Sentences are split at `.` `!` `?` `।` `…` and line breaks. A sentence qualifies only when, after
   optional greetings (`hey`, `hi`, `hello`, `ok`, `okay`, `oh`, `so`, `aney`, `අනේ`, `හලෝ`, `ஏய்`, `ஹலோ`, …), it
   **starts** with a name form.
4. **Addressed (rules)** when the name is followed by
   - a vocative separator (`,` `:` `!` `-` `–` `—`, or the sentence ends there, e.g. "Risi? Remind me…") and the
     remainder is a request: it contains a request cue or ends with `?`; or
   - directly (speech often has no comma) by a request cue as its first word(s).
   Request cues (en/si/ta/Singlish): `can/could/would/will you`, `please`, `remind`, `tell me`, question words
   (`what`, `when`, `where`, `who`, `how`, `which`, `why`), `set`, `add`, `schedule`, `book`, `send`, `summarise`,
   `find`, `show`, `check`, `note`, `list`, `cancel`, `help`; `මට`, `කරුණාකර`, `මතක් කරන්න`, `කියන්න`, `බලන්න`,
   `පුළුවන්ද`, `මොකක්ද`, `කවදාද`, …; `எனக்கு`, `தயவு`, `நினைவூட்டு`, `சொல்லு`, `என்ன`, `எப்போ`, `முடியுமா`, …;
   `mata`, `mage`, `mathak karanna`, `kiyanna`, `balanna`, `puluwanda`, `monada`, `kawadada`, `enakku`, `sollu`, …
5. **Mentioned (rules)** when the name is followed by a reporting or descriptive word (`said`, `says`, `told`, `is`,
   `was`, `has`, `did`, `knows`, `and`, `about`, `කිව්වා`, `කියනවා`, `ගැන`, `சொன்னது`, `சொன்னார்`, `பற்றி`, `kiwwa`,
   `gana`, …), or appears anywhere but a sentence start.
6. **Unsure**: a vocative separator with no request in the remainder ("Risi, the venue is booked."), a request cue
   later in the sentence but not first ("Risi හෙට meeting එක මතක් කරන්න"), or a trailing vocative ("Can you remind me,
   Risi?"). Unsure goes to the Fast router's model (`RISI_ROUTER_MODEL`, the local loopback model; never a commercial
   fallback) with a ≤ 40-token yes/no prompt; it counts as addressed only with **confidence ≥ 0.9**. No model
   configured, an error, a timeout (1.5 s) or a lower confidence: **not @Risi**.

When unsure, the note is **never treated as a request**, but Risi is **never silent** to a note that addresses it (below).
A note that merely mentions Risi is never acted on at all (zero false positives).

**The @Risi chip on a voice note.** In Official, when the @Risi chip is on while the user records, the app stamps the
note's §37.2 **`request_id`** (a fresh UUIDv4, `voice_payload_official_chip.json`), exactly as it does for every note
in the Risi chat (§37.8). The app keeps it on resend; it is dropped on forward. The server then handles the note as
that user's `ask` with that `request_id` **whatever its words** (no name needed; a leading "Risi, …" is dropped from
the question), and:
- sends `risi_progress` `working` with that `request_id` as soon as the note arrives ("Risi is typing…" under the note);
- transcribes it even with `transcribe: false` or the sender's §37.7 `transcribe` off: the chip is the sender's explicit
  request for this note (as §37.8);
- a note over 5 min gets the fixed §37.8 "too long" answer; an `unclear`/`failed` transcript gets "I couldn't hear
