  that clearly. Could you say it again?" (never "type it", §37.8). Never silence.
Phones that don't know the Official meaning ignore the field (unchanged display).

**Never silent.** When a note is addressed to Risi but the request is unclear (step 4 with nothing asked: "Hey Risi.",
"Risi, the venue is booked.") or the address is borderline (step 6 and the model is below 0.9 or absent), Risi posts a
rule-made `answer` in the same Official conversation (`notify: [sender]`, `voice_ref`, `refs: [note]`, `request_id` =
the derived id below) asking back in the note's language, e.g. "Did you want me to do something with this? Tell me what
you need: start a voice note with "Risi, …" or use @Risi." The note itself stays an ordinary message (§27/§38 as usual).

**Effect when addressed (and the flag is on for the sender).** The note is handled as a §24.11 `risi_request` `ask` from
its sender, exactly as if the @Risi chip had been used: the same §24.13/§25.6 limits and queue, the §25 loop, §34
routing and the "never silent" progress (`risi_progress` with the `request_id` below).
- `request_id` = a deterministic UUID (version 4 layout) derived from the note's `message_id`, so a repeat (a
  re-delivery, a restarted job) is idempotent: one request, one answer.
- `text` = the transcript from the word after the vocative to the end (1–1000 grapheme clusters). The rest of the
  transcript is context only.
- The answer is in the transcript's `language` (as §37.8). It is the ordinary Risi reply of that request (`answer`,
  `confirm`, `draft`, `error`, …) in the **same Official conversation**, visible to all members, `notify: [sender]`.
- An addressed (or chip) note is a request, so (as §37.8) it gets **no §38 action-items extraction and is not counted
  by the §27.2 quiet rule**: its promises come from the request's own confirm cards, never twice. The §37.6 transcript
  is still posted as usual.
- Risi's memory opt-out (§35.3) does not block the request (a request is not learning, as typed @Risi).

**`risi.voice_ref` (additive, optional; `envelope_risi_answer_voice_ref.json`).** Every Risi post made for a spoken or
chip request carries in its `risi` object **`"voice_ref": {"message_id": timeuuid (the note), "speaker": uuid (its
sender)}`**, and an `answer`'s `refs` contains the note's `message_id`.
- Phones that know `voice_ref` draw the card as a reply to the note (the §33.9 quote block, rendered from the local copy
  of the note: "🎤 Voice note 0:12", tap scrolls to it), and may show a "Risi is typing…" bubble for a `risi_progress`
  whose `request_id` they did not create when it arrives right after their own Official voice note.
- Phones that don't know it ignore it (§0) and show the card as today.
- `voice_ref` is honoured only from a leaf whose attested kind is `agent` and only in Official (§24.11 rule for `risi`).

**Learning log and shadow.** One run row per decision (`risi_agent_runs`, agent `spoken_mention`): mode
(`shadow`/`canary`/`on`), decision (`addressed`/`unsure_yes`/`chip` → request; `ask`/`unsure_ask` → ask back;
`mentioned`), by (`rules`/`model`/`chip`), language, latency, reason code, hashed user. **Never text** (§24.12). The
model check, when it runs, is an ordinary `RisiMe.Agent.LLM` call (task `spoken_mention`): its learning-log row holds
the prompt's SHA-256 and the note id, never the transcript.

**Switch (server flag, read at runtime).** `RISI_SPOKEN_MENTION` = `off` | `shadow` | `canary` | `on`, default
**`canary`**: `off` no detection; `shadow` detect and log for everyone, nobody's note becomes a request; `canary` notes
from `RISI_DEV_USERS` act, everybody else is shadow; `on` everybody acts. The chip path acts under the same flag.

**Eval.** Golden set `spoken_mention` (`server/test/risime/agent/golden/spoken_mention.jsonl`): addressed and merely
mentioned examples in English, Sinhala, Tamil and Singlish (romanised forms, realistic speech with fillers and mixed
script), each labelled `expect: "request" | "ask" | "none"`. Gate: **zero false positives** and ≥ 85 % recall on the
addressed cases by rules alone; the model path is scored with `scripts/risi-eval --live`.

- **Examples:** `envelope_risi_answer_voice_ref.json`, `voice_payload_official_chip.json`.
- **Gate:** server (fake speech service and fake LLM): addressed forms in all four languages → exactly one request job
  with the remainder as `text`; merely mentioned → none; the golden set (0 false positives); unsure without a model →
  none, under 0.9 → none, ≥ 0.9 → one; Private / `dm:` → no path; the Risi chat → the §37.8 path only; `unclear` /
  `failed` → no request; a repeat → the same `request_id`; `shadow` and `canary` for a non-dev user → a run row and no
  request; no transcript text in run rows or logs; an addressed note gets no §38 extraction; a chip note is an ask
  whatever its words. Server: Risi fast lane. Android: the chip on Official voice notes ships in the next APK; `voice_ref` rendering
  follows in a later APK.

### 37.12 Transcription progress, Stop and Try again (v1.49)
Applied from `contract/proposals/2026-10-14-transcribe-progress.md` (Harsha 2026-10-14 07:41 SLT, the Sinhala-slow
incident; the proposal named it §37.11, renumbered). **Additive:** one ephemeral signal kind and two endpoints, behind
the server flag **`RISI_VOICE_PROGRESS`** (`off` | `canary` | `on`, default `off`; `canary` = `RISI_DEV_USERS` only).
Old apps ignore the unknown signal kind (§2.3) and never call the endpoints. **Never Private.**

**Signal `voice_transcribing`** (§2.3 `signal`; ephemeral, never stored, never replayed; `signal_voice_transcribing.json`).
Sent **only to the note's sender** (every live socket of the sender's user; a Risi-chat note goes only to `risi_tools`
sockets, as every Risi-chat event, §25.2), only while the flag is on for the sender:
```
{"kind": "voice_transcribing",
 "data": {"conversation_id", "message_id" (the note), "state", "language", "seq", "can_cancel", "can_retry", "server_ts"}}
```
| field | rule |
|---|---|
| `state` | `working` (Risi is transcribing the note) \| `done` (finished: the §37.6 `voice_transcript` follows or has arrived, whatever its status) \| `cancelled` (stopped by the sender) \| `failed` (no transcript: the speech service and every fallback failed; the §37.6 `failed` card is also posted) |
| `language` | the expected language (`si` \| `ta` \| `en`) from the sender's and the chat's language hint (decision 071 d), or `null`. A hint, not a detection: the app shows "Transcribing (Sinhala)…" for `si`, "Transcribing (Tamil)…" for `ta`, "Transcribing…" otherwise |
| `seq` | grows by 1 per signal of one note; a client drops a signal with a lower `seq` than one already applied |
| `can_cancel` | true while `working` |
| `can_retry` | true on `cancelled` / `failed` while the server still holds the note's key **in memory** (at most 10 min after the stop or failure; never on disk, §36.4) |

**Stop and Try again** (REST, auth; the note's sender only). Body `{"conversation_id", "message_id"}`
(`me_risi_voice_cancel.json`). These two endpoints answer errors as `{"error": "<code>"}` (a bare code string, not the
§0 object); clients act on the HTTP status.
- **`POST /api/v1/me/risi/voice/cancel`**
  - `200 {"state": "cancelled", "can_retry": bool}` (`me_risi_voice_cancel_reply.json`): the transcription is stopped;
    **nothing is posted** for the note (no `voice_transcript`, no §38 items); the note itself is untouched. A
    `cancelled` signal also goes out.
  - `404 {"error": "not_running"}`: nothing is running for that note (already done, not the caller's note, or the flag
    off for the caller).
- **`POST /api/v1/me/risi/voice/retry`** (same body)
  - `202 {"state": "working"}` (`me_risi_voice_retry_reply.json`): transcription starts again (signals as above).
  - `409 {"error": "running"}`: it is still running.
  - `410 {"error": "expired"}`: the server no longer holds the note (over 10 min, a restart, the note was deleted, or
    it never held it): the app shows "Can't try again. Send the note again." and hides Try again.
  - `404 {"error": "not_found"}`: not the caller's note, or the flag off for the caller.
- Both endpoints refuse any conversation that is not an Official `grp:` or the caller's Risi chat: Risi never had a
  Private note, so nothing is ever running or held for one.

**Client (Android, normative where it says "must").**
- Under the sender's **own** voice-note bubble (Official tab and the Risi chat only; never Private):
  - `working`: one line "Transcribing (Sinhala)… " + **[Stop]** (when `can_cancel`);
  - `cancelled`: "Transcription stopped" + **[Try again]** (when `can_retry`);
  - `failed`: "Couldn't transcribe" + **[Try again]** (when `can_retry`);
  - `done`: the line disappears (the §37.6 transcript block shows as before).
- Stop → `cancel`; Try again → `retry` (on `410`: the "Can't try again" line, no button).
- The state is in memory only (per process): never persisted, never backed up. A `working` line with no signal for
  `max(60 s, 4 × duration)` disappears. Old servers never send the signal: nothing changes.

**Server notes (non-normative).** The running transcription's task is kept per (conversation, note) in memory; Stop
kills it (its loopback speech request is dropped). The validated note (blob id, key, sizes) stays in memory for Try
again at most 10 min, then is dropped; a restart drops it. Codes and timings only in logs and `risi_agent_runs`
(`agent=voice`, `mode=stt`, `status=cancelled`); never text.

- **Examples:** `signal_voice_transcribing.json`, `me_risi_voice_cancel.json`, `me_risi_voice_cancel_reply.json`,
  `me_risi_voice_retry_reply.json`.
- **Gate:** server: signal shape and `seq`; cancel stops the task and posts nothing; retry re-runs; 410 after expiry;
  Private refused; the flag off → 404 and no signal. Android: the line per state; Stop/Try again call the endpoints;
  an old server (no signal) unchanged; Private never shows the line. APK hotfix gates (additive, no protocol break).

### 37.13 Wrong language and Translate (v1.49)
Official only (an Official `grp:` with Official on, §37.6), never Private, never the user's Risi chat. Available to a
user when `GET /api/v1/me/risi/voice` says `"translate_available": true` (the server's `RISI_VOICE_TRANSLATE` is on
for that user). Otherwise the phone shows neither link.

#### 37.13.1 "Wrong language?" (re-transcribe with a language)
Under every `voice_transcript` (§37.6) with `status` `ok` or `unclear`, every member (sender and receivers) sees
**"Wrong language?"**. It opens three choices, **Tamil / Sinhala / English**, without the transcript's own
`language`. One tap sends, in the same conversation, a **`risi_action` `transcript_language`**
(`envelope_risi_action_transcript_language.json`):
```
{"v": 1, "type": "risi_action", "action": "transcript_language", "target": "<the note's message_id>",
 "edit": null, "language": "si" | "ta" | "en",
 "voice": {"blob": {…the note's §37.2 blob…}, "enc": {…the note's §37.2 enc…}, "duration_ms": int}}
```
- `voice` is copied from the note's own envelope (the tapping member has it): Risi deletes a note's key right after
  transcribing it (§37.6), so the request carries it again, inside MLS like the note. Never sent outside MLS.
- **Server (normative):** the action is honoured only when the sender is an active human member, the conversation is
  Official, the flag is on for the sender, `language` is one of `si`/`ta`/`en`, Risi posted a transcript for
  `target`, and `voice.blob.blob_id` and `sha256` equal the note's (recorded when Risi transcribed it; a note
  transcribed before v1.49 has no record and is refused). At most **3** per note per 10 minutes (more are ignored).
  Then Risi fetches and decrypts the blob as §37.6 (in memory, deleted after), transcribes it **with that language
  forced** (§36.5 route for that language, no detection), and:
  1. posts a **new `voice_transcript`** for the same `message_id` with the new text and `language`, plus
     **`"relang": {"from": "si" | "ta" | "en" | null, "to": "ta", "by": uuid}`**
     (`envelope_risi_voice_transcript_relang.json`);
  2. deletes its earlier `voice_transcript` post (sender delete, §15) and everything learned from it (as
     `transcript_delete`, §37.6: memory, facts, promises, the working copy, §38 items), then the new transcript goes
     through the normal §37.6 understanding path (memory, Ledger, §38 action items);
  3. updates the **speaker's** language profile (the note's milliseconds move from `from` to `to`, one correction
     counted; codes and numbers only) and writes the learning-log `transcribe` row with `"relang": true`, the two
     language codes, `output: null`. Never text or audio.
  If the speech service fails, the earlier transcript stays and nothing is posted.
- **Receivers:** a `voice_transcript` with `relang` **replaces** any earlier transcript of the same `message_id` (the
  later envelope wins even if the delete hasn't arrived). It renders as §37.6 with a small line "Re-transcribed in
  Tamil" (the `to` language's name). Old phones: the delete removes the old card, the new one renders as today.

#### 37.13.2 Translate (per viewer)
- Every transcript keeps its **original language and script** (unchanged §37.6).
- Under a `voice_transcript` with `status: "ok"`, and under every **typed `text` message in Official whose text has
  Sinhala or Tamil letters**, the phone shows **"Translate to Sinhala / English / Tamil"** without the text's own
  language (§37.13.3 `language`).
- One tap calls **`POST /api/v1/risi/translate`** (auth; `risi_translate_request.json`):
  `{"conversation_id": "grp:…", "message_id": timeuuid, "target": "en" | "si" | "ta"}` (v1.54: or any code in
  `translate_languages`, below; another one is `422 bad_request`)
  → **`200`** (`risi_translate_reply.json`):
  `{"conversation_id", "message_id", "target", "source_language": "si" | "ta" | "en" | null, "text": str (≤ 4000),
  "label": "Translated by Risi", "made_by": {"model": alias, "provider": "risime", "at", "also": []}}`.
  Errors: `403 {"error": "not_available"}` (flag off for the user, not Official, not a member, a Risi chat or
  Private); `404 {"error": "not_found"}` (Risi doesn't hold that message's text: never seen, deleted, or its
  speaker opted out of memory); `422 bad_request` (the §0 error object); `429 {"error": "rate_limited"}` (30 per user per
  10 min); `503 {"error": "unavailable"}` (the model is down).
- The server translates **only text Risi already holds for that Official message** (the transcript's working copy,
  or Risi's sealed memory of a typed message); the request never carries message text. It runs on **risi-l1**,
  on demand. The translation is **not posted, not stored, not remembered and not logged** (the learning log row is
  `task: "translate"`, the two language codes, latency, `output: null`). The phone keeps the result in memory for
  the session and doesn't ask twice (no server cache in v1.49).
- **Rendering:** shown **only on the tapping person's phone**, under the original, labelled **"Translated by
  Risi"** (never a chat message, never sent to anyone). It isn't backed up and isn't in history shares.
- **Setting** (per user, §37.7): `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/me/risi/voice` gain `"always_translate": "off" | "en" | "si" |
  "ta"` (default `"off"`; `me_risi_voice_put_v149.json`, `me_risi_voice_reply_v149.json`) and the read-only
  `"translate_available": bool` (ignored on `PUT`). While Translate isn't available to the user the server sends the
  v1.41 shape unchanged: both fields absent (absent = not available; `always_translate` on a `PUT` is then ignored).
  With a target set, the phone requests the translation itself
  when it shows a transcript (or a typed si/ta message) whose language isn't the target, and shows it expanded. A
  `PUT` without `always_translate` keeps the stored value. Settings → Risi: "Always translate voice notes to:
  Off / English / Sinhala / Tamil".
- **More languages (v1.54, architect 2026-10-16 14:05 item 3; proposal 2026-10-16-translate-languages):** `GET` /
  `PUT /api/v1/me/risi/voice` gain the read-only `"translate_languages": ["si", "en", "ta", "hi", …]`: every target
  this server translates to, lowercase ISO 639-1 codes (639-3 only where no 639-1 code exists). Present only while
  `translate_available` is true; absent = `["en", "si", "ta"]`; ignored on a `PUT` (`me_risi_voice_reply_v154.json`).
  `target` and `always_translate` accept any listed code. The phone shows Sinhala / English / Tamil (without the
  text's own language), then "More languages…": a searchable list of `translate_languages`, the user's recent picks
  (this phone, up to 5) first; without the field, the three buttons only. Unchanged: "Wrong language?" (§37.13.1)
  stays `si | ta | en`, and §37.13.3 detection stays si / ta / en.

#### 37.13.3 The text's language
- A transcript's `language` (§37.6). After v1.49 the language always matches the text's script (Sinhala letters →
  `si`, Tamil letters → `ta`).
- A typed message: Sinhala letters (U+0D80–U+0DFF) → `si`, Tamil letters (U+0B80–U+0BFF) → `ta` (the one with
  more letters when both), else `en`. Latin-script Singlish counts as `en` and gets no Translate link (v1.49).

#### 37.13.4 Memory, the Ledger and action items
Risi's understanding of a Sinhala or Tamil note or message works from **the original plus an internal English
rendering** (risi-l1, made when the note is understood): §38 extraction sees both, so a Tamil note makes the same
action items an English one would. The rendering lives only in Risi's sealed working copy (24 h) and is never
posted, logged or written to the learning log; items keep §38's language rules.

#### 37.13.5 Amendments
- §24.11 `risi_action`: `transcript_language` (fields above).
- §37.6 `voice_transcript`: optional `relang`; a later transcript of the same `message_id` replaces the earlier.
- §37.7: `always_translate`, `translate_available`.
- §2 REST list: `POST /api/v1/risi/translate`.

- **Examples:** `envelope_risi_action_transcript_language.json`, `envelope_risi_voice_transcript_relang.json`,
  `risi_translate_request.json`, `risi_translate_reply.json`, `me_risi_voice_put_v149.json`,
  `me_risi_voice_reply_v149.json`.
- **Gate:** as the proposal (server: `transcript_language` honoured / refused, the forced language, `relang`, the
  old post and its learned rows deleted, codes-only logs; `translate` 200/403/404/422/429/503, nothing posted or
  stored). Server behind `RISI_VOICE_TRANSLATE` (default `canary`); Android ships with a later APK (the full
  release gates apply).

## 38. Risi voice → action items (v1.42)
Decision 082 (Part 5). **One pipeline:** the §27.2 extraction (already run on §36 call transcripts, §36.7)
gains item kinds and line references, and also runs on §37.6 voice-note transcripts. **Scope: Official calls
(§36) and Official voice notes only.** Never Private, never the Risi chat. Additive: two Risi kinds, three
`risi_action` values, a `/auth/config` switch, and fields on My promises.

### 38.0 Principles
- **Visible to all, confirmed by the owner.** One `action_items` card in the Official conversation lists what
  Risi heard for everyone; each owner confirms **their own** items in their Risi chat. Nothing is tracked
  without the owner's ✓ (§27.5).
- **Honest about hearing.** When the speech was unclear, Risi says so and extracts nothing.
- **Every item links back** to the exact line it came from.

### 38.1 Switch
`GET /auth/config` gains **`"risi_action_items": "on" | "off"`** (`auth_config_v142.json`; absent = `off`;
server env `RISI_ACTION_ITEMS`, default `off`). While `off`, §36.7 runs unchanged (§27.3/§27.4 kinds) and no
voice-note extraction happens. While `on`, for the sources in scope `action_items` replaces the §27.4
`discussion_card` for voice notes; a **call** posts the §27.4 `discussion_card` (summary and key points) **and then**
`action_items`, sharing one `summary_id` (P0 2026-10-15: a call always gets its summary; long calls are summarised in
sections, server-side, no wire change), and `action_confirm` replaces the §27.3 `discussion_summary` **items** for owners with a
`risi_ledger` device (others keep the §27.3 legacy path unchanged).

### 38.2 Triggers (server, normative)
- **Call:** the §36.7 trigger (≥ 30 s of speech from ≥ 2 speakers, at `ended` or `stopped` without `discard`).
- **Voice note:** a `voice_transcript` with `status: "ok"` and ≥ 5 s of speech, whose sender's
  `action_items` setting is not `false`. Context: the note's transcript only (plus the members' names for
  owner resolution).
- **Quality gate → "couldn't hear clearly":** no items are extracted, and an `action_items` card with
  `status: "unclear"` is posted, when the source's majority-language probability is under **0.6**, or more
  than **25 %** of its Sinhala turns are `romanised` (§36.11), or (voice note) the transcript was `unclear`.
  `reason`: `"low_confidence"` | `"script_check"` | `"no_speech"`. A clear source with no items posts nothing.

### 38.3 The Official card: `action_items`
A `text` envelope from Risi's leaf in the Official conversation (`envelope_risi_action_items.json`,
`envelope_risi_action_items_voice_note.json`, `envelope_risi_action_items_unclear.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "action_items", "summary_id": uuid (the §27 extraction id),
  "conversation_id": "grp:…", "source": "call" | "voice_note", "source_ref": SourceRef (the whole source),
  "status": "ok" | "unclear", "reason": null | "low_confidence" | "script_check" | "no_speech",
  "summary": str (≤ 280) | null, "lang": "si" | "ta" | "en",
  "items": [ActionItem], "call_ref", "made_by", "notify": []}
ActionItem = {"item_id": uuid, "kind": "promise" | "task" | "meeting" | "decision" | "question" | "number",
  "text": str (1–200), "owner": uuid | null, "counterpart": [uuid], "due": ts | null, "all_day": bool,
  "due_text": str | null, "lang": "si" | "ta" | "en",
  "state": null | "proposed" | "confirmed" | "edited" | "declined" | "done" | "cancelled" | "expired",
  "source_ref": SourceRef (the line)}
SourceRef = {"type": "call" | "voice_note", "call_id": uuid | null, "session_id": uuid | null,
  "message_id": timeuuid | null, "turn": int | null, "offset_ms": int | null, "at": ts}
```
- **Kinds:** `promise` (someone will do something), `task` (something to be done, owner maybe unknown),
  `meeting` (an agreed time together), `decision`, `question` (left open), `number` (a figure stated: price,
  quantity, date). `promise`/`task`/`meeting` with an `owner` are **ledger items** (`risi_commitments`,
  `commitment_id = item_id`, §27.9) and carry `state`; the others have `owner: null` and `state: null`.
- **`owner`:** the speaker for first-person items; a named member resolved among the conversation's members;
  else `null` (no confirm card; shown as "Unassigned").
- **`source_ref` per item:** call → `call_id`, `session_id`, `turn` (the §36.5 `call_transcript` turn) and
  `offset_ms` within it; voice note → `message_id` and `offset_ms` within the note. `at` = the line's time.
  The card-level `source_ref` names the whole source (`turn`/`offset_ms` null).
- **Text language:** each item is written in the language spoken (`lang`); `summary` in the majority language
  (§36.7 rule).
- **`body`:** "Risi found 4 items in this call: 1 promise, 1 meeting, 1 decision, 1 number. Owners confirm
  theirs in their Risi chat." (voice note: "…in Harsha's voice note…"). Silent.
- **Rendering:** grouped by kind, each with owner ("You" / name / "Unassigned"), due and live state
  (from `item_update`, §27.5). For a voice note the card is attached under the note (like §37.6). **Tap an
  item → jump to its line:** call → the `call_transcript` post with that `session_id` + `turn` (highlighted);
  voice note → the note, playback seeked to `offset_ms`, transcript expanded. "Line no longer available" when
  missing.

### 38.4 The owner's card: `action_confirm` (in the owner's Risi chat)
One per owner per extraction, only when that owner has items (`envelope_risi_action_confirm.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "action_confirm", "summary_id", "chat_id", "conversation_id": "grp:…",
  "source", "source_ref", "for": [owner], "lang", "items": [ActionItem + {"buttons": [str]}],
  "expires_at": ts (posted + 48 h), "call_ref", "made_by", "notify": [owner]}
```
- `items`: only that owner's ledger items, `state: "proposed"`. `buttons` per item, from
  `["add_promise", "add_calendar", "remind", "not_mine", "edit"]`: `add_calendar` only for a `meeting` or an
  item with a non-all-day future `due`; `remind` only when the Reminders skill is `ask`/`allowed` (§26.3).
- **Actions** (`risi_action` in the owner's Risi chat, `target` = `item_id`; only the owner; repeats
  idempotent; after `expires_at` → ignored and the item `expired`, §27.5):

  | Button | `action` | `edit` | Effect |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Add to My promises | `item_confirm` (§27.5) | `null` | `confirmed` |
  | Edit | `item_edit` (§27.5, §28.6 partial) | `{"text"?, "due"?, "all_day"?}` | `edited` |
  | Add to calendar | **`item_calendar`** | `null` or `{"title"?, "start"?, "end"?, "all_day"?}` (§28.4 shapes) | confirms the item, then creates the Risi Calendar event at once as §29.8 [Add] (default 60 min from `due`), posts `event_card` `mode: "added"`; with no usable time the phone opens the picker first and sends `edit` (`envelope_risi_action_item_calendar.json`) |
  | Remind me | **`item_remind`** | `null` (default `due − 1 h`, §24.11 timing) or `{"remind_at": ts}` (future) | confirms the item and sets a §27.6 reminder (`envelope_risi_action_item_remind.json`) |
  | Not mine | **`item_not_mine`** | `null` | `declined`; the item is offered to nobody else; recorded as owner feedback (`envelope_risi_action_item_not_mine.json`) |

  The tap is the owner's explicit consent, so `item_calendar` and `item_remind` are not asked again under
  §26.3 "Ask me each time"; under "Allowed" nothing runs without the tap either (§28.5: proactive = asked).
- After each accepted action Risi posts the §27.5 **`item_update`** (`summary_id`, `item_id`, `state`, …) in
  the Risi chat of every participant of the source **and** applies the state to the Official card: phones
  update the `action_items` item with the same `item_id` from any `item_update` they hold.
- **No `risi_ledger` device:** that owner's items stay on the Official card only, `proposed`, and expire.

### 38.5 My promises: "From calls & voice notes"
- `GET /api/v1/risi/commitments` items gain **`"kind"`** (the `ActionItem.kind`, or `"promise"` for older
  items) and **`"source_ref": SourceRef | null`**; `source` may now be **`"voice_note"`**
  (`risi_commitments_reply_v142.json`).
- New query parameter **`source=call,voice_note`** (comma list of `chat` | `call` | `voice_note`; default all)
  filters by `source`. The app's filter chip **"From calls & voice notes"** uses it; a tap on such an item
  jumps to the line (§38.3) instead of `source_message_id`.

### 38.6 Opt-out, Stop, retention, deletes
- **Speaker opt-out:** a user whose `action_items` setting (§37.7) is `false`, or who opted out of memory
  (§35.3), is **not extracted from**: their lines and notes are removed from the extraction input (other
  speakers' items may still name them as `counterpart`, never as `owner`).
- **Stop:** a §36 `stop` with `discard` → no extraction (as §36.7). A `transcript_delete` (§37.6) or a §15
  delete of the note → its items are `cancelled` and removed from the cards (`item_update`).
- **Retention (NEXT-PHASE §B):** raw transcript text only in `risi_buffer`, sealed, **≤ 24 h**, deleted after
  extraction. Derived items are sealed with `RISI_DATA_KEY`: ledger items follow §27.5 (unconfirmed expire at
  48 h; confirmed live until done/cancelled); `decision`/`question`/`number` items are kept **30 days**
  (longer only if pinned, later), viewable and deletable by any participant via §35.3. Official off deletes
  everything of that chat within 1 h (§24.4).

### 38.7 Learning log (§24.12)
- **`extract_actions`** row per extraction: `source` type and ids, model, latency, cost, `lang`, input sizes
  (turns, audio seconds), output **counts per kind** and per owner — no item text.
- **`action_feedback`** row per owner action: `item_id`, `kind`, `action` (`confirm` | `edit` | `calendar` |
  `remind` | `not_mine` | `expired`), which fields an edit changed (`text` / `due`), seconds from posting.
  This is the training signal for the extraction (own L1/L2 cascade). Never text, never audio.

### 38.8 Amendments, examples, gate
- **§24.11:** `risi_action` gains `item_calendar`, `item_remind`, `item_not_mine`. **§27.3/§27.4:** replaced
  for in-scope sources while `risi_action_items` is `on` (§38.1). **§28.7:** `kind`, `source_ref`,
  `source=` filter. **§36.7:** output via §38.
- **Examples:** `auth_config_v142.json`, `envelope_risi_action_items.json`,
  `envelope_risi_action_items_voice_note.json`, `envelope_risi_action_items_unclear.json`,
  `envelope_risi_action_confirm.json`, `envelope_risi_action_item_calendar.json`,
  `envelope_risi_action_item_remind.json`, `envelope_risi_action_item_not_mine.json`,
  `risi_commitments_reply_v142.json`.
- **Gate:** server: the quality gate (unclear → no items), opt-out speakers excluded, owner-only actions,
  `item_calendar` creates exactly one event, retention TTLs, no text in the learning log; android: the source
  jump for both types, the card updates from `item_update`, the filter chip. Server-only changes ship via the
  Risi fast lane (rule 12); the app parts with the next APK.

### 38.9 Ask, never guess (v1.43)
Harsha 2026-10-13: "never guess names, amounts or dates; ask instead"; "confirmed items go to My promises and
the calendar with reminders"; "on a failed language or script check, say 'I couldn't hear this clearly' and
create NO items". From `contract/proposals/2026-10-13-action-items-gaps.md`. Server parts only while
`risi_action_items` is `on` (§38.1). **Additive:** one `risi_action` value (`item_claim`), one `ActionItem`
field (`claim`), one `action_confirm` field (`reminders_skill`), `owner` on `item_update`; v1.42 apps ignore
the fields, never send `item_claim`, and keep working as before.

**1. Unowned items: Risi asks who** (amends §38.3 "`owner`" and §38.4)
- On the Official card a `promise`/`task`/`meeting` whose owner can't be resolved keeps `owner: null`,
  `state: null` and gains **`"claim": true`** (absent = false; only on such items;
  `envelope_risi_action_items_v143.json`). The card's `body` asks: one such item → ` Who's taking "Book the
  van"? Tap "It's mine" on the card.`; more → ` 2 items have no owner yet: tap "It's mine" on yours.`
- **`item_claim`** (`risi_action` in the **claimer's own Risi chat**, `target` = `item_id`, `edit: null`;
  `envelope_risi_action_item_claim.json`). Accepted from any active human member of the Official conversation
  with a `risi_ledger` device, while the item is unclaimed and before the extraction + 48 h (the card's post
  + 48 h). **The first claim wins**; later claims and repeats are ignored.
- **Effect:** the item becomes the claimer's ledger item in state **`proposed`** (expiring with the
  extraction, §27.5); the claimer receives an `action_confirm` (§38.4) with that one item; and an
  `item_update` `{"state": "proposed", "owner": claimer, "by": claimer}` goes to every member (point 4).
  **Nothing is tracked until the claimer confirms** (§27.5). The claimer's [Not mine] → `declined`, as for
  any owner (offered to nobody else). Learning log: `action_feedback` `action: "claim"` (§38.7).
- **Phones:** under an item with `claim: true` whose live owner (the newest `item_update` `owner`) is still
  null, a button **[It's mine]**, hidden after the card's `server_ts` + 48 h; the item then shows its owner.

**2. Undated items: Risi asks when** (amends §38.4, reuses §28.6)
- When an action item is confirmed (`item_confirm`, `item_remind`) while it has no `due` or a vague
  `due_text` (§28.6 `needs_clarification`), Risi posts the §28.6 **`item_clarify`** to the owner (once per
  item, whatever the Skills switch). [New date] sends `item_edit` with `due`; the §27.6 reminders start
  from it. No date is ever guessed.
- `item_remind` with no `due` and no `remind_at`: the item is confirmed, no reminder is set, and
  `item_clarify` asks for the date.
- `item_calendar` with no usable time and no `edit`: nothing is created and `item_clarify` asks for the date
  (the phone normally opens the picker first and sends `edit`).

**3. Calendar for all-day and undated items** (amends §38.4 "`add_calendar` only for …")
- `add_calendar` is offered for a `meeting`, for an item with **no** `due` (the phone asks with its picker),
  and for an item whose `due` is not in the past, **timed or all-day**.
- `item_calendar` on an **all-day** `due` without `edit` creates an **all-day** Risi Calendar event on that
  date (00:00 to 00:00 the next day in the owner's zone, §29.2). The phone shows "Add "…" to your Risi
  Calendar on Fri 16 Oct (all day)?" and may send `edit` `{"start", "end", "all_day": true}` (§28.4 shapes).

**4. Every member's Official card follows the items** (amends §38.4 "every participant of the source")
- For §38 items the `item_update` goes to the Risi chat of **every active human member of the Official
  conversation** with a `risi_ledger` device, not only the extraction's recipients.
- **Members who are not recipients of the extraction get the state only:** `text`, `due` and `all_day` are
  **`null`** (= unchanged: their card keeps what it showed; an owner's edit never leaks), and `body` names no
  item ("Shenika confirmed an item from the call."). Recipients get the §27.5 `item_update` unchanged.
- `item_update` gains **`"owner": uuid | null`** (the item's owner after the action;
  `envelope_risi_item_update_v143.json`). Phones apply `state` and `owner` to the `action_items` item with the
  same `item_id`, and show **no bubble** for an `item_update` whose `action_items` card is on the phone.

**5. Remind me is always offered** (amends §38.4 "`remind` only when …")
- `remind` is in the buttons of every `action_confirm` item. It sets the **§27.6 reminder in the owner's
  Risi chat** (a Risi message with a push), which needs no skill; it **never** changes the §26 Reminders
  skill and never sets a phone alarm.
- `action_confirm` gains **`"reminders_skill": "off" | "ask" | "allowed"`** (the owner's §26.2 state when
  posted; absent = unknown; `envelope_risi_action_confirm_v143.json`). When `"off"`, the phone says under the
  buttons: "Reminders come here in your Risi chat. Phone reminders (the Reminders skill) stay off." Nothing
  is turned on silently.

**6. Wording: "I couldn't hear this clearly"** (amends §38.2, §38.3)
- The `status: "unclear"` card's `body` is, for every `reason`, **"I couldn't hear this clearly."** in the
  source's language (`lang`): si **"මට මෙය පැහැදිලිව ඇසුණේ නැහැ."**, ta **"இதை என்னால் தெளிவாகக் கேட்க
  முடியவில்லை."** (`envelope_risi_action_items_unclear_v143.json`). Phones show the same text by the card's
  `lang` (English for any other). Still no items, no model call.

**Examples (v1.43):** `envelope_risi_action_items_v143.json`, `envelope_risi_action_item_claim.json`,
`envelope_risi_action_confirm_v143.json`, `envelope_risi_item_update_v143.json`,
`envelope_risi_action_items_unclear_v143.json`.
**Gate:** server (`ExamplesV143Test`, action items tests): claim (first wins, members only, before expiry,
then `action_confirm` + `item_update` to every member), `item_clarify` after an undated confirm, an all-day
`item_calendar` → one all-day event, the state-only `item_update` for non-recipients, `remind` always offered
with `reminders_skill`, the unclear wording; android: [It's mine], the live owner, the all-day calendar slot,
the reminders note, the unclear text in en/si/ta, `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 1.43; device: `scripts/voice-actions-test`.

## 39. Link previews (v1.44)
Harsha PART 11 (WhatsApp-standard messaging), 2026-10-13. **Additive:** one optional field, `link_preview`, on the
`text` envelope (§10.3, §33). No capability, no server logic, no REST, no new event: old apps ignore the field and
show the text with its link. **No agent: infrastructure** (decision 085): a deterministic client feature, no
decisions to learn.

### 39.0 Principles
- **The sender's phone fetches the preview** and sends it **inside the E2EE message**. The server never fetches a
  URL and never sees one: the link and its preview exist only inside the MLS ciphertext.
- **Receivers render the card from the message alone.** A receiver never fetches the URL, its page or its image.
- **E2EE only.** A conversation that isn't end-to-end encrypted (a plaintext `msg:send`, decision 048) never carries
  a preview, and its sender fetches nothing.
- **The user's switch.** Settings → Chats → **"Link previews"** (default **on**). Off: no fetch, nothing sent. The
  subtitle says what it does: "Your phone loads a preview of links you send. The website sees your phone's
  address." It is device-local, never sent to the server.

### 39.1 The field (`envelope_text_link_preview.json`)
```json
{"v": 1, "type": "text", "body": "Agenda for Friday: https://example.org/agenda",
 "link_preview": {"url": "https://example.org/agenda", "title": "Friday agenda",
                  "description": "Site visit at 10, review at 3.", "site_name": "Example",
                  "thumb": {"mime": "image/jpeg", "w": 128, "h": 67, "data": "<b64, ≤ 4096 bytes decoded>"}}}
```
- **`url`** (required): an absolute `https://` URL of at most 2048 characters that occurs **verbatim in `body`**.
- **`title`** (required): 1–200 characters after trimming, not whitespace-only.
- **`description`** (optional, omitted when empty): at most 300 characters.
- **`site_name`** (optional, omitted when empty): at most 100 characters.
- **`thumb`** (optional, or `null`, `envelope_text_link_preview_no_thumb.json`): **exactly the §14.4 thumbnail
  rules**: JPEG or WebP, `1 ≤ w, h ≤ 128`, `data` ≤ 4096 bytes decoded.
- Senders cut longer `title` / `description` / `site_name` at a grapheme boundary (with "…") before sending.
- `link_preview` may sit next to `reply_to` and `forwarded` (§33). It is defined on `text` only in v1.44 (captions
  of `image`/`file` carry none).
- **Envelope bound:** the §14.4 22 KiB bound applies. Over it, the sender first sets `thumb` to `null`, then drops
  `link_preview`.
- **Validation (receiver):** a `link_preview` that breaks any rule above (not an object, a non-https `url`, a `url`
  not in `body`, a missing or blank `title`, an over-long field, a bad `thumb`) is **ignored**, and the message is
  shown without a card, never dropped (`envelope_text_link_preview_bad.json`: an `http://` URL that is not in the
  body).

### 39.2 Why an inline thumbnail, not a blob
The preview image travels as the inline §14.4 `thumb`, **not** as a §14 `media` blob:
- the receiver fetches nothing at all (no blob download, so no read-timing or "this message has a link" signal at
  the server, and nothing to fail later);
- no upload, quota, rate limit or 30-day TTL (§14.5): the card survives in backups and history shares (§17, §22)
  with the message, and a forward never needs a fresh per-conversation blob (§33.5);
- a link card needs only a small image; 128 px at ≤ 4 KiB fits the 22 KiB envelope next to a long body.
A larger "hero" image would need a blob; it is out of scope for v1.44.

### 39.3 The sender's fetch (android, normative)
Only when the switch is on and the conversation is E2EE (an MLS group exists), for the **first** `https://` URL in
the body:
- **Request:** `GET`, no cookies, no cache, no `Authorization`, no `Referer`, `User-Agent: RisiMe-LinkPreview/1`,
  `Accept: text/html`. **`https` only**, on every hop; at most **3 redirects**.
- **No private addresses:** the host is resolved and the fetch is refused when **any** resolved address is
  loopback, link-local, site-local/private (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, fc00::/7), CGNAT (100.64/10),
  unspecified, multicast or broadcast; an IP-literal host follows the same rule. It applies to every redirect.
- **Limits:** connect 3 s, the whole fetch (page and image) at most **5 s**; the page must be `text/html`, and only
  its first **512 KiB** are read; the image (`og:image`, absolute https after resolving against the page) at most
  **2 MiB**, decoded, scaled to a longest side of 128 px and re-encoded (JPEG, starting at q60 and stepping down as
  §14.4) so no metadata travels.
- **Fields:** `og:title` (else `<title>`), `og:description` (else `meta name=description`), `og:site_name`, and
  `og:image`; HTML entities decoded, whitespace collapsed.
- **Failure is silent:** any error, refusal or timeout sends the message without `link_preview`. The message waits
  for its preview at most 5 s; it is shown at once as pending meanwhile.
- **Debug builds only:** a test seam may route one named test host to a `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` origin (the
  device gate's local Open Graph page). Release builds contain no such route.

### 39.4 Receiver (android, normative)
- **Links in text are tappable:** `http(s)://…` and `www.…` in a message body open in the browser (the system
  `ACTION_VIEW`), in every message type with text. The app fetches nothing for a link it receives.
- **The card** (from `link_preview` only): the thumbnail (if any), `site_name`, `title` (2 lines), `description`
  (2 lines), above the text inside the bubble. Tapping it opens `url`.
- Room: `link_preview TEXT NULL` on `messages` (the validated object as JSON); a nullable column, so the upgrade
  keeps every message (rule 9, decision 055).

### 39.5 Copy, forward, backups, Risi, the server
- **Copy and Share** copy the body only (§33.3). **Forward** drops `link_preview` (§33.4: a forward is a new
  message; it carries no preview in v1.44).
- **Backups and history shares** keep `link_preview` as part of the `text` payload (§17.6, §22).
- **Risi** (Official): Risi decrypts Official messages as a visible member (§24.11) and ignores `link_preview`; it
  never fetches a URL from a message. No learning-log record (§24.12): there is no model call.
- **The server** stores ciphertext only and logs no message content (§10). Nothing changes server-side.

### 39.6 Amendments, examples, gate
- **§10.3 / §33.9:** `text` gains the optional `link_preview`. **§33.3 / §33.4:** not copied, not forwarded.
- **Examples:** `envelope_text_link_preview.json`, `envelope_text_link_preview_no_thumb.json` (with `reply_to`),
  `envelope_text_link_preview_bad.json` (ignored, the message is still shown).
- **Gate:** server: `ExamplesV144Test` parses the examples (no logic). android: the validator (every rule above),
  the address filter, the Open Graph parser, the switch, the Room migration 18 → 19 keeping every row; the device
  gate `scripts/messaging-polish-test` (two redroid phones): swipe-to-reply in 1:1 and a group, a link with a card
  on both phones (a local OG page through the debug seam), no card with the switch off, and no URL text in the
  server's log or database.

## 40. Adding people to a call (v1.45)
Decision 086 (Harsha PART 10, 2026-10-13). Extends §16, §19, §20, §23 and §36 and overrides them where they
differ. **Additive:** one device capability `add_to_call`; the `features` value `add` (§23.1); one ephemeral
envelope `call_escalate`; the `call_end` reason `moved` with `moved_to`; `ring_users` on the `mode: "sfu"`
`call_offer` and the re-ring of a running group call; `Friend.group_calls`; relaxed group ring limits; §36.9
activated (Official group calls, the Session state `paused`). No MLS core change, no new exporter label, no
server call table, no new store.

### 40.0 Principles
- **More than two people is always a §20 call on a `grp:`.** A `dm:` group can't hold a third member
  (§10.2, §24.1), so adding people to a 1:1 call **creates a new group chat** of everyone in the call (the
  adder, the peer, the invitees) with the normal §12.3 create (and §24.2 Official) flow, and **moves the
  call into it**. Frame keys come from that group's MLS exporter exactly as §20.6; rooms, tokens, caps,
  removal and the roster rule (K7) are §20/§23 unchanged.
- **The tab follows the call.** A Private 1:1 call moves into the new group's **Private** conversation; a
  call placed from the 1:1's **Official** tab (`call_offer.tab`, §36.1) into its **Official** conversation.
  If Official isn't `active` within 15 s of the group, the call moves into Private and both screens say
  "Continuing as a Private call" (never toward Risi hearing, §24.0).
- **Nobody is dropped.** Make before break: the room comes up, both devices connect and install the
  group's keys while the peer-to-peer call carries the audio; each side switches when it sees the other in
  the room (§40.3). If the room doesn't come up within 30 s, nothing changes for the two.
- **Old peers are never asked** (per-call `features`, §40.1); old invitees can't be picked; old sibling
  devices ring briefly and stop on `call_member` `joined` (§20.3).
- No model calls for the call itself (decision 086: infrastructure). Official group calls may be
  transcribed only under §40.9, with everyone's consent.

### 40.1 Capability, features, readiness
- A v1.45 app advertises **`"add_to_call"`** in `mls.capabilities` (`device_put_add_to_call.json`) together
  with `group_calls` and `call_switch`, once it implements §40.2–§40.8. The server keeps it (it joins
  `@known_capabilities`); it is information only (no readiness field).
- **Per call, inside MLS:** `call_offer` (first offer) and `call_answer` list **`"add"`** in `features`
  (§23.1) when the sending device advertises `add_to_call` (`call_offer_features_add_payload.json`,
  `call_answer_features_add_payload.json`).
- **"Add participant"** in a 1:1 call is enabled only while the call is connected (`ACTIVE`), both
  selected devices listed `add`, and this device advertises `add_to_call`. Otherwise it stays visible and
  disabled with why: "<name> needs to update RisiMe to add people" (no `add` from the peer), "Update
  RisiMe on this phone to add people", "Add people once the call has connected".
- **`Friend` (§9.2) gains `"group_calls": bool`** (`friends_reply_v145.json`; absent = unknown, treated
  as true): the friend has a `group_calls` device that can still receive (§12.1). The picker greys out
  friends with `group_ready: false` ("needs to update") or `group_calls: false` ("can't join calls yet").

### 40.2 The picker (client)
- Lists my friends except the peer, searchable, multi-select. **Caps:** with the people already in the
  call, at most **32** in a voice call and **8** in a video call (§20.9; the picker says "Up to 8 people
  in a video call").
- Official call: the subtitle says "Risi will be in this group chat (Official)"; Private: "A new group
  chat with everyone in the call".
- **[Add]** confirms: "Add Sunil to this call? A group chat with Nimal, Sunil and you is created; Sunil
  hears the call from when they join." Nothing is created before this tap.

### 40.3 Moving a 1:1 call into a group (normative)
**The adder (A), with the peer's selected device B:**
1. **Create the group:** `POST /groups` with `member_ids` = the peer plus the invitees (§12.3), the
   epoch-0 commit; name = the members' first names as A knows them, sorted, A last ("Nimal, Sunil and
   Harsha"), cut to 100 grapheme clusters; for an Official call also `POST /chats/{chat_id}/official`
   (§24.2) and wait ≤ 15 s for it to be `active`. The **call conversation** is the Official `grp:` if it
   is active, else the Private one. Errors (`not_friends`, `not_ready`, `too_many_*`, offline) end the add
   with "Couldn't add people" and nothing else changes.
2. **Start the room:** a new `room_call_id` (UUIDv4); `POST /calls/rooms` `start` with the call
   conversation, `room_call_id` and **`media`** = `"video"` if the 1:1 call is in video mode (§23.2),
   else `"audio"`; connect (§20.5) and install the keys (§20.6) — **in hold**: nothing is published.
3. **Tell the peer:** `call_escalate` `state: "move"` (§40.5) to B over the 1:1 path (`call:signal`,
   `to` = the peer, `ring: false`, the 1:1 call's start `media`).
4. **Ring the invitees:** the durable `group_call` `started` (§20.4, with the room's `media`), then the
   `call_offer` `mode: "sfu"` with **`ring_users`** = the invitees (never the peer) (§40.4).
5. **Switch** when B's identity (`<peer user>/<B device>`) is present in the room and is a leaf of the
   call conversation at the current epoch: stop the peer-to-peer media (the `RTCPeerConnection` closes;
   nothing more is sent on it), then publish the microphone (and the camera if it was on, §19.5), and
   send the durable **`call_end`** with **`reason: "moved"`** (§40.5) in the `dm:`. The call screen
   becomes the group call screen; the platform call and audio route stay (§40.7).
6. **Give up** if B isn't in the room **30 s** after step 3, or on B's `call_escalate` `failed`: leave the
   room, send `call_cancel` `ended` and `group_call` `ended` `timeout` in the call conversation (invitees
   stop ringing), stay on the 1:1 call and show "Couldn't add people. You're still in the call with
   Nimal." The new group chat stays (it was created; nothing is deleted).

**The peer (B), on `call_escalate` `move`** from the selected peer device of its connected call:
1. Validate (§40.5) and show "Kamal is adding Sunil…" (the invitees: the call conversation's members
   other than the two of us, from MLS).
2. Catch up groups until the call conversation's MLS state is local and its leaves include the sender
   device and this device (≤ 20 s; a Welcome arrives with the group's `created`, §12.7). Then
   `POST /calls/rooms` `join` with `room_call_id` and the envelope's `media`, connect, keys, **hold**,
   send `call_member` `joined` (§20.3: B's other devices stop ringing).
3. **Switch** when the sender's identity is present and a leaf (as step 5 above): stop the peer-to-peer
   media and publish. B sends **no** `call_end` (A's `moved` is the line).
4. **Fail** (no group state in 20 s, `call_ended`/`call_full`/an error from `join`, keys failing, busy):
   `call_escalate` `state: "failed"` with `reason`, leave the room if joined, stay on the 1:1 call.
- While a move is pending (from step 3 for A, from receipt for B, until the switch or the give-up), the
  1:1 machine **ignores** peer-to-peer ICE loss and restarts caused by the move for 30 s (no `failed`,
  no restart storm), and a hang-up on either side ends both the 1:1 call (`call_end` `hangup`, as
  before) and the room (leave; A also cancels the ring).
- **Invitees (C)** ring as for any §20 group call ("Kamal · Nimal, Sunil and Kamal", Answer / Decline);
  answering is §20.4 step 2.
- A move happens at most once per 1:1 call; the button is gone afterwards (the group call has its own
  Add, §40.4).

### 40.4 `ring_users`, late adds and re-rings (groups)
- **`call_offer` `mode: "sfu"`** gains optional **`"ring_users": [uuid]`** (1–31 user ids, lowercase;
  `call_offer_sfu_ring_users_payload.json`). When present, **only devices of those users ring**; other
  members' devices note the call (the chat line with Join) and don't ring. Absent = every other member
  (v1.19). The sender's own user is never rung.
- **Re-ring:** a participant of a running group call may send the offer again for the **same `call_id`**
  with a fresh `sent_at` and `ring_users` = the people to ring. A receiver rings again for a `call_id` it
  has seen only when `ring_users` names its user, the offer passes §20.3 freshness, its `sent_at` is
  later than the last offer of that `call_id` this device acted on, and the device isn't in that call.
  Declining a re-ring is `call_member` `declined` as before.
- **"Add participant" in a group call** (any §20 call, including a moved one): the picker shows
  (a) members of the call conversation who aren't in the room → **Ring** (a re-ring); (b) for an
  **admin** of the chat, friends who aren't members → **Add to group and ring**: a confirmation ("Add
  Sunil to Pilot team? They'll see new messages in this group."), the §12.3 add (§24.3 for both tabs),
  then, once the add commit has landed (the call rekeys, K3), a re-ring naming them. Non-admins see "Only
  admins can add people to this group".
- **Server limits** (amends §20.3 S5): `ring: true` at most **1 per user per group per 10 s** and **20 per
  user per hour** (was 30 s and 10); `ring: false` unchanged.

### 40.5 Envelopes
**`call_escalate`** (ephemeral: `call:signal`, `to` = the peer, `ring: false`, the 1:1 call's start
`media`, the cleartext `call_id` = the 1:1 call's; sender pinning of §16.3: only from the selected peer
device of that call):
```json
{"v": 1, "type": "call_escalate", "call_id": "<1:1 call_id>", "to_device": "<peer device_id>",
 "state": "move", "conversation_id": "grp:…", "room_call_id": "<uuid>", "media": "audio"}
{"v": 1, "type": "call_escalate", "call_id": "<1:1 call_id>", "to_device": "<peer device_id>",
 "state": "failed", "reason": "no_group"}
```
(`call_escalate_move_payload.json`, `call_escalate_failed_payload.json`.)
- `state` `move` (adder → peer) carries `conversation_id` (a `grp:` id), `room_call_id` (a lowercase
  UUIDv4, never equal to `call_id`) and `media` (`audio` | `video`); `failed` (peer → adder) carries
  `reason`: `no_group` | `room` | `keys` | `busy` | `cancelled`.
- Accepted only for a connected call (`ACTIVE`/`RECONNECTING`) whose peer listed `add`, at most one `move`
  per call (a repeat with the same `room_call_id` is ignored, another one dropped).
- Strict validation: the UUIDs, `to_device` = this device, `state` one of two, the fields of that state
  present and valid; else drop and log. ≤ v1.43 apps never receive it (they didn't list `add`) and would
  ignore it (§16.2 unknown type).

**`call_end` `reason: "moved"`** (durable, the adder only; `call_end_moved_payload.json`):
```json
{"v": 1, "type": "call_end", "call_id": "…", "media": "audio", "reason": "moved",
 "connected_at": "…", "duration_s": 130, "moved_to": {"conversation_id": "grp:…", "call_id": "…"}}
```
`connected_at`/`duration_s` cover the 1:1 part. `moved_to` is optional (display only: the line links to
the group chat). §16.2 renders an unknown reason as "Voice call", so older devices of either user show
"Voice call · 2:10".

### 40.6 History lines (client)
- **The 1:1 chat:** "Voice call · 2:10 · continued as a group call" (or "Video call …"); tapping opens the
  `moved_to` chat when this device has it. Same direction rules as §16.6.
- **The group chat:** the §20.4 lines of the room's call: "Kamal started a voice call" with Join while it
  runs, then "Voice call · 12:03"; an invitee who never answered sees "Missed voice call" (and gets the
  §16.6 missed-call notification once, at the end of the ring); one who declined sees "Voice call ·
  12:03" like every member. The Calls tab lists both lines.

### 40.7 Group voice and video behaviour (android, normative where it says "must")
- **One platform call.** The Telecom call of the 1:1 call **must** carry on for the group call (no
  disconnect and re-add): its audio route (earpiece, speaker, wired, Bluetooth) and the user's pick stay.
  The ongoing notification and the call bar switch to the group's name.
- **Join and leave:** Leave = disconnect (§20.4). **The call goes on when the starter leaves:** the room
  lives while anyone is in it; the last one out sends `group_call` `ended` (§20.4 step 4).
- **Late join:** Join on the running line (§20.4) at any time while the room exists, also after a decline
  or a missed ring, and after this device left (rejoin).
- **Reconnect:** LiveKit's reconnect (ICE restart, resume, full reconnect) for up to **20 s**
  ("Reconnecting…"); then "Lost connection", and Join on the line while the room exists. A network change
  (Wi-Fi ↔ mobile) is a reconnect, not a leave.
- **Mute** shows on every tile (the participant's microphone mute state); the **active speaker**
  (LiveKit's speaker list, only for MLS members whose frames decrypt) is highlighted and, in video, takes
  the large tile.
- **Video:** a grid of up to 8 tiles (1 large + 7 small with adaptive stream, §20.5), my camera preview,
  **Flip** (front/back), camera on/off. Screen sharing in groups follows §23.5/§23.6 when the device
  advertises `screen_share`.
- **Busy:** a device in a group call doesn't ring for a 1:1 call and vice versa (§20.4); the move itself
  is the one allowed overlap of the two.

### 40.8 Rollout and old apps
- **Order:** server (capability, `Friend.group_calls`, limits, §40.9) → a nightly with the android flag
  `ADD_TO_CALL_ENABLED` off (receives `ring_users`, `moved` lines, re-rings) → the flag on (advertises
  `add_to_call`, sends `features` `add`, shows the button) once `scripts/group-call-test` is green.
- **v1.19–v1.43 devices:** never get `call_escalate`; a group ring with `ring_users` rings them anyway
  (they ignore the field) until a sibling's `call_member` `joined`/`declined` or the ring's end; `call_end`
  `moved` reads "Voice call · m:ss".
- **A server before v1.45:** `Friend.group_calls` absent (treated as true; an invitee who can't join
  simply never answers), a re-ring within 30 s gets `rate_limited` ("Try again in a moment").

### 40.9 Official group calls: transcription (activates §36.9; reuses §36, §38)
Everything in §36 holds for a §20 call on an **Official** `grp:` with Official `on` (a group's Official
call, or a 1:1 Official call moved there), with these differences (server and android, normative):
- **The call record** (server): a group `call:signal` `ring: true` records `{conv, starter, media}` for its
  `call_id` (4 h, §36.2; the first ring wins). `call:transcribe` names that conversation in
  `conversation_id` (it must equal the record's); `unknown_call` without a record; `not_official` unless
  it is Official with Official on.
- **Participants are the human users in the LiveKit room** (`ListParticipants` of the room named by
  §20.2 from `conv`, `call_id` and the start `media`; agent identities excluded), read at every push and
  on the 5-s tick. A user who isn't in the room and isn't already a participant can't request or allow
  (`not_participant`).
- **A joiner** (a user who appears in the room after the request) is added as `pending`. If the session
  is `requested`, it waits for them too; if it is `on`, it turns **`paused`**: uploads get `409
  not_consented`, the phones show "Paused until Sunil allows" and the joiner gets the §36.6 prompt. The
  joiner's `allow` (the last pending) → `on`; their `decline` → `declined` (stops for everyone); 30 s
  without an answer → `stopped` with `reason: "expired"`. A participant who leaves the room keeps their
  consent and stops uploading.
- **Session** gains `"paused"` in `state` (`signal_call_transcribe_paused.json`); `paused` is not final.
  Transitions added: `on ──joiner──▶ paused`, `paused ──allow by the last pending──▶ on`, `paused
  ──decline──▶ declined`, `paused ──30 s──▶ stopped (expired)`, `paused ──stop──▶ stopped`. `left` (§36.2)
  applies when **no** participant has uploaded for 90 s; a session also ends (`ended`) when the room is
  gone.
- **Hang-up:** a participant leaving a group call sends `stop` `ended` **only** when nobody else is left
  in the room; otherwise nothing (the others carry on).
- `peer_not_ready`: some other participant in the room has no live `call_transcribe` device.
- **The §27.7 listener and this path never run in the same call** (`busy`).
- **After the call:** §36.7 unchanged (≥ 30 s of speech from ≥ 2 speakers): per-person summaries to each
  speaker's Risi chat, one card in Official, §38 action items with the §38.9 rules.
- **Phones:** [Transcribe] on the group call screen under §36.1 (Official, `on`, this device has
  `call_transcribe`); the red bar names who is pending while `paused`.

### 40.10 Limits on spark2 (measured 2026-10-13)
The §20.9 caps (32 voice, 8 video) stay the contract. Measured with `scripts/livekit-capacity` (a throwaway
LiveKit with the pilot's settings, loopback, synthetic participants; table in decision 086): the SFU needs
under 1.5 % of one core and 5–10 MiB per participant, so **spark2's 31 Mbit/s uplink is the bound**:
- **voice:** ≈ 0.12 Mbit/s per participant with several talkers: a 32-person call ≈ 4 Mbit/s; about 7 full
  voice calls (≈ 250 participants) at once;
- **video:** an 8-person room at worst-case layers took the whole uplink (32 Mbit/s); with the app's simulcast
  and adaptive stream ≈ 11 Mbit/s: about 2 full 8-person video rooms at once (≈ 20 video participants).

### 40.11 Examples, gate
- **Examples:** `device_put_add_to_call.json`, `call_offer_features_add_payload.json`,
  `call_answer_features_add_payload.json`, `call_escalate_move_payload.json`,
  `call_escalate_failed_payload.json`, `call_end_moved_payload.json`,
  `call_offer_sfu_ring_users_payload.json`, `friends_reply_v145.json`,
  `signal_call_transcribe_paused.json`.
- **Server:** `add_to_call` kept; `Friend.group_calls`; the new ring limits (a re-ring after 10 s passes,
  within 10 s `rate_limited`, the hour cap 20); `call_escalate` is an ordinary 1:1 `ring: false` signal;
  §40.9 (participants from the room, a joiner pauses, decline stops, the 30-s pause expiry, uploads
  refused while paused, the listener's `busy`); every new example.
- **Android (JVM):** encode/decode and the strict drops of every new example; the button gating; the
  move machine (both sides: hold, the switch on presence, give-up at 30 s, `failed`, hang-up during the
  move, ICE loss ignored while moving); `ring_users` and the re-ring rule; the history lines; one
  Telecom call through the move.
- **Device:** `scripts/group-call-test` (3 redroids, a test LiveKit): A calls B (voice), A adds C, C
  answers, every phone receives decrypted audio from both others, B leaves and A–C go on; then the same
  with video (frames decoded from every remote). `GCTEST OK`.

## 41. Call Risi: a voice call that Risi answers (v1.48)
Decision 090; proposal `contract/proposals/2026-10-14-call-risi.md` (applied). Behind flags: server
`RISI_CALL_RISI` = `off` | `canary` | `on` (default `off`; `canary` = `RISI_DEV_USERS`), Android developer flag
`call_risi` (`risi_dev` accounts only, off by default). With the flags off every call is byte-identical to v1.47.

**Additive only:** one device capability, one optional `User` field, one optional field on `POST /calls/rooms`
`start` with its reply object and four errors, one room-metadata key, one optional field on the durable
`group_call` envelope, one Risi kind, one optional field on the `answer` kind. Old apps never see a Risi call
and render `risi_call_transcript` as `body`.

**Harsha's decisions (2026-10-14):**
1. **v1 ships without frame E2EE**, with the honest wording of §41.4 on the call screen and `"e2ee": false` in
   the start reply. Frame encryption to Risi (`call_frame_keys` in Risi's MLS core, crypto C10) comes later.
2. **The Call Risi button is also on the Risi contact sheet** (the Friends list), with the same rules as the
   button in the Risi chat (§41.1, §41.3).
3. **Each Risi call is limited to 30 minutes by default, adjustable in the admin console** (setting
   `call_risi_max_s`, 60–3600 s, §41.6). The start reply's `max_s` carries the limit in force.

### 41.0 Principles
- **A call with Risi, not between humans.** It happens only in the caller's own **Risi chat** (§25.2, a `grp:`
  with one human and Risi). Risi is the other party, so **Risi hears and decrypts this call's audio**. It is
  never a Private or an Official call between people, and nothing in §16, §19, §20, §27.7 or §36 changes.
- **Honest label (decision 048):** v1 media is protected per hop (DTLS-SRTP to the SFU, loopback to Risi) but
  **not frame-encrypted end to end** (§20.6 needs Risi's server-side `call_frame_keys`, crypto C10, later).
  The call screen says so (§41.4) and the start reply carries `"e2ee": false`.
- **Visible agent:** the call screen shows from its first frame that Risi hears the call.
- **No raw audio stored**, anywhere. The transcript is a Risi post in the Risi chat (MLS, encrypted to the
  user's devices), like any Risi answer.

### 41.1 Capability, switch
- **Capability `"call_risi"`** in `mls.capabilities` (`device_put_call_risi.json`), only together with
  `group_calls` and `risi_tools`, once the app implements §41.2–§41.5. The server ignores `call_risi` from a
  device without both.
- **`User` (§1.3) gains `"call_risi": true`** (`me_reply_call_risi.json`) while the server switch
  `RISI_CALL_RISI` applies to that user (`on`, or `canary` and the user is in `RISI_DEV_USERS`); absent
  otherwise. The app shows **Call Risi** (in the Risi chat's top bar and on the Risi contact sheet) only when
  this is true, the device has `call_risi`, and (v1) the developer flag `call_risi` is on. The server re-checks
  on every `start`, so turning the switch `off` hides the button at the next `GET /me` and refuses new calls at
  once; running calls end within 10 s.

### 41.2 Starting: `POST /api/v1/calls/rooms` `start` with `risi_call`
Request (`calls_room_request_call_risi.json`):
```json
{"conversation_id": "grp:…(the caller's Risi chat)", "call_id": "<uuid-v4>", "media": "audio",
 "action": "start", "risi_call": true}
```
Checks, after §20.2's (in order): `RISI_CALL_RISI` for the caller (**`403 call_risi_off`**,
`error_call_risi_off.json`) → the conversation is the caller's active Risi chat (`chat_kind: "risi"`) and
`media` is `"audio"` (**`422 not_risi_chat`**, `error_not_risi_chat.json`) → the device advertises `call_risi`
(`403 invalid_device`) → fewer than `RISI_CALL_RISI_MAX` (default 2) Risi calls running (**`409 risi_busy`**,
`error_risi_busy.json`) → speech, TTS and the media worker healthy (**`503 risi_unavailable`**,
`error_risi_unavailable.json`). Rate limit: `start` as §20.2.

The server creates the room with `max_participants` **2**, `empty_timeout` 60 s, `departure_timeout` 20 s and
the metadata `{"c": conv, "m": "audio", "k": "risi"}`, starts Risi's voice session, and answers `200`
(`calls_room_reply_call_risi.json`): the §20.2 reply plus
```json
"risi_call": {"state": "joining", "e2ee": false, "risi_identity": "<risi_user_id>/<risi_device_id>",
              "max_s": 1800, "lang": "en"}
```
- `max_s` is this call's length limit in seconds: the admin setting `call_risi_max_s` at the time of `start`
  (default 1800, always 60–3600, §41.6). The server ends the call at `max_s` (transcript `reason: "max_length"`).
- `join` on a room with `"k": "risi"` answers `409 call_full` to anyone but the starter's identity
  (a refresh); `status` works as §20.2. `risi_call` on any other action is `400 bad_request`.
- Without `risi_call` (or `false`), `start` is exactly v1.47 (parity).
- **Risi's token** is minted inside the server (never through REST) with the §20.2 claims except
  `canPublish: true`, `canPublishSources: ["microphone"]`, `canSubscribe: true`, `canPublishData: false`
  (`livekit_token_claims_call_risi.json`). Risi subscribes only to the caller's microphone track.
  **v1.52:** `canPublishData: true` on both tokens of a `k: "risi"` room (§41.8).
- Risi joins within 10 s; otherwise the session ends and the phone leaves (§41.3). Risi leaves when the caller
  leaves, at `max_s`, when the switch turns off (reason `switch_off`), or on an error (reason `error`).

### 41.3 The phone (android, normative where it says "must")
- **Start:** the Call Risi button (the Risi chat's top bar and the Risi contact sheet, same rules) → TURN
  credentials → `start` with `risi_call: true` → connect (§20.5 options) **without `E2EEOptions`** for this room
  only (the reply said `"e2ee": false`) → publish the microphone with the WebRTC audio processing **on**: noise
  suppression, echo cancellation, auto gain control, high-pass (LiveKit defaults, which must not be turned
  off) → send the durable `group_call` `started` with **`"with": "risi"`**
  (`group_call_started_call_risi_payload.json`). **No `call_offer`, no ringing**, no `call_member`.
- **Fail closed:** the only remote participant the app plays is `risi_identity`. Any other identity → leave
  at once ("This call isn't safe any more").
- **Timeout:** Risi's identity not in the room 12 s after connect → leave, `group_call` `ended`
  `reason: "timeout"`, "Risi couldn't answer. Try again in a moment."
- **End:** hang up = disconnect, then `group_call` `ended` `reason: "hangup"` with `"with": "risi"`
  (`group_call_ended_call_risi_payload.json`). Risi leaving (e.g. at `max_s`) ends the call the same way.
  History line: "Call with Risi · 3:12" / "Call with Risi · Didn't connect".
- Telecom, foreground service, wake lock, audio route: as §20.5.

### 41.4 The call screen (android, normative)
From the first frame of the call screen until it closes:
- the title **"Risi"** with Risi's avatar and the agent badge, a state line "Connecting…" → "Listening" →
  "Thinking…" → "Speaking" (from the room's audio levels: Risi's track speaking = Speaking);
- a persistent banner **"Risi hears this call"** and below it **"Not end-to-end encrypted: audio is processed
  by Risi on RisiMe's server. Audio isn't kept."** (never a lock icon);
- mute, speaker, hang up. No add-people, no video, no screen share, no Transcribe button.
- The first Risi call shows a one-time sheet: "You're calling Risi. Risi hears what you say and answers. A
  transcript goes to your Risi chat. Audio isn't kept." [OK].

### 41.5 The transcript: Risi kind `risi_call_transcript` (in the Risi chat)
(Not `call_transcript`, which §36.5 already uses for a per-turn line.)
Posted by Risi (§24.11 envelope, `risi` object) once the call ends (`envelope_risi_call_risi_transcript.json`):
```json
{"kind": "risi_call_transcript", "call_id": "…", "started_at": "…", "ended_at": "…", "duration_s": 192,
 "reason": "hangup", "lines": [{"who": "user", "text": "What's on my calendar tomorrow?", "lang": "en",
 "at": "…"}, {"who": "risi", "text": "Hmm, let me check your calendar…", "lang": "en", "at": "…",
 "filler": true}, {"who": "risi", "text": "Tomorrow you have two meetings…", "lang": "en", "at": "…",
 "interrupted": false, "request_id": "…"}],
 "e2ee": false, "made_by": {…}, "call_ref": null, "notify": [user_id]}
```
- `who` is `"user"` or `"risi"`; `text` 1–2 000 chars; at most 400 lines (older lines dropped first, with
  `"truncated": true`). `lang` is `en` | `si` | `ta`. `filler: true` marks a holding line. `interrupted: true`
  marks a Risi line cut by the user (only the words Risi actually spoke before the cut are kept; v1 keeps the
  sentences fully spoken). `request_id` links to the `answer` card a tool turn posted during the call.
- `reason`: `hangup` | `max_length` | `switch_off` | `error` | `timeout`.
- `body` (old apps): "Call with Risi · 3:12\nYou: …\nRisi: …".
- A call with no utterance posts nothing.
- **Tool answers during the call:** a turn that needs a tool runs the §25 loop as an `ask`; its `answer` card
  appears in the Risi chat during the call, with the optional **`"call_id"`** of the call
  (`envelope_risi_answer_call.json`). Confirm cards (writes) work as today and are never confirmed by voice in v1.

### 41.6 Server (normative)
- Learning log: one row per model call as today (`transcribe`, the answer's `voice_call_answer`, the router
  if it calls a model), **never** text or audio; plus one `risi_agent_runs` row per turn (agent `voice_call`)
  with the timings: end-of-turn wait, STT, first token, first sentence, **first audio after the user stopped
  talking** (the budget: 1.5 s p50, 3 s p95), barge-ins. The daily scoreboard shows the `voice_call` row.
- At most `RISI_CALL_RISI_MAX` calls at once.
- **Length limit:** the admin setting **`call_risi_max_s`** (the admin console, Voices page, "Call Risi";
  resolved as table → env `RISI_CALL_RISI_MAX_S` → default **1800**, live within 5 s, every change
  audit-logged). Values outside **60–3600** are refused by the console and clamped when read. A call keeps the
  limit it started with; the server ends it at `max_s`.

### 41.7 Examples, gate
- **Examples:** `device_put_call_risi.json`, `me_reply_call_risi.json`, `calls_room_request_call_risi.json`,
  `calls_room_reply_call_risi.json`, `error_call_risi_off.json`, `error_risi_busy.json`,
  `error_risi_unavailable.json`, `error_not_risi_chat.json`, `livekit_token_claims_call_risi.json`,
  `group_call_started_call_risi_payload.json`, `group_call_ended_call_risi_payload.json`,
  `envelope_risi_call_risi_transcript.json`, `envelope_risi_answer_call.json`.
- **Server:** the order of refusals, the 2-seat `k: "risi"` room, Risi's claims, `join` refused, the reply's
  `max_s` from the setting (bounds), the session's limit, parity with the flag off or without `risi_call`,
  every new example.
- **Android (JVM):** decoders of every new example; the button gating (chat bar and contact sheet); no
  `E2EEOptions` only for this room; fail closed; the 12-s timeout; the history lines; parity with the flag off.

### 41.8 Meeting titles over the call's data channel (v1.52)
Proposal `contract/proposals/2026-10-15-call-risi-calendar-titles.md` (applied; Harsha 15 Oct, P0 RISI INTELLIGENCE
item 3). The phone is present during a Risi call, so Risi asks it for the event titles of the range a voice answer
needs, over the call's LiveKit data channel, for **that spoken answer only**: never stored on the server.
**Additive:** without the capability every call is byte-identical to v1.50.

**Capability.** The device capability **`"call_risi_cal"`** in `mls.capabilities` (`device_put_call_risi_cal.json`)
is valid only together with `call_risi`; the server drops it otherwise. The phone advertises it once it implements
this section. Without it the server never asks, a voice answer speaks times only (from the §25 calendar tool) and,
once per call, Risi adds: "I can see the times; the titles stay on your phone."

**Tokens.** In a `k: "risi"` room only, **both** tokens get `canPublishData: true`:
- Risi's token: `livekit_token_claims_call_risi_v152.json` (§41.2's claims with that one change);
- the caller's token (`start` with `risi_call`, and the starter's `join` refresh): §20.2 with that one change.

No other room changes: every other token keeps `canPublishData: false`.

**Request (Risi → phone).** A **reliable** LiveKit data packet with topic **`"risi.cal"`**, destined to the
caller's identity only (`call_risi_cal_request.json`):
```json
{"t": "cal_req", "id": "<uuid-v4>", "from": "2026-10-15T00:00:00+05:30", "to": "2026-10-17T00:00:00+05:30"}
```
`from`/`to` are the range the voice turn needs: RFC 3339 (the user's offset), `to` after `from`, at most 8 days apart.

**Reply (phone → Risi).** Within **2 s**, the same topic, reliable, to `risi_identity` only
(`call_risi_cal_reply.json`):
```json
{"t": "cal", "id": "<same>", "ok": true,
 "events": [{"start": "2026-10-15T10:00:00+05:30", "end": "2026-10-15T10:30:00+05:30", "all_day": false,
             "title": "Call with Shakya", "busy": true}]}
```
On a failure (`call_risi_cal_reply_error.json`): `{"t": "cal", "id": "<same>", "ok": false, "reason": "no_permission"}`.
- `reason`: `no_permission` (no calendar permission) | `no_calendars` | `off` (the user's setting below) | `error`.
- `events`: at most 100, ordered by `start`; the instances overlapping [`from`, `to`) with the §29 exclusions
  (deleted, cancelled, declined); all-day events on the phone's own dates. `start`/`end` RFC 3339.
- `title`: at most 200 characters, or `null` for a private or untitled event.
- The phone reads only the calendars the §29 client tool may read.

The phone **must**:
- answer only while the Risi call is up (the call screen's call, connected) and the request came from `risi_identity`;
- ignore any other topic or sender, and any malformed request;
- never send anything on `risi.cal` without a request.

**The user's control.** The setting **"Risi can say my meeting titles on calls"** is **on by default**, under
Settings → Risi and as a line in the first-call sheet (§41.4).
- **Off:** the phone replies `ok: false, reason: "off"`; Risi says times only, and says so once per call.
- The §44.5 "Only times / Yes" choice is unchanged: it still governs what the server may store and what the
  Risi chat card shows.

**Root decision, recorded:** default on. The user is asking aloud for their own calendar on a call with Risi, and the
titles are used for one spoken answer and then dropped. Harsha can switch the default to off.

**Server (normative).**
- The titles live only in the call session's memory, for the one answer being composed, and are dropped after it.
- They are never written to the database, Cassandra, the learning log (it records `titles_n` only), logs, telemetry
  or the `answer` card.
- In the `risi_call_transcript` line of that answer each title is replaced with the time-only wording (e.g.
  "10:00–10:30 busy"), and the line gets **`"titles_spoken": true`** (`envelope_risi_call_risi_transcript_titles.json`).
  The phone shows a small note under that line: **"Meeting titles were said aloud, not saved."** The transcript's
  `body` carries the same time-only text.
- No reply within 2 s → speak times only, and say so once per call.
- Never say "I can't see your calendar" when the §25 read succeeded.

**Honesty (decision 048).** LiveKit data travels with DTLS between the phone and RisiMe's SFU, then over loopback to
Risi's worker: the same path and the same honest label as the call audio (§41.0, "Not end-to-end encrypted: audio is
processed by Risi on RisiMe's server").

**Examples:** `device_put_call_risi_cal.json`, `livekit_token_claims_call_risi_v152.json`,
`call_risi_cal_request.json`, `call_risi_cal_reply.json`, `call_risi_cal_reply_error.json`,
`envelope_risi_call_risi_transcript_titles.json`.

**Gate.**
- **Server:** the request is sent only with the capability; after the 2-s timeout Risi speaks times only; titles
  never persisted (the learning-log row, the transcript line and the card are checked); the transcript line carries
  `titles_spoken`; parity without the capability; both tokens' data grant only in a `k: "risi"` room.
- **Android (JVM):** decoders for every new example; a reply only to `risi_identity`, only on request, only while
  the call is up; the setting off gives `reason: "off"`; at most 100 events.

### 41.9 Voice confirm of the caller's own writes, and the claim guard (Harsha 2026-10-15 20:01)
Amends §41.5 ("never confirmed by voice in v1"). Server-only, no wire change. Behind `RISI_CALL_VOICE_CONFIRM`
(`off` | `canary` | `on`, default `canary`).
- **Scope:** only the caller's OWN calendar events and reminders (the Risi Calendar / reminder write cards of §25.3).
  The rule never covers money, payments, HR, deletion, legal matters or sending anything to other people (decision
  085). Those keep the tap on the card.
- **Flow:**
  1. Risi drafts the write, which posts the usual confirm card to the Risi chat.
  2. Risi reads the draft back: "Tomorrow 9 to 10, racing discussion. Shall I add it?"
  3. A clear yes from the caller ("yes", "add it", "that's correct") confirms that pending card exactly like a tap on
     **Add**: the same write path, the same `write_id`, and the card shows as done on the phone as today. A change
     ("make it 10 to 11") updates the draft, and Risi reads it back again. A no, or silence, leaves the card for the
     user to tap.
  4. **Where a voice add goes (Harsha confirmed 2026-10-15 21:34 SLT):** the phone's own `calendar_add` (§25.3) runs only after its user's tap or typed
     request, so a voice yes cannot write to the phone's calendar. On a call, an add with voice confirm on goes to the
     caller's **Risi Calendar** (`risi_calendar_add`). A phone-calendar add still needs the tap on the card.
- **Claim guard (every output path: chat answers, Call Risi speech, spoken @Risi, cards):** Risi says
