  added / sent / saved / booked / scheduled / deleted only when the turn holds a successful tool result for that
  action (its result id). Otherwise the reply is rewritten to the honest status, for example "I've drafted it. Tap Add
  to confirm.", and counted as a `claim_violation` in the learning log (no text).
  - **"I can't see it":** Risi re-checks the write and says what it finds. It never insists.
- **Gate:**
  - the 15 Oct 19:43 call replayed;
  - 10 adversarial prompts ("did you add it?", "you said you added it", …);
  - a voice add followed by "did you add it?" on the device test.

### 41.10 The caller's speech recognised on the phone (v1.53, Harsha 2026-10-16 04:52 SLT, P0)
Harsha's 04:4x call: the server's speech-to-text "heard" lines nobody said (Whisper hallucinating on noise, silence
and echo). For English, the phone now recognises the caller's speech **on the device**
(`SpeechRecognizer.createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer`, en) and sends Risi the text over the call's data channel (§41.8).
The server's speech-to-text stays the fallback. **Additive:** without the capability every call is byte-identical to
v1.52.

**Capability.** The device capability **`"call_risi_stt"`** (`device_put_call_risi_stt.json`) is valid only together
with `call_risi`; the server drops it otherwise. The phone advertises it when it implements this section, **and** the
device has an on-device recogniser for English (`SpeechRecognizer.isOnDeviceRecognitionAvailable`, API 31+). A
device without one doesn't advertise the capability, and its calls use the server's speech-to-text.

**Topic `"risi.stt"`.** Reliable data packets on the call's LiveKit data channel (the §41.8 grants). The phone
publishes to `risi_identity` only, Risi to the caller only.

**Phone → Risi:**
- **A partial** (`call_risi_stt_partial.json`), at most 5 per second, while the caller speaks:
  `{"t": "partial", "seq": 7, "text": "do I have anything"}`
- **The final text of one utterance** (`call_risi_stt_final.json`), once the recogniser ends it:
  `{"t": "final", "seq": 7, "text": "Do I have anything to do today?", "conf": 0.91, "lang": "en"}`
  - `conf` is the recogniser's confidence, 0–1, or `null` when the recogniser gives none.
  - `text` is at most 1000 characters. An utterance with no recognised words sends nothing.
  - `seq` grows by one for each utterance, starting at 1. A partial carries its utterance's `seq`.
- **On-device recognition not available or stopped** (`call_risi_stt_off.json`):
  `{"t": "off", "reason": "unavailable"}`
  - `reason`: `unavailable` | `error` | `busy` | `language`.
  - After `off`, the phone sends no more finals in that call, and Risi uses its own speech-to-text for the rest of it.

**Risi → phone:**
- **`{"t": "mode", "mode": "phone"}`** (`call_risi_stt_mode.json`), once, after the call connects, when Risi accepts the
  phone's text for this call. Until it arrives (or with `"mode": "server"`), the phone **must not** send finals.
- **`{"t": "speaking", "on": true}`** / **`false`**: when Risi's own speech starts and ends. While `on`, the phone keeps
  recognising (for "Risi, stop" and real barge-in) but marks its finals `"during_risi": true`.

**Server (normative).**
- With `mode: "phone"`, a turn's text is the phone's `final` text. The server doesn't run its own speech-to-text on
  that call's audio for turns.
  - The audio still drives end-of-turn timing and barge-in.
  - The server takes over again (`mode` back to server) after an `off`, or after 3 utterances with clear speech on
    the audio that get no `final` within 3 s of their end.
- A final with `conf` < 0.4, or with `during_risi: true` and no clear barge-in energy on the audio, gets no answer.
  At most once in a row, Risi says "Sorry, I didn't catch that."
- The phone's text is handled like the server's transcript: it goes into the `risi_call_transcript` (§41.5). It never
  goes into logs, telemetry or the learning log (which records `stt: "phone"` and the latency only).
- The same low-confidence rule applies to the server's own speech-to-text: a voice-activity gate (no speech, no text);
  drop segments with a high no-speech probability, a low average log-probability or a high compression ratio; drop
  known hallucination phrases when the audio is quiet; no transcription of Risi's own speech (the echo window) unless
  barge-in energy is clear. Server-only, no wire change: it ships before the app.

**The phone (normative).**
- Recognise only while the Risi call is connected. Stop and release the recogniser when the call ends.
- Recognise only the call's own microphone audio. Never open a second microphone capture that could get silence while
  the call holds the microphone. Feed the recogniser the call's captured audio
  (`RecognizerIntent.EXTRA_AUDIO_SOURCE`, API 33+). Where that isn't possible, don't advertise the capability.
- **On-device only:** `createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer` never sends audio to Google. The phone must never fall back to
  a networked recogniser (`createSpeechRecognizer`).
- Never send anything on `risi.stt` before `mode: "phone"`, or after the call ends.

**Honesty (decision 048).** The call label stays as §41.0 says: the audio still reaches RisiMe's server for timing,
barge-in and fallback.

**Examples:** `device_put_call_risi_stt.json`, `call_risi_stt_partial.json`, `call_risi_stt_final.json`,
`call_risi_stt_off.json`, `call_risi_stt_mode.json`, `call_risi_stt_speaking.json`.

**Gate.**
- **Server:**
  - `mode: "phone"` only with the capability;
  - a final drives a turn;
  - low `conf` gets no answer, plus one "Sorry, I didn't catch that.";
  - after `off`, the server's speech-to-text takes over;
  - parity without the capability;
  - the phone's text is never logged.
- **Android (JVM):**
  - decoders for every new example;
  - nothing is sent before `mode: "phone"`, after the call ends, or to anyone but `risi_identity`;
  - no networked fallback;
  - the capability only with an on-device recogniser.

- **v1.54** (2026-10-16, architect 14:05 item 3; proposal 2026-10-16-translate-languages): §37.13.2 Translate to
  more languages: the read-only `translate_languages` on `/me/risi/voice` (present only with `translate_available`;
  absent = en/si/ta), `target` and `always_translate` take any listed code, the picker's "More languages…".
  Additive; without the field every reply is byte-identical to v1.53.
- **v1.53** (2026-10-16, Harsha 04:52 SLT, P0 hearing): §41.10, the caller's English speech recognised on the phone
  (capability `call_risi_stt`, data topic `risi.stt`: `partial`, `final`, `off`, `mode`, `speaking`). The server's
  speech-to-text stays the fallback, with a voice-activity gate and low-confidence drops. Additive.
- **v1.52 text amendment** (2026-10-15, Harsha 20:01): §41.9 voice confirm of the caller's own calendar and reminder
  writes (`RISI_CALL_VOICE_CONFIRM`, default `canary`; amends §41.5), and the claim guard on every output path. No
  wire change.
- **v1.48** (2026-10-14, decision 090): §41 Call Risi, a voice call in the caller's own Risi chat that Risi
  answers (flags `RISI_CALL_RISI`, Android developer flag `call_risi`): capability `call_risi`, `User.call_risi`,
  `start` with `risi_call: true` → a 2-seat room (`k: "risi"`) and the reply's `risi_call` {`state`, `e2ee: false`,
  `risi_identity`, `max_s`, `lang`}, errors `call_risi_off`/`not_risi_chat`/`risi_busy`/`risi_unavailable`,
  `group_call` `with: "risi"`, Risi kind `risi_call_transcript`, `answer.call_id`. Harsha: v1 without frame E2EE
  (honest label; C10 later), the button also on the Risi contact sheet, the length limit `max_s` from the admin
  setting `call_risi_max_s` (default 1800, 60–3600). Additive; parity with the flags off.

## 42. Send a message for me (v1.49)
Applied from `contract/proposals/2026-10-14-send-for-me.md` (P1 2026-10-14, Harsha's phone). Amends §25.4, §26.2,
§26.3, §26.6, §26.8, §28.4 and §37.8 where the subsections below say so. Server behind `RISI_SEND_FOR_ME`
(default `canary`); the app advertises `risi_send` only while its developer flag `send_for_me` is on. (§41 is
reserved for Call Risi, v1.48.)

### 42.0 Principles
- **One card, one tap** (§28.0). "Send Upali a message asking him to update" ends in **one** card:
  **To: Upali · the text · [Send] [Edit]**. [Send] is the only confirmation. No skills page, no second card.
- **The phone sends, as the user, end-to-end encrypted.** The server never sends a message on the user's
  behalf and never stores the text (it passes once in the tool call's args, as §26.6).
- **Private unless the user says Official.** A friend's name → that 1:1's Private conversation. Official only
  when the request says so ("in Official", "on the official tab").
- **Not a consequential action.** Sending the user's own chat message is not money, pay, HR, deletion or legal
  (principle 4, decision 085). The consequential-action guard registers `send_message` as
  **non-consequential**; the user's own [Send] tap (or their per-contact standing choice) is the approval.
- **Scheduled messages only with a time.** `schedule_message` is chosen only when the request gives a time or a
  repeat ("tomorrow 9am", "every day"). Without one it is `send_message`.
- **Never a false "off".** The server says a skill or permission is off only when the user turned the skill off
  (server state) or the phone **freshly** reported the permission denied. Unknown or stale: it proceeds with
  the card, and the phone's own answer at run time decides.

### 42.1 Capability and switch
- **Device capability `risi_send`** (in `mls.capabilities`, kept only with `risi_tools`;
  `device_put_risi_send.json`). A device advertises it once it handles everything in this section.
- **Server switch `RISI_SEND_FOR_ME`** = `off | canary | on` (`RisiMe.Agents.Flags`, runtime; default
  `canary`: on for `RISI_DEV_USERS`). Not in `/auth/config` (the capability is what the phone needs; the
  server decides per asker).
- **Phase 1 (server only, works with nightly.7 unchanged):** for an asker with the switch on but **no**
  `risi_send` device, `send_message` ends in the §25.4 **`draft`** card the app already renders: the text, in
  the user's language, `target_conversation_id` = the recipient's conversation; [Use] opens that chat with the
  composer filled (it never sends). The answer says: "Here's the message for Upali. Tap Use to open your chat
  with it ready, then send." Honest: nothing is claimed sent.
- **Phase 2 (APK with `risi_send`):** the card, the phone send and the "✓ Sent" reply below.

### 42.2 The tool `send_message` (client tool, write, no skill)
Model-facing args (server schema, informative): `{"to": str (a friend's name, or "this chat"),
"text": str (1–4096 graphemes; the message in the user's language and style), "tab": "private" | "official"}`.
`tab` defaults to `private`.

**Offered** (`authorize/3`) when: the switch is on for the asker, the asker has an active Risi chat, the asking
device advertises `risi_tools`, and (Phase 2) the device advertises `risi_send`. **No skill** (§26.1 is
unchanged; it never needs Settings → Risi skills).

**Resolving the recipient** (server, deterministic, before any card):
- a whole display name or first name of the asker's **friends** (§9) or chat co-members, normalised as §34.5;
- **several matches** ("Upali" names Upali Perera and Upali Silva): no card; the §34.5 `clarify` question
  "Which Upali?" with one chip per person (up to 3, `keep: false`, no [Keep]); a chip resolves it without a
  model call, exactly as §34.5;
- **a close but not exact match** ("Upaly"): the §34.5 name question as for every named write;
- **a contact with no 1:1 chat** (a co-member who isn't a friend, or no MLS 1:1 yet): no card; "You don't have a
  1:1 chat with Upali yet. Open Contacts → Upali to start one, then ask me again.";
- **no match**: "I couldn't find Upali in your contacts." (the model may ask who it is);
- `tab: "official"`: the chat's Official conversation when it is `on`; otherwise "Official is off in your chat
  with Upali." and no card;
- "this chat" from an Official conversation: that conversation; from the Risi chat: ask who it is for.
- Never a Risi chat.

**The card** (`confirm`, §25.4, in the asker's Risi chat; `envelope_risi_confirm_send_message.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "confirm", "request_id", "write_id", "tool": "send_message",
       "summary": "Send to Upali Perera (Private): Hi Upali, please update RisiMe to the latest version.",
       "when": null, "text": "<the message>",
       "args": {"conversation_id": "dm:…", "text": "<the message>"},
       "send": {"to": {"name": "Upali Perera", "user_id": uuid | null}, "tab": "private" | "official",
                "language": "en" | "si" | "ta"},
       "for": [asker], "buttons": ["send", "edit", "cancel"], "expires_at", "turn_ref", "call_ref",
       "made_by", "notify": [asker]}
body: "Send to Upali Perera (Private): <text>? Update RisiMe to answer."
```
- `args` is the tool call's exact args without `write_id` (§26.5 rule). `send` is display data.
- The app shows **To: Upali Perera · 🔒 Private**, the text, **[Send]** (filled) and **[Edit]**; Cancel is a small
  text button. An old app shows `summary` and no buttons (an unknown `tool`, §26.9).
- At most 1 `send_message` card per turn; the §25.1 bound of 2 writes still holds.

**[Edit]** = the §28.4 `confirm_write` with **`edit: {"text": str}`** (1–4096 graphemes, trimmed, not
empty). The server re-seals the args with the edited text and sends the tool call with it, no model call. Any
other key, or an edit on another tool, runs nothing (unchanged §28.4 rule). The phone's rule (§42.3) accepts the
card's args merged with **its own user's** `edit`.

**[Send]** = `confirm_write` (no `edit`). The server sends the `risi_tool_call` to the confirming device
(if it advertises `risi_send`, else the turn's device), as §25.4.

**Tool call** (`event_risi_tool_call_send_message.json`):
`"tool": "send_message", "args": {"write_id": uuid, "conversation_id": str, "text": str}`.

**Result** (`risi_tool_result_send_message.json`): `{"status": "ok", "result": {"state": "sent" | "queued"}}`
(exactly this key). `sent`: the server acknowledged the message (it has a `message_id`) within 10 s; `queued`: it
is in the phone's outbox. Errors (`status: "error"`): `{"code": "not_member"}` (the phone doesn't hold that
conversation or the user isn't an active member), `{"code": "bad_args"}`. `declined`: the §42.3 rule failed.

**The reply** (new `risi.kind` **`message_sent`**, in the asker's Risi chat, silent for others;
`envelope_risi_message_sent.json`):
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "message_sent", "request_id", "write_id", "conversation_id": "dm:…",
       "to": {"name": "Upali Perera", "user_id": uuid | null}, "tab": "private" | "official",
       "state": "sent" | "queued", "turn_ref", "call_ref": null, "made_by" (rule), "notify": [asker]}
body: "✓ Sent to Upali Perera" | "Queued for Upali Perera: it goes as soon as your phone is online."
```
- **Never the text** (it is in the user's own chat already). Honesty: "Sent" only for the phone's `sent`.
- The app shows the body as a tappable line; a tap opens `conversation_id` (that tab). Other apps show `body`.
- `not_member`: "I couldn't send it: you're not in a chat with Upali on this phone." Timeout (15 s): the §25.4
  `tool_timeout` error with [Retry] ("I couldn't reach your phone to send it.").
- Activity: no §26.4 entry (no skill). No `risi_items` row (nothing pending). Nothing in the learning log
  beyond the hashes-only `risi_turn_steps` row.

### 42.3 The phone's rule for `send_message` (android, normative)
The phone runs a `send_message` call only when **all** hold (the §25.3/§26.5 card rule, rule (a)):
- this phone holds the agent-leaf `confirm` with the call's `write_id` and `tool: "send_message"`, with its
  own user in `for`, not expired;
- the first answer to it from **its own user's leaf** is `confirm_write`;
- the call's args minus `write_id` equal the card's `args`, or the card's `args` merged with the `edit` of that
  `confirm_write` (only `text`);
- the `write_id` is unused on this phone; the conversation is one this phone holds, the user an active member,
  never a Risi chat.
Then it sends the text through the normal send path (encrypted at that moment, a fresh `client_msg_id`
persisted first), exactly as if typed, and answers `sent`/`queued` as above. The recipient sees a normal message.

**"Send without asking next time" (per contact, stored on the phone only).** Under [Send] the card offers the
tick **"Send to Upali without asking next time"** (off by default). When it is on for that contact (user id and
tab) and this phone is the device that sent the request (its history holds its own leaf's `risi_request` or
voice note with the card's `request_id`, at most 10 minutes earlier), the phone sends the `confirm_write` itself
when the card arrives, and the card shows "Sent without asking (your choice for Upali) · Ask me next time".
The wire is unchanged: it is the user's own `confirm_write`, sent on the user's standing choice. The setting
never leaves the phone and is listed in Settings → Risi skills → "Send a message for me", where each contact
can be switched back to "Ask me".

### 42.4 Scheduled messages only with a time (amends §26.6)
- `schedule_message` is described to the model as "only when the request gives a time or a repeat". The server
  refuses a `schedule_message` step whose `at` resolves to less than 2 minutes ahead or is not a time
  ("now", "") with `failed` reason `no_time: use send_message`; when `send_message` is offered the turn then
  continues with it.
- The card follows the same one-tap pattern for a `risi_send` device: `buttons: ["schedule", "edit",
  "cancel"]`, the `send` display object, [Edit] = `confirm_write` `edit: {"text"}`. Devices without `risi_send`
  keep `["add", "cancel"]`.
- The success line (`skill_done` body, §26.5) reads **"✓ Scheduled for Upali, Thu 09:00"** (or "…, every day at
  09:00"). The activity summary is unchanged (recipient and time, never the text).

### 42.5 The skill and permission state (amends §26.2, §26.8)
**Phone (normative):** the app sends the §26.2 `PATCH` with `client_permission` for every skill with an Android
permission **on start, whenever the app comes to the foreground, and on every change** it sees. A report whose
values didn't change is sent at most every 30 minutes (so `reported_at` stays fresh); a change is sent at once.

**Server (normative):**
- **The device of a voice request** is the device that sent the note (Agent.Voice carries it to the buffered
  request). When a request has no device (a note retried from the voice queue), the turn uses the asker's
  `risi_tools` device seen most recently.
- **`no_permission`** (the `skill_needed` reason, and the `authorize/3` gate) only when the device's report is
  `denied` or `unsupported` **and** was reported within the last **6 hours**. `unknown`, `not_asked`, a stale
  report, or no device: the tool is offered; the phone answers at run time (`no_permission`), and Risi says so
  then, with the [Open Risi skills] chip.
- **Scheduled messages never need `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`** (§26.1 "optional"): a `denied` report for it is never
  `no_permission`; the card says "may be a few minutes late".
- The skill's own **state** (`off` / `ask` / `allowed`) stays the server's record of the user's choice.

### 42.6 Speech hints (server ↔ speech service, loopback; no client wire change)
- `POST /v1/audio/transcriptions` gains an optional multipart field **`hotwords`** (comma-separated, at most 60
  entries and 1000 characters). The service passes them to faster-whisper's `hotwords`, appends them to the
  Qwen3 ASR context, and to Whisper's initial prompt after the Sinhala-script prompt (never replacing it). The
  CTC model ignores them. An old service ignores the unknown field.
- **Always:** `Risi`, `රිසි`, `ரிசி`. A Risi-chat request transcript that starts with a common mis-hearing of the
  name ("Rishi,", "Risky,", "Reesi,", "රිෂි,") is normalised to "Risi," (only at the start, only in the user's own
  Risi chat).
- **The user's own contacts:** for a note in the **sender's own Risi chat** only, the display names the server
  already holds for that user's friends and chat co-members (at most 50, first names and full names) are added,
  **per request**. They are never logged, never stored, never in the
  learning log, never used for another user's note. The phone-side recogniser (the Voice agent's L1) is the
  preferred place for contact names; this is the server fallback.

### 42.7 Agent design (decision 085)
- **Owner:** the Risi Brain (`RisiMe.Agent.Turn`) chooses the action; `RisiMe.Agent.SendForMe` is its tool
  (recipient resolution, the card, the reply). Sending itself is infrastructure (the phone's MLS send path).
- **Signals:** card shown → [Send] / [Edit] / Cancel / superseded; edit distance of edits (count only); auto-send
  per contact (phone-local, never reported). **Eval:** the gate's four utterances (en/si × voice/text) plus
  "message Upali tomorrow 9am" (must schedule), "message Upali" with two Upalis (must ask), "send Upali" with no
  1:1 chat (must say so). **Index:** share of send requests that reach [Send] in one card without an edit.
- **Flags:** shadow is not meaningful (the card is the action); `canary` (Harsha) → `on`.
- **Consequential-action guard:** `send_message` and `schedule_message` are registered **non-consequential**.

### 42.8 Gate
`scripts/send-for-me-test` (redroid, two phones): "send a message to <name> …" by **text and by voice**, in
**English and Sinhala** → one card → one [Send] → the message arrives (decrypted) on the second phone →
"✓ Sent to <name>" shown in the Risi chat. Also: [Edit] then [Send] delivers the edited text; a request with a
time gives the scheduled card. Server tests: the tool, the card, the edit, the reply, the recipient cases, the
skill-state rules (voice device, staleness, optional exact alarm), the hotwords never in logs. Android (JVM):
decoding the new examples, the §42.3 rule (card, edit, own leaf, unused write_id), auto-send only on the asking
device, the report on resume.

### 42.9 Examples
`device_put_risi_send.json`:
```json
{"mls":{"signature_key":"…","capabilities":["groups","tabs","risi_tools","risi_skills","risi_send"]}}
```
`envelope_risi_confirm_send_message.json`:
```json
{"v":1,"type":"text","body":"Send to Upali Perera (Private): Hi Upali, please update RisiMe to the latest version.? Update RisiMe to answer.","risi":{"v":1,"kind":"confirm","request_id":"6d1f2e3a-4b5c-4d6e-9f7a-8b9c0d1e2f3a","write_id":"2b3c4d5e-6f70-4812-93a4-b5c6d7e8f901","tool":"send_message","summary":"Send to Upali Perera (Private): Hi Upali, please update RisiMe to the latest version.","when":null,"text":"Hi Upali, please update RisiMe to the latest version.","args":{"conversation_id":"dm:0b9d7e8a-1c2f-4a3b-8d4e-5f6a7b8c9d0e_7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3","text":"Hi Upali, please update RisiMe to the latest version."},"send":{"to":{"name":"Upali Perera","user_id":"0b9d7e8a-1c2f-4a3b-8d4e-5f6a7b8c9d0e"},"tab":"private","language":"en"},"for":["7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3"],"buttons":["send","edit","cancel"],"expires_at":"2026-10-15T02:23:30.456Z","turn_ref":"8a1b2c3d-4e5f-4a6b-8c7d-9e0f1a2b3c4d","call_ref":"7e2a3f4b-5c6d-4e7f-8a9b-0c1d2e3f4a5b","notify":["7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3"]}}
```
`envelope_risi_action_confirm_write_send_edit.json`:
```json
{"v":1,"type":"risi_action","action":"confirm_write","target":"2b3c4d5e-6f70-4812-93a4-b5c6d7e8f901","edit":{"text":"Hi Upali, please update RisiMe today."}}
```
`event_risi_tool_call_send_message.json`:
```json
{"event_id":"e5b2c3d4-a5b0-11f1-8000-0242ac120002","kind":"risi_tool_call","data":{"tool_call_id":"4c5d6e7f-8a9b-4c0d-9e1f-2a3b4c5d6e7f","turn_id":"8a1b2c3d-4e5f-4a6b-8c7d-9e0f1a2b3c4d","request_id":"6d1f2e3a-4b5c-4d6e-9f7a-8b9c0d1e2f3a","conversation_id":"grp:2f3a4b5c-6d7e-4f8a-9b0c-1d2e3f4a5b6c","device_id":"c0a80101-0000-4000-8000-000000000001","tool":"send_message","args":{"write_id":"2b3c4d5e-6f70-4812-93a4-b5c6d7e8f901","conversation_id":"dm:0b9d7e8a-1c2f-4a3b-8d4e-5f6a7b8c9d0e_7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3","text":"Hi Upali, please update RisiMe to the latest version."},"undo_entry_id":null,"expires_at":"2026-10-14T02:25:30.000Z","to_devices":["c0a80101-0000-4000-8000-000000000001"],"server_ts":"2026-10-14T02:23:30.000Z"}}
```
`risi_tool_result_send_message.json`:
```json
{"status":"ok","result":{"state":"sent"}}
```
`envelope_risi_message_sent.json`:
```json
{"v":1,"type":"text","body":"✓ Sent to Upali Perera","risi":{"v":1,"kind":"message_sent","request_id":"6d1f2e3a-4b5c-4d6e-9f7a-8b9c0d1e2f3a","write_id":"2b3c4d5e-6f70-4812-93a4-b5c6d7e8f901","conversation_id":"dm:0b9d7e8a-1c2f-4a3b-8d4e-5f6a7b8c9d0e_7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3","to":{"name":"Upali Perera","user_id":"0b9d7e8a-1c2f-4a3b-8d4e-5f6a7b8c9d0e"},"tab":"private","state":"sent","turn_ref":"8a1b2c3d-4e5f-4a6b-8c7d-9e0f1a2b3c4d","call_ref":null,"made_by":{"model":null,"provider":"rule","at":"2026-10-14T02:23:33.000Z","also":[]},"notify":["7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3"]}}
```

### 42.10 What ships where
- **Server only (fast lane, works with nightly.7):** the voice-device fix and the §42.5 server rules (the P1's
  false "off"), `schedule_message` only with a time, `send_message` Phase 1 (the `draft` card), the hotwords
  field and the "Risi," normalisation, the flag.
- **Needs the APK:** `risi_send` (the [Send] [Edit] card, the phone send, `message_sent`, per-contact auto-send),
  the report on resume, the plain-words Risi skills page.
- **Needs the speech service rebuilt** (root, `infra/whisper`): `hotwords` taking effect.

- **Examples (applied):** `device_put_risi_send.json`, `envelope_risi_confirm_send_message.json`,
  `envelope_risi_action_confirm_write_send_edit.json`, `event_risi_tool_call_send_message.json`,
  `risi_tool_result_send_message.json`, `envelope_risi_message_sent.json`.

## 43. Permission fix-it: "I need your calendar to do that." (v1.49)
Applied from `contract/proposals/2026-10-14-permission-fixit.md` (Harsha 2026-10-14 10:55 / 10:57). Amends §26.2,
§26.5 and §28.3: for a device with `risi_fixit` (and the switch on for the asker), `needs_permission` replaces
`skill_needed`; phones without `risi_fixit` keep `skill_needed` exactly.

### 43.0 Principles
1. **One plain sentence:** "I need your calendar to do that." (in the request's language).
2. **One button:** **[Allow calendar]**. It shows the Android permission prompt directly. If Android blocks
   the prompt ("don't ask again"), it opens RisiMe's app-permissions page with the hint "Tap Calendar →
   Allow". If the skill was off, the same tap turns it on: `ask` ("Show me first") by default.
3. **After the user allows,** the phone tells Risi. **Risi retries the original request automatically** and
   confirms it as usual (the card, then "✓ Added to your calendar").
4. **After the user says no:** "OK, I won't use your calendar. You can change this in Risi skills." There is
   no nagging. Risi asks again only on the next request that needs the permission.
5. **This applies to every permission:** calendar, contacts, microphone, notifications, location, alarms, and
   any future one. **Ask at first need,** never from the background. The user's own tap is the consent.
6. **Never a false "off"** (§42.5 still holds): the card is shown only when the user's skill is off, or the
   phone's fresh report or its own tool result says the permission is not granted.

### 43.1 Capability and switch
- **Device capability `risi_fixit`** (`mls.capabilities`, kept only with `risi_skills`). A device advertises
  it once it handles this whole section.
- **`RISI_PERMISSION_FIXIT`** = `off | canary | on` (runtime, `RisiMe.Agents.Flags`; default `canary`).
- **The card is sent** when the switch is on for the asker **and** the asking device (or, when no device is
  known, any device of the asker) advertises `risi_fixit`. Otherwise the server sends the §26.5
  `skill_needed`, unchanged.

### 43.2 The card `needs_permission` (new `risi.kind`, in the asker's Risi chat)
```
risi: {"v": 1, "kind": "needs_permission", "request_id": uuid, "resume_id": uuid,
       "permission": "calendar" | "contacts" | "microphone" | "notifications" | "location" | "alarms",
       "skill_id": "calendar" | "reminders" | "alarm" | … | null,
       "turn_on": bool,          the skill is off: the tap also turns it on (state "ask")
       "text": "I need your calendar to do that.",
       "buttons": ["allow"], "expires_at": ts, "turn_ref": uuid | null, "call_ref": null,
       "made_by" (rule), "notify": [asker]}
body: "I need your calendar to do that. Update RisiMe to allow it here, or turn it on in Settings → Risi skills."
```
- `resume_id` names the server's sealed record of the original request (24 h; §43.4).
- `permission` → Android (informative): calendar = `READ_CALENDAR` + `WRITE_CALENDAR`; contacts =
  `READ_CONTACTS`; microphone = `RECORD_AUDIO`; notifications = `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (API 33+); location =
  `ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION`; alarms = no runtime permission (the tap only turns the skill on). The app labels the
  button "Allow calendar" / "Allow contacts" / …; an unknown `permission` shows "Open Risi skills".
- If the request came from a group, the group gets only the §25.1 pointer line, as for every personal card.
- Old apps (no `risi_fixit`) never get this kind.

### 43.3 The two answers (`risi_action`, from the asker's own leaf, in the Risi chat)
| `action` | `target` | when |
|---|---|---|
| **`permission_granted`** | `resume_id` | after Android says granted. The phone has already sent the §26.2 `PATCH` (`client_permission: "granted"`, and `state: "ask"` when `turn_on`) |
| **`permission_declined`** | `resume_id` | the user refused the prompt, or closed the settings page without allowing |
`edit: null` (`envelope_risi_action_permission_granted.json`). Anyone else's, a second one, or one after
`expires_at`, is ignored.

- **`permission_granted`:** Risi runs the original request again (a fresh turn with the same `request_id`, the
  same text and the same routing, §34). No model call is spent on the card itself.
- **`permission_declined`:** Risi posts the `answer` "OK, I won't use your calendar. You can change this in Risi
  skills." with `next_actions: [{"label": "Risi skills", "action": "open", "target": "settings.risi_skills"}]`.
  Nothing else: no reminder, no second card for that request.

### 43.4 Server (normative)
- **When:** instead of `skill_needed` (§26.5, §28.3) for a skill that is off or lacks the phone permission; and
  when a client tool step answers `no_permission` (§25.3) in a turn.
- **The resume record:** a `risi_pending_writes` row with `state: "resume"`, `tool: "needs_permission"`, the
  original request's `message_id`, `permission` and `skill_id` sealed with `RISI_DATA_KEY`, 24 h, one use. It
  holds ids only, never the request text: the text stays in the sealed 24-h buffer it is read from.
- Learning log: the step stays `need_skill` / `denied` in `risi_turn_steps` (hashes only).

### 43.5 The phone (android, normative)
- **The card:** the sentence and **[Allow calendar]**. The tap runs `requestPermissions` from the chat
  screen. If `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale` is false after a refusal (Android blocks the prompt), the
  app opens `Settings.ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS` and shows "Tap Calendar → Allow". When the user
  comes back, the app checks the permission again.
- **Granted:** first the `PATCH`, then `permission_granted`. The card shows "✓ Risi can see your calendar".
  **Refused:** `permission_declined`; the card shows "Not allowed".
- **Settings → Risi skills → Calendar** (and contacts, notifications, microphone, the same pattern):
  - not granted: one line and one button, "Risi can't see your calendar yet." **[Allow calendar]** (the same
    prompt-or-settings logic);
  - granted: "✓ Risi can see your calendar", then the "Show me first" / "Do it without asking" choice. Nothing
    else.
  - `READ_CALENDAR`/`WRITE_CALENDAR`, auto-sync, the window, the calendar count, error text and Copy diagnostics
    are shown **only in developer mode**.
- **Ask at first need:** the first "Add to calendar" (or the morning brief's calendar read) on a phone that
  never asked shows the card's sentence and the prompt, with one line on why.

### 43.6 Gate
- Server tests, one per permission (calendar, notifications, alarms; contacts, microphone and location have
  the same shape): denied → `needs_permission` card with `resume_id` → `permission_granted` → the original
  request runs again (for the calendar, its card) → declined gives the one sentence. Old devices still get
  `skill_needed`.
- Android (JVM): the card decodes; the button picks the prompt or the settings page; granted sends the
  `PATCH` and then `permission_granted`; declined sends `permission_declined`; the Calendar settings screen
  shows only the line and the button until the permission is granted; developer details are hidden.
- Device gate (scripts/send-for-me-test grows a step): "add dentist Friday 10am" with the calendar permission
  revoked (`pm revoke`) → card → [Allow calendar] → `pm grant` stands in for the dialog → the calendar card
  appears without a new request.

### 43.7 Examples
`envelope_risi_needs_permission.json`:
```json
{"v":1,"type":"text","body":"I need your calendar to do that. Update RisiMe to allow it here, or turn it on in Settings → Risi skills.","risi":{"v":1,"kind":"needs_permission","request_id":"6d1f2e3a-4b5c-4d6e-9f7a-8b9c0d1e2f3a","resume_id":"3c4d5e6f-7a8b-4c9d-8e0f-1a2b3c4d5e6f","permission":"calendar","skill_id":"calendar","turn_on":true,"text":"I need your calendar to do that.","buttons":["allow"],"expires_at":"2026-10-15T05:30:00.000Z","turn_ref":"8a1b2c3d-4e5f-4a6b-8c7d-9e0f1a2b3c4d","call_ref":null,"made_by":{"model":null,"provider":"rule","at":"2026-10-14T05:30:00.000Z","also":[]},"notify":["7e3f1a2b-9c8d-4e5f-a6b7-c8d9e0f1a2b3"]}}
```
`envelope_risi_action_permission_granted.json`:
```json
{"v":1,"type":"risi_action","action":"permission_granted","target":"3c4d5e6f-7a8b-4c9d-8e0f-1a2b3c4d5e6f","edit":null}
```

### 43.8 What ships where
- **Server only (fast lane):** the card, the resume record and the two actions, behind `RISI_PERMISSION_FIXIT`.
  It is inert until a phone advertises `risi_fixit`.
- **APK:** `risi_fixit`, the card, the prompt-or-settings button, the new Calendar settings screen, asking at
  first need, and developer-only diagnostics.

- **Examples (applied):** `envelope_risi_needs_permission.json`, `envelope_risi_action_permission_granted.json`,
  `device_put_risi_fixit.json`.

  - the action card [Add] [Edit] [Cancel]: `confirm.calendar` (the phone's last calendar), the
    `calendar_add` result `calendar` and the Calendar skill `PATCH` `calendar`, `confirm_write`
    `edit` (the phone accepts the card's args merged with its own user's edit), the success line
    "Added to your Google Calendar: … · Mon 12 Oct, 2–3 PM" with [Open] [Undo]; chips only fill the
    composer;
  - proactive offers: `confirm.origin: "offer"` and `confirm.item_id` (no request, `turn_ref` null,
    expiring at the event), once per item, person and kind, always asked;
  - near-duplicate merge and vague items: `needs_clarification` and the kind `item_clarify`
    ([New date] = `item_edit`);
  - My promises: `direction`, `owner_name`, `status`, `needs_clarification`, `all_day` on every
    item, `source_conversation_id`, `source_message_id(s)` and the reply's `totals`; the personal
    digest lists exactly My promises, with item `direction` and the same `totals`;
  - the §27.2 quiet-rule thresholds from `RISI_QUIET_*`; the §27.13 examples. Additive; no new
    capability, switch or error code.
- **v1.27** (2026-10-09): model transparency, the Commitment Ledger follow-ups and call
  transcription (§27, decision 070; proposal `2026-10-09-ledger-followups-transcription.md`,
  requirements by Harsha):
  - `risi.made_by {model, provider, at, also}` on every Risi envelope (alias `risi-l1` or the
    commercial provider and model; `model: null` for rule messages), shown as "Made by: … · time";
  - the quiet rule (last human message ≥ 600 s old, ≥ 6 messages from ≥ 2 humans since the last
    summary, ≥ 30 min apart, ≤ 8 a day; 4-h long-discussion rule; a listened call triggers at its
    end) and the per-person `discussion_summary` in each recipient's Risi chat (key points,
    "You agreed:" / "<Name> agreed:" items with owner and due), with the short `discussion_card`
    in Official replacing the in-group proposed commitment card for ledger owners;
  - owner-only `item_confirm`/`item_decline`/`item_edit` (counterparts may mark `done`),
    `item_update` applied to every copy, 48-h expiry, My promises with `role`;
  - reminders in each person's Risi chat: `item_due` `before`/`at` to the owner, `today` to
    counterparts only for confirmed items, `item_overdue` to the owner, then `item_nudge` to
    counterparts; a personal digest;
  - Official call transcription: the starter's `risi_listen` on `POST /calls/rooms` `start`
    (metadata `r`, a +1 seat, human-only caps), `risi: "listen"` on `call_offer`/`group_call`,
    Risi as a visible, non-publishing LiveKit participant with its leaf identity and MLS-derived
    frame keys, microphone tracks only; "Risi is listening" on the call, incoming and Join
    screens; `risi_stop` (any participant; deletes the call's transcript), a device without
    `risi_ledger` joining stops Risi first (the call goes on), `call_listen` durable lines and the
    `call_risi` signal; `risi-listen` (Rust, Erlang Port, audio in memory only) and faster-whisper
    on `127.0.0.1:8200`; the transcript only in the sealed 24-h buffer, deleted after extraction;
    a `transcribe` learning-log row with no output; never Private;
  - the capability `risi_ledger`, `/auth/config` `risi_ledger` and `risi_transcribe`. Additive; no
    new error codes.
- **v1.26** (2026-10-09): Risi skills (§26, decision 069; proposal `2026-10-09-risi-skills.md`,
  requirements by Harsha). Skills are the permission layer every tool runs under:
  - a server registry (`alarm`, `reminders`, `calendar`, `scheduled_messages`, and `email` not yet
    available) with exact permissions, `modes` and undo kind;
  - per-user state `off` (default) / `ask` / `allowed` and the per-device reported Android
    permission (`GET`/`PATCH /api/v1/risi/skills`; turning on asks for the permission right then);
  - "Allowed" skips the confirm card only for writes that affect the asker alone (alarm, own
    reminder, own calendar add), and the phone checks its own user's `risi_request`; sends to
    others, conversation reminders and cancels by request always confirm;
  - an activity log sealed with `RISI_MEMORY_KEY`, kept 90 days, never in the learning log
    (`GET`/`DELETE …/skills/{id}/activity`), and undo through `POST …/activity/{entry}/undo` with
    a single-use `undo_token` (a server action, or a client tool call to the device that did it
    with `undo_entry_id`; `manual` for alarms);
  - revoke voids open cards and optionally cancels pending items;
  - the pseudo-tool `need_skill` and the kind `skill_needed`;
  - the client tools `set_alarm` (`AlarmClock.ACTION_SET_ALARM`), `schedule_message` (stored and
    sent by the asker's phone, encrypted at send time, never stored by the server; daily repeat,
    sent-late rules), `cancel_scheduled` and `calendar_remove`;
  - `confirm` `skill_id`/`args` (an exact args match on the phone) and the kind `skill_done`;
  - the meeting card `calendar_offer`, with per-person `risi_action` `calendar_accept`/
    `calendar_decline` (`options.reminder`) leading to a `calendar_add` on that person's phone
    only;
  - the capability `risi_skills`, `/auth/config` `risi_skills`, and the errors
    `skill_unavailable` and `undo_unavailable`.

  Additive; users without a `risi_skills` device keep v1.25.
- **v1.25** (2026-10-09): Risi with tools (§25, decision 068; proposal
  `2026-10-09-risi-client-tools.md`, approved by Harsha). A bounded server tool loop
  (`RisiMe.Agent.Turn`, the `risi_next_action` next-action protocol, tools offered only as
  `authorize/3` allows; 6 tool steps, 8 model calls, 20 s/15 s/60 s, 24k tokens, 2 write
  proposals; a `final` on any bound) with `risi_turn_steps` (hashes only); the audience rule
  (personal sources, drafts and personal confirms go to the asker's Risi chat); the Risi chat (an
  Official `grp:` with `chat_kind: "risi"` and `chat_id` = its own id, one user plus Risi,
  `POST /api/v1/risi/chat`, `Chat.kind: "risi"`, `422 risi_chat`, core rules in `risi_cases`);
  client tools over TLS (the per-device stored event `risi_tool_call` with a 2-min TTL and a wake
  to that device only; `POST /api/v1/risi/tool_calls/{id}/result` with `404`, `409
  tool_call_expired`, `409 write_not_confirmed`, `413`; `calendar_check` free/busy only,
  `calendar_add` only after [Add]); the signal `risi_progress`; `answer` v2 (`steps`, `sources`,
  `next_steps`, `local_search`, `turn_ref`); the kinds `confirm`, `reminder_set`, `draft`;
  `reminder.reminder_id`; `risi_action` `confirm_write`/`cancel_write`/`me_too`/`not_me`; sealed
  notes (`kind: "note"`, `preference` for old apps); `error` `queue_overflow` and
  `tool_timeout`; the queue (20/min, FIFO, one turn per user, round robin); model routing and the
  egress allowlist; Private search only on the phone; the capability `risi_tools` and
  `/auth/config` `risi_tools`; `contract/v1/risi_gate_script.json` for
  `scripts/fake-llm --script`. Additive.
- **v1.24** (2026-10-08): two tabs per chat (Private | Official) and Risi stage 1 (§24, decisions
  065 and 066; proposal `2026-10-08-two-tabs-risi.md`, approved by Harsha). A chat is two MLS
  conversations sharing a `chat_id` (= the existing anchor id, so every existing conversation is
  its chat's Private tab); Official is always a `grp:` (also for 1:1s, `chat_kind: "dm"`, both users
  admin, `422 dm_chat`) and holds Risi (`Member.kind: "agent"`, attestation claim `kind: "agent"`,
  never admin) while on; Private never holds an agent (`403 private_tab` on every server path; the
  core rejects agent leaves in Private and `dm:` groups and `tab`/`chat_id` changes after epoch 0;
  `group_meta` gains `tab`, `chat_id`, `agents`); `Group` gains `chat_id`, `tab`, `chat_kind`,
  `agents`; the `Chat` object, `GET /api/v1/chats[/{chat_id}]`, `POST …/official` (lazy for 1:1s
  and migrated chats, immediate for new groups; `409 not_ready` with `tab`, `503
  agent_unavailable`), `PATCH /api/v1/chats/{chat_id}` `official: off | on` (1:1 either person,
  group admins; off stops delivery to Risi at once, any member may commit the agent `remove`,
  `official_off` on send, Risi deletes the chat's facts within 1 h, the conversation stays
  read-only; one Official conversation per chat, ever; `409 risi_required`); membership per chat
  (one op per tab, `422 invalid_member` for agents, tabs-ready adds while Official is on); the
  stored event `chat_event`; the `tabs` capability with delivery, `GET /groups` and push filters so
  old apps never see Official; `/auth/config` `tabs: on | off`; backups' `conversation` line gains
  `chat_id`/`tab`/`chat_kind` and a bundle with Official is schema 2; Risi stage 1 envelopes
  (`text` with a `risi` object: commitment, commitment_update, reminder, escalation, digest,
  answer, summary, report, offer, error; members' `risi_request` and `risi_action`), private
  feedback, "What Risi knows about me" and "My promises" REST, `PATCH /me` `tz`, timing, offer
  rules, learn-and-delete storage (24-h sealed buffer, derived facts, a learning log without raw
  input), the model cascade with the commercial fallback off, rate limits; policy `tab_cases` in
  `group_policy_cases.json`. Additive.
- **v1.23** (2026-10-08): switching voice and video mid-call, and screen sharing (§23, decision
  062), reviewed by server, android and crypto. Capabilities `call_switch` and `screen_share`;
  per-call `features` (`switch`, `screen`) on `call_offer`/`call_answer` (inside MLS) gate every new
  button, so v1.13–v1.22 peers see nothing new; 1:1 voice and video modes with the ephemeral
  `call_switch` (`request` camera/screen, `accept`, `decline`, `cancel`, `voice`; `seq` per sender,
  20-s timeout, crossing requests accept each other); one renegotiation per call at most, only by the
  original caller, `call_offer` `renegotiate: true` (applied only after a local accept; same
  fingerprint, DTLS role, ICE credentials and mid 0 transport; the (e) check again; rollback keeps
  audio; binding `ring == offer && !restart && !renegotiate`); `call_media` `video`
  (`off`/`camera`/`screen`); screen sharing as one video sender whose source switches (camera or
  screen, never both), screencast source with `MAINTAIN_RESOLUTION`, ≤ 1600 px, ≤ 15 fps,
  ≤ 1.2 Mbit/s (groups: plus a ≤ 800 px, 5 fps, 250 kbit/s layer), no device audio, normative
  privacy rules (consent every time, banner and Stop, stop on lock, own notifications without
  content, own windows `FLAG_SECURE`, fitted pinch-zoom viewer); groups: `POST /calls/rooms` `upgrade`
  (cap 8 over present identities plus tokens minted in 600 s, `409 too_many_for_video`,
  `409 not_in_call`, `UpdateRoomMetadata` `m: "video"`, `UpdateParticipant` grants, screen share only
  for `screen_share` devices), `join` from a present identity is a refresh, `media` in the rooms
  replies, group `call_switch` `video` and `call_media`, rendering only per the sender's MLS
  `call_media`, local "Voice only"; `call_end.media` and `group_call` `ended.media` are `video` if
  video was ever on; the call-info screen's data used is local only. No server change for 1:1, no MLS
  core change. Additive.
- **v1.22** (2026-10-08): encrypted backups (§22, decision 059), reviewed by crypto, server and
  android. `GET /auth/config` `backup: "on" | "off"` (server `BACKUPS`; off stops writes only,
  `503 backup_unavailable`); a per-account backup key `BK` (local copy in `mls_kv`, recovery copy
  wrapped under Argon2id(m 64 MiB, t 3, p 1) of a 120-bit recovery key shown as 7×4 Crockford
  characters with a checksum, and optionally of a passphrase with a strength floor; HKDF-derived
  wrap key and a key-check value, AAD bound to `user_id`, kind and `bk_id`); a fresh `DEK` per
  backup wrapped by `BK`; the file format `RISIMEBK` v1 (header hash bound into every chunk,
  `A256GCM-STREAM64K` with the chunk index and last flag in nonce and AAD, raw DEFLATE, Padmé),
  identical for server, local and exported backups; the bundle schema 1 (§17.7 entries plus
  conversation, tombstone, group-event and contact lines; never MLS state, cursor, device id, HPKE
  keys, tokens or the behaviour log); restore through the §17.7 import (dedupe, deletes win, not
  bound to the gap index; restored ids add no §13.3 marker); the blob purpose `backup` (owner-only,
  33 562 624-byte parts, 16 parts/512 MiB, newest 2 current, replaced kept 7 days, at most 5,
  1.5 GiB live, 60 parts/h, 200/day, 6 backups/24 h); `POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /api/v1/backups` (one
  backup device per account, `replace_device`) and `PUT`/`GET /api/v1/backup_key` (no silent key
  change); errors `backup_unavailable`, `no_backup_key`, `backup_key_conflict`,
  `backup_device_mismatch`; local backups daily, before every in-app update and before any
  confirmed wipe; no server backup before a fresh install restored or skipped;
  `contract/v1/backup_vectors.json` (format here, generated by crypto). Additive.
- **v1.21** (2026-10-08): reinstalls without reset, and stale leaves (§12.12, decision 060),
  reviewed by server, android and crypto. Admin is a user role (a reinstalled admin's new device is
  re-added by the §12.4a member path like anyone's; the only admin is no exception); every device
  rejoins and resets only as a last resort (DM: `candidates: 0` or `exhausted`; group admin:
  `exhausted`, or no candidate for 24 h; replaces the DM 2-minute rule and §12.8's admin reset); the
  rejoin replies gain `op`/`candidates`/`exhausted` (groups) and `exhausted` (DMs); the reset guard
  `409 rejoin_pending` (groups and DMs); a per-device naming budget (3 strikes, cleared by a widened
  op, an update or 24 h) with the `exhausted` state, replacing the DM 6-naming cap and the unbounded
  group renaming; DM wake pushes; `devices` ops pruned of devices that can no longer receive; stale
  leaves (superseded, unseen 24 h, the user keeps a live leaf) removed by the user's own device or
  an admin through cleanup ops from an hourly sweep (never by members); the recovery task
  `stale_device_ops`; two cleanup cases in `group_policy_cases.json`. Adds the v1.16 examples that
  were missing (`event_mls_dm_op.json`, `mls_commit_request_dm_op.json`,
  `mls_dm_rejoin_reply.json`). No MLS core change. Additive on the wire.
- **v1.20** (2026-10-08): open sign-up behind the server switch `OPEN_SIGNUP` (§21, decision 058).
  `GET /auth/config` `signup: "open" | "invite"`; `403 signup_required` when nothing maps and the
  switch is on (else `not_allowlisted`); `POST /auth/signup` `{"phone", "display_name"}` →
  `200 {"user"}`, idempotent, with `403 signup_closed`, `400 bad_request`, `409 phone_taken`
  (user, allowlist or pending invite) and `429 rate_limited` (5/h per IP, 3/24 h per `sub`, 200/24 h
  global); membership for open sign-ups; `phone_confirmed` on User, Friend, incoming Request and
  the `friend` signal (absent = true); an unconfirmed phone is never matched (no delivery, no
  incoming list, no crossing auto-accept); `phone_verified` keeps its §7.1 meaning; auth-log kinds
  `signup_refused`/`signup_rate_limited`; the email domain plus a salted hash for refused
  sign-ins. Additive; old apps show a generic refusal for `signup_required`.
- **v1.19** (2026-10-08): group calls with LiveKit, voice and video (§20, decision 056), reviewed
  by server, android and crypto; replaces the §16.15 outline. The `group_calls` capability with
  `group_calls_ready`/`missing_group_calls`; `POST /api/v1/calls/rooms` (`start` creates the LiveKit
  room with 32/8 participants and metadata, `join` → `404 call_ended`/`409 call_full`, `status`;
  HMAC room names; 10-minute LiveKit tokens for a `<user_id>/<device_id>` identity with publish
  microphone (camera for video) and subscribe only; `503 calls_unavailable`; `RemoveParticipant` on
  removal, device removal and reset; no call table); group `call:signal` by `conversation_id` with
  fan-out to `group_calls` devices, a push without fallback and group ring limits; `call_offer`
  `mode: "sfu"`, the ephemeral `call_member` and `call_cancel` `ended`; the durable silent
  `group_call` `started`/`ended` and its history lines; frame E2EE from the MLS exporter
  (`risime-call-v1`, per-identity HKDF frame keys, key index `epoch mod 16`, rekey on every epoch
  with a 10-s overlap, fail closed, roster from MLS, no auto-ratchet), the core FFI
  `call_frame_keys` over a label-restricted `export_secret`, `contract/v1/call_vectors.json`; the
  exporter label registry (§10.3); stated limits (insider forgery, clear codec headers, audio
  level kept for the SFU); LiveKit UDP 49500–49999, coturn relay 49152–49499, no ICE-TCP. Additive.
- **v1.18** (2026-10-08): 1:1 video calls (§19), reviewed by server, android and crypto. The
  `video` capability with `video_ready`/`missing_video`; a cleartext `media` on `call:signal` and
  the `call_signal` event (delivery and push only to `video` devices, `video_not_ready`, bound to
  the envelope); `call_offer` `media: "video"` with one `m=audio` and one `m=video`; SDP rules
  (BUNDLE required, one fingerprint value across m-sections, VP8 only in answers, no simulcast,
  `b=AS` ≤ 1500, more header extensions stripped); camera on/off without renegotiation
  (`setTrack(null)` plus the ephemeral `call_media` envelope and a 3-s frozen-frame fallback); the
  camera only while the call screen is visible and never unasked; video history lines; coturn
  `max-bps` 300 000 bytes/s. No voice-to-video upgrade. Additive.
- **v1.17** (2026-10-08): profile photos (§18), reviewed by server, android and crypto. The MLS
  envelope `profile_photo` (`ver` in server-corrected ms, the §14.4 icon object or `null`, the
  subject from the MLS sender only, a deterministic winner rule, strict validation, JPEG 64–512 px
  square); the blob purpose `avatar` (no conversation, 512 KiB, 3/h and 10/day, one current per
  user, the previous expires after 24 h, readers = owner, friends without a block, group
  co-members); the optional `silent: true` on the e2ee `msg:send` and the `message` event (no push;
  a pre-install silent event adds no marker or gap row); send triggers (change, new conversation,
  new leaf) with pacing, a 5–30 s sibling wait and lazy groups; group photo UI rules and local
  system lines (§18.7). Plaintext DMs carry no photo. Additive.
- **v1.16** (2026-10-07): §10.6 DM device ops: a reinstalled phone is re-added to its DMs (committer: an in-group device of either participant with `member_devices`; superseded leaves of the same user removed under server authorisation; a DM `reset` fallback; the `mls_dm_op` event; the optional `op_id` on DM commits; the recovery task `dm_device_ops`). Additive.
- **v1.15** (2026-10-07): history sharing between devices (§17, decision 049, consent option A),
  reviewed by crypto, server and android. The `history_share` capability; a content-free client
  gap index (`message_id`, `client_msg_id`, sender, `server_ts`) per pre-install message; the
  pushes `history:request` (with `own_devices`), `history:refresh`, `history:escalate`,
  `history:respond` (`unable` with `stale`/`no_data`), `history:deliver`, `history:ack` and
  `history:cancel`, and the error `request_open`; own devices named all at once (superseded ones
  when they connect) for about 10 minutes, then up to 3 members at a time for 24 h; the stale-request
  refresh; a 30-minute delivery deadline; requests closed on a reset; the event kinds
  `history_request`, `history_request_closed`, `history_status`, `history_share` (inbox, 48 h TTL,
  `to_devices`, content-free wake only); the MLS envelopes `history_request`/`history_share` with
  the 18-byte `'H'` authenticated data (the core skips its admin check for it); HPKE base mode with
  the 0x0001 suite to a per-request key, exact `info`/`aad`, a fresh `K` and seal per part, the
  `risime-history-v1` blob label, `rsk`/`K` never crossing the FFI, and
  `contract/v1/history_vectors.json` (format here, generated by crypto); own versus member decided
  from the MLS sender; the bundle format and import matching on `message_id` + `client_msg_id` +
  `from` + `server_ts`; the blob purpose `history` (deleted on `history:ack` or at 48 h); day/week
  limits in Postgres; the marker exception and `sys:history-shared`; call lines only between own
  devices; the honest forgery limits. Additive.
- **v1.14** (2026-10-07): members restore an existing member's devices (§12.4a), reviewed by
  crypto, server and android, plus an independent crypto check at merge. Any active member's
  in-group device may commit a `devices` op for an existing member (adds of users who already hold
  a leaf; removals only as a same-device re-add; never agents, roles or `group_meta`); the
  `member_devices` capability gates it per group; committer naming and authorisation (exact
  `op_id` + list match) widened; the shared fixture `group_policy_cases.json` v2 with the base
  epoch's leaves (one case flips to accept); wake pushes to candidates' own tokens (10, every 6 h,
  4 per device per day); the pilot recovery task (§12.11). Additive on the wire; the policy change
  is gated by the capability.
- **v1.13** (2026-10-06): 1:1 voice calls (§16), reviewed by server, android and crypto. The
  `calls` capability with `calls_ready` (one `calls` device per user) / `missing_calls` and the
  `calls_not_ready` refusal; the MLS call envelopes (`call_offer` with `sent_at`, `call_ringing`,
  `call_answer`, `call_accepted`, `call_ice`, `call_busy`, `call_cancel`; the durable `call_end`);
  the push `call:signal` (best-effort idempotency, per-pair ring caps) and the event kind
  `call_signal` in its own short-lived `call_signals` store (60/120 s), never a message; binding of
  the cleartext `call_id`/`ring` to the envelope, freshness, a 24-h dedupe and sender pinning; one
  encrypt-and-push lane, no fast path, ring after the page; first-answer-wins with a 10-s accept
  wait; glare by lowest `call_id` with the sibling rule; busy without `READ_PHONE_STATE`; DTLS-SRTP
  bound to MLS with strict SDP validation, a per-call certificate, a pinned fingerprint, a
  post-connect check and the audio-level extension stripped; `GET /api/v1/calls/turn` (5-h TURN
  REST credentials, `503 calls_unavailable`); the device-level high-priority `{"type":"call"}`
  push with a 3-s fallback; a locked app rings without a name (decision 051); the Android library
  and platform rules; `out_of_order_tolerance` 32. Calls connect only directly until decision
  046's ports open. An additive change.
- **v1.12** (2026-10-06): deleting messages and chats (§15), reviewed by server, android and
  crypto. The push `msg:delete` with `scope: "me" | "everyone"` (1–100 targets, `deleted`/`gone`
  reply, all-or-nothing errors with `failures`, new reasons `not_sender` and `too_old`); own
  messages within 48 h, group admins any age; the event kind `delete` with per-recipient target
  metadata; the `delete` envelope with the targets bound into MLS `authenticated_data`, checked by
  the server; receivers authorise by attested user id and the core's `sender_is_admin` at the
  control's epoch; tombstones, hidden tombstones re-checked on arrival, the purge with
  `secure_delete`; the push `chat:clear` and the local `cleared_upto` watermark; Clear chat and
  Delete chat; the `deletes` capability with `deletes_ready`/`missing_deletes`; logical server
  deletion (purged by TTL, backups up to 14 days); `maximum_forward_distance` 20 000; a
  receive-first rollout with no required update. An additive change.
- **v1.11** (2026-10-06): encrypted images (§14), reviewed by crypto, server and android. The
  `images` capability with `images_ready`/`missing_images`; blob purposes `media` (16 MiB, 2 GiB
  live per user, 120/h, 1000/day) and `icon`; streamed idempotent uploads by `client_blob_id`;
  single-range downloads with ETag/`If-Range`/`304`/`416`; per-purpose readers with the
  membership-interval rule and the owner; `GET /blobs/usage`; the `A256GCM-S64K` segmented,
  Padmé-padded blob format with `contract/v1/media_vectors.json`; the `image` envelope with strict
  receive validation; encrypted group icons; errors `409 not_e2ee`, `415 bad_media_type`,
  `507 storage_full`. No new event kinds. An additive change.
- **v1.10** (2026-10-06): history after a reinstall (§13), reviewed by server and android.
  Every stored `message`/`reaction` event goes to every member user, the sender included (the
  plaintext DM sender copy, never pushed, published before the recipient's event); self-acks are
  ignored; `history_before` on join/sync replies (`app_instances.first_seen_at`, null for
  pre-v1.10 rows, reset by device removal); the client history gap marker "Earlier messages
  aren't available on this device" and the "Some messages couldn't be decrypted" line; a
  256 MiB per-user `mls` blob quota with `413 quota_exceeded {used, limit}`; a one-off pilot
  backfill of sender copies. An additive change.
- **v1.9** (2026-10-06): groups with MLS (§12), reviewed by crypto, server and android.
  `grp:` conversations, the `groups` device capability and group readiness, group REST
  (create, members, leave, roles, rejoin), pending ops with one named committer, commit
  authorisation and the shared policy fixture, co-member key-package claim, generic blobs,
  `group_event`, `group_op` and `group_receipt`, paged commit catch-up with `410 log_expired`,
  reset, group `msg:send` and typing, caps of 256 users and 768 devices. An additive change.
- **v1.8** (2026-10-06): emoji and reactions (§11). Grapheme-cluster limits with the server
  authoritative, reactions as an e2ee envelope type or a plaintext `reaction` field and event
  kind, no acks for reactions. An additive change.
- **v1.7** (2026-10-06): E2EE with MLS (§10). The calling device is identified by the
  `X-Device-Id` header on MLS REST calls, a clarification added at implementation. Adds the device census, attestation, key
  packages, groups with epoch compare-and-set, ciphertext messages, the `mls_*` events and the
  readiness check. Additive: conversations upgrade only when every app instance is MLS-capable.
- **v1.6** (2026-10-06): invites and friends (§9). Invite-only sign-up (with the allowlist kept),
  `User.vouched_by`, friend requests by phone (no registration leak), accept, decline, cancel,
  block, `/contacts` returning friends only, `not_friends` on `msg:send` and `typing`, and the
  `friend` signal. An additive change.
- **v1.5** (2026-10-06): push notifications (§8). Adds `PUT`/`DELETE /me/devices/{id}` and the
  data-only FCM payload `{"type":"inbox","v":"1"}`. An additive change.
- **v1.4** (2026-10-06): one-time SMS phone verification (§7). Adds `phone_verification` in
  `/auth/config`, `User.phone_verified` (absent means true), `403 phone_unverified`,
  `/me/phone/verify/request|confirm`, `Retry-After` on every 429, and `phone_unverified` on
  `auth:refresh`. An additive change.
- **v1.3** (2026-10-06): Keycloak access tokens on REST and the socket (§6). Adds
  `GET /auth/config`; mapping to phone-first users; `403 not_allowlisted` and
  `409 identity_conflict`; `DEV_LOCAL_AUTH`-only dev login; `Authorization` on the socket upgrade;
  `auth:refresh` and `auth:expired`. An additive change.
- **v1.2** (2026-10-06): presence / last seen (`presence:watch`, §2.5), typing (`typing`, §2.6),
  and the ephemeral server push `signal` (§2.3). An additive change: v1.1 clients ignore the
  unknown push.
- **v1.1** (2026-10-06): added the error reason `bad_request` for malformed pushes and joins (§2.1,
  §2.2). An additive change; v1.0 clients stay compatible.
- **v1.0**: the Release 0.1 baseline.
